# Anchoring on zoom, and whether a vector city fixes it: research dossier

Verified 2026-10-07. This dossier covers 354 unique items. Each was checked by two independent verifiers against the HEAD export (an exact copy of the committed repo) and against fetched primary sources. The items are:

- 57 shift sources and 2 tie-point sets (Epēshu, Summarch);
- 177 round-0 claims from eight lenses: canon, sim, registration, vector LOD, vectorisation, references, atlas ownership, sim ownership;
- 65 round-1 gap items from seven lenses: anchor collateral, three coasts at Epēshu, shipped vectors against the print, consumers of placement, mobile-unit overlap, generated-plan vector budget, reference anchoring;
- 53 round-2 gap items: G0 orphaned files, G7 chart-conditioned Epēshu prototype, G8 seam clip, G9 era sheets. Five G7 items were supplied under both a short and a long id and are counted once.

**112 survived (2/2), 184 disputed (1/2), 58 refuted (0/2).** By group:

| group | survived | disputed | refuted |
|---|---|---|---|
| shift sources | 9 | 40 | 8 |
| ties | 2 | 0 | 0 |
| round-0 claims | 64 | 84 | 29 |
| round-1 gap | 17 | 29 | 19 |
| round-2 gap | 20 | 31 | 2 |

None of the 301 items carried over from the previous draft changed status; that draft stood at 92 / 153 / 56. No browser was run. Every dynamic number is either computed from code constants or measured on the z5 tiles and the art files, and "Browser probes still needed" lists what still needs a browser.

**How to read the status markers.** Only survived and disputed items are stated as findings. Disputed items are marked **[D]**, and the text says what the failing check objected to, or says that its objection is cut off in the record. Refuted items appear only in each section's closing list, with the reason. Where a check corrected an item, the corrected figure is the one given.

**How the five sections relate.** Each section was written over the whole item set from its own angle, so an item can appear in more than one section. It is counted once.

**Where the records live.** The machine record is `findings.json`, beside this file:

- `items[]` holds one entry per unique item, with id `<lens>:<raw id>`, every raw id form (`raw_ids`), the status, the votes, and each section's summary;
- `items[].prior_record` holds the full earlier record (mechanism, magnitude, fix, evidence, verifier texts) for the 299 round-0 and round-1 items and the two tie sets;
- `by_section` lists the items each section cites;
- the deduplicated owner questions, probes and open questions are stored next to the verbatim section lists they merge (`*_raw`).

When a section says "the prior dossier" or "the earlier dossier", it means the previous draft of this file. The raw measurements are in the companion JSON files beside it: `anchor-correction-table.json`, `anchors-off-print.json`, `three-coasts-epeshu.json`, `vector-overlays-vs-print.json`, `placement-consumers.json`, `mobile-overlap.json`, `generated-plan-vector-budget.json`, `reference-anchoring.json`, `rivers-vs-plans.json`, `epeshu-coast-warp.json`, `epeshu-scale-vs-sim.json`, `crispness-by-dpr.json` and `dm-chart-vector-budget.json`.

> **KEY DISTINCTION.** **REGISTRATION** (where art or geometry sits relative to the atlas coast, rivers, roads and ring-dots) is what fixes misalignment. The **RENDERING MEDIUM** (raster or vector) brings crispness past the stretched image, interactivity and shared road vertices. It moves nothing. **A vector city placed through the same `cityBox` misaligns exactly as the raster does.** Registration bends the art to the atlas, never the atlas to the art.

**Units.** Magnitudes are in atlas px: 1 atlas px = 1 world px at z4, which is 1 pixel of the 4390×2188 PatrinorModern image. On screen an error grows by 2^(z−4):

| zoom | screen factor |
|---|---|
| z7.3 (inside the z6.8-7.8 overlay fade) | ×9.85 |
| z8 | ×16 |
| z9.885 (Epēshu's live ceiling, `cityMaxZ`) | ×59.08 |
| z11.5 (`Z_STREET`) | ×181 |

`Z_STREET` 11.5 is only the global clamp. No city reaches it, and no street hand-off exists in code.

---

## Executive summary

### Why things shift

**Nothing in Leaflet drifts.** The base tiles, the city overlay and its wash all go through one projection, and Leaflet's own rounding stays within about one screen pixel (vlod VL10, 2/2; refs C1 **[D]**). What the owner sees has three causes.

**1. Fixed placement errors that zoom magnifies.** An error of *d* atlas px becomes *d*·2^(z−4) screen px. A 20 px error is about 197 px at z7.3 and 1.5k px at z10.3. The errors, roughly by size:

- **Anchors on labels.** Most city anchors that disagree with their marker sit on the first letter of their printed label, not on the ring-dot. 39 anchors are more than 5 px from their marker: Damoklir 69.1, Paga 68.6, Pontoron 67.5, Penthelon 112.8. The full audit reads **45 move / 2 keep / 4 owner calls over 138 anchors** (G0-1, 2/2).
- **Summarch** is hard-coded at (523,58). That is 19.9 px from its printed ring, about 196 screen px at z7.3.
- **Hordon** was copied from Maeges' dot, 42.7 px from its own, so Maeges is never selected.
- **The two DM-drawn charts are placed by a box, not fitted.** `cityBox` centres any city image on its anchor and sizes it by city class (`FOOT_R`: capital 26, town 14, village 9, ruin 8 atlas px). It reads nothing the art shows. The Epēshu chart's coast is 8.48 atlas px rms off the printed coast (max 21.05). That is 84 screen px at z7.3 and 501 at 9.885, and it lays 666 px² of chart land over atlas sea. Summarch's tie rms is 27.8.
- **A seam on both sides.** The canon JPEGs have no alpha, so the coast jumps 20-31 atlas px at Epēshu's north edge.
- **The generated plans register only their coast.** Their coast follows the z5 tiles by construction. Their gates, stubs, sea sides and rivers do not:
  - 18 of 135 plans have a printed river nearby, and 17 of them draw none (G0-7, 2/2);
  - road stubs miss the drawn road by a median 4.2 px;
  - Pomos gets false land gates from a sea leg.

**2. Camera motion nobody asked for.** This is independent of both registration and medium:

- `setMaxZoom` eases the view down by 1.3-3.0 zoom levels after a pan;
- overlays pop when the nearest city changes;
- a synthetic plan is hot-swapped for the masked one mid-fly (coast rotation up to 132°);
- `ensureArrival` teleports after the reader takes over;
- `exitChart` lands up to 1113 atlas px off.

**3. Presentation timing:** opacity steps, tier classes that flip late, CSS-scaled strokes during a fly.

Of the 25 computed major shift sources, only two depend on the medium: `svg-css-scale-bloom` and `gen-jank-frame-drops`.

### What fixes it: registration, mostly as data

- **Anchors.** Re-derive them onto the printed rings with an offline deterministic tool and a reviewed by-name exception table, after the census second pass. Rule the point convention first. Then re-measure the sea fields, regenerate the gazetteer, and approve one `cityCanvasSha` re-cut for Tamaron, Sokundo and Kanae.
- **Summarch.** Move it by translation only onto its ring, together with its `factions.json` seat and route waypoint.
- **Hordon.** Move it to (1124,1690).
- **Epēshu.** Give it a stored per-city translate plus uniform scale, chosen by policy.
  - Of six tested placements, 0.6x+3E fits the print best (3.58 rms). Coast shape cannot choose the scale, and the sim's labels favour about 1.0x.
  - One function must carry the record, and every placement consumer must read it: the overlay bounds, the hover POIs, the deep-link targets, the wash and the zoom cap.
  - Ship it as one unit with a stored print-land ring, applied as a percent `clip-path` on the overlay `<img>`, and a sea-toned fill in the existing `pCityWash` pane.
  - No warp, no rotation, no TPS. A coast-band warp was measured and gave no net gain in misregistered area.
- **Lifecycle fixes.** Never lower the zoom cap below the current zoom; add catchment hysteresis, a per-descent arrival token, the `exitChart` fix and the `#view` fix.
- **Roads and rivers.** Re-trace them onto the print: the Sun Road is a median 31.3 px off the ink. A plan-river consumer is new work.

Even the best Epēshu placement leaves about 3.6 atlas px rms of coast misfit in both directions (35 screen px at z7.3, 211 at 9.885). So keep a crossfade rather than a hard cut. The war and imperial sheets carry the same ink as modern, within 1 atlas px, so a record measured once serves all three eras.

### What a vector city does and does not do

**It does not move anything.** A vector city placed through `cityBox` lands exactly where the raster does:

- `L.svgOverlay` is an `L.ImageOverlay` with the same bounds box.
- The shipped Sun Road is already a vector and sits 31.3 px off the printed road.
- The repo's chart-conditioned Epēshu prototype, placed through today's box, has a 7.7 atlas px rms coast error and puts 1,780 of its 5,424 buildings on print sea.
- A vector form of a generated plan carries the same anchor and frame.

**What it buys:**

- **Crispness.** On phones the DM raster goes soft from z8.3 (DPR 3), and `flyIntoCity` lands at 0.471 texels per device px on an iPhone 13. A higher-resolution registered raster would also address this.
- **Hit geometry** for hover and tap through the same transform, under R4, R7 and the single tap resolver.
- **Road vertices** shared with the atlas, once the roads are re-traced.

**What it costs:**

- Only one city is live at a time. A generated plan is 715-2,065 features (2.6k-8.5k vertices) and is viable only merged by class into 11-30 paths, with level of detail below about z8.
- The prototype Epēshu is 7,758 features / 29,360 vertices and takes about 1.2 s to build in node.
- A skeleton trace of the canon charts fits (Epēshu 4,624 vertices, about 24 KB gzip). A full-building trace does not (177,225 vertices, 903 KB gzip). The charts hold no street network: their lanes are unpainted ground.
- `genCityCanvas` returns no geometry, so a vector consumer needs a sibling function. It must also avoid the mobile hash pins.

**The order the evidence supports:**

1. Registration first, medium-blind.
2. Then, optionally, a skeleton trace as registration and hit geometry.
3. Then a vector fabric as its own owner-gated item, after asking the DM for any MFCG export.
4. Keep the sim out of the first cut.

### What already exists in the repo

- **The frame.** CRS.Simple atlas px at NZ=4, shared by the three era pyramids. This is the one-frame principle every reference uses: swisstopo registers sheets once into its LV95 grid, and 3D Tiles chains transforms that preserve registration authored in. City art is simply not registered into it yet.
- **Canon art.** Two DM charts, each held twice: tile pyramids (`charts/epeshu` 3914×4200, `charts/summarch` 4245×3951) and overlay JPGs (2863×3072, 3072×2859). Their 28 POIs are in `chart-pois.json`.
- **Generated plans** that follow the coast by construction (`sampleCityMask`, `genCityCanvas`).
- **Shipped vectors.** Named ways, sea lanes and realm rings. The realm rings sit 3.5-5.4 px from the printed wash edge, which shows that the medium registers fine when traced against a clear edge.
- **Two prototypes**, `prototypes/epeshu-chart.html` and `prototypes/parcels-roofs.html`. They are deterministic, but they have a zero-area-building bug and ignore atlas water.
- **The sim's own Epēshu streets** (`planStreetsFor`), unrelated to the chart. Its labels sit 100-490 m from the chart POIs.
- **A tool pattern to copy**, `tools/build-gazetteer.js`.

**What does not exist:**

- any registration record or control point;
- `traced-roads.json` in the atlas (the file exists but is not loaded);
- an owner. Nobody owns `cityBox`, the seam data, the plan rivers or the ease-down.

The evidence supports a standalone atlas registration item that reaches Filigree 2 through a README §7 row and lands after Mobile 9/14 and before Filigree 3. The vector city comes later, and the sim later still.

## 1. The shift audit: every mechanism that moves the view or misregisters on zoom

*Cross-references: the registration fixes for these sources are worked out in section 2; what changing the medium would and would not change is section 3; the reference tools are section 4; who can land each fix, and when, is section 5. Probes P1-P15 and R1-R2 (see also P16-P19) are under "Browser probes still needed".*

This section lists everything in the atlas (`maps-site/index.html`, Leaflet 1.9.4, `L.CRS.Simple`, NZ = 4) that makes the view move on its own, or makes city art or geometry sit away from the authoritative PatrinorModern print when the reader zooms. It covers the 57 shift sources from the first research round and folds in the 98 later gap-research items that bear on them. Those come from round 1 (anchor collateral, three coasts at Epēshu, shipped vectors against the print, consumers of placement, mobile-unit overlap, generated-plan vector budget, reference anchoring) and round 2 (G0 orphaned files, G7 chart-conditioned Epēshu, G8 seam clip, G9 era sheets). Where a gap item disagrees with a shift item, the gap item wins.

Every item got two independent verifier checks against the HEAD export, and no browser was run. Only items that **survived** (both checks hold) or are **disputed** (one of two holds) are stated as findings here. Disputed items are marked **[D]**, with a note on what the dissenting check objected to. Where a check corrected an item, the corrected figure is the one given. Refuted items (0/2) appear only in §1.8. Tally for the 57 shift sources: 9 survived, 40 disputed, 8 refuted. For the 98 gap items: 25 survived, 54 disputed, 19 refuted. Per-item rows are in `findings.json` (`by_section["1-shift"]`). The full verifier texts for the shift sources and the round-1 gap items are in `findings.json` `items[].prior_record`. The round-2 items (G0, G7, G8, G9) exist only as the records summarised here.

> **KEY DISTINCTION.** **REGISTRATION** means where the art or geometry sits relative to the atlas coast, rivers, roads and ring-dots, and it is what fixes misalignment. The **RENDERING MEDIUM** (raster or vector) brings crispness past the stretched image, interactivity and shared road vertices. It does not move anything. A vector city placed through the same `cityBox(a,w,h)` misaligns exactly as the raster does. Registration bends the art to the atlas, never the atlas to the art.

**Reading the numbers.** Magnitudes are in atlas px (1 atlas px = 1 world px at z4) unless marked as screen px. Screen px = atlas px × 2^(z−4): ×9.85 at z7.3, ×16 at z8, ×59.08 at Epēshu's cap. Over Epēshu the atlas ceiling is cityMaxZ = 4 + log2(3072/52) = **9.885**. Z_STREET 11.5 is a global clamp, not a handoff that exists in code (gap-coasts tc-8 **[D]**: the dissent objected only that 11.5 is not shown to be a street handoff). Each finding carries a tag:

- **R** registration
- **L** lifecycle (motion nobody asked for)
- **P** presentation or timing
- **M** depends on the rendering medium

Each finding also carries **VI**: "yes" means a vector city would have the same problem.

---

### 1.1 Three separate causes behind "things shift"

1. **Fixed placement errors that zoom magnifies (R).** Leaflet keeps every layer on one coordinate map, so nothing drifts. But art that sits *d* atlas px off is *d*·2^(z−4) screen px off. A 20-px error is 197 px at z7.3 and about 1.5k px at z10.3. These are the owner's complaint. Most are **data** errors: anchors on labels, the Summarch literal, the Hordon/Maeges duplicate, route and seat coordinates, trait sea sides, approach classes. Only the two DM charts need a **transform**. Rivers are a fourth registration source that the first round missed (§1.2.6). The registration error is therefore *not* "concentrated in the anchors and the two DM charts", as the prior dossier's synthesis line said (G0-7, 2/2).
2. **Lifecycle motion the reader did not ask for (L).** `setMaxZoom` eases down. Overlays swap or vanish. Deep links clamp. `ensureArrival` teleports. These are independent of both registration and medium (§1.3).
3. **Presentation timing (P/M).** Opacity steps, tier classes that flip late, constant-size glyphs over scaling print, CSS-scaled strokes during a fly. Only `svg-css-scale-bloom` and `gen-jank-frame-drops` depend on the medium (§1.4, §1.5).

**Major sources.** These have status survived or disputed and at least one check rated them major. The list is computed, not picked: anchor-on-label, summarch-anchor-literal, hordon-maeges-duplicate, canon-art-fit-by-box, canon-hard-edge-seam, crossfade-double-image, maxzoom-ease-down, catchment-swap-pop, synthetic-to-melded-swap, stubs-unramped-and-gap, gates-ignore-atlas-roads, trait-sea-side-vs-atlas, pinch-bounce-late-instantiation, svg-css-scale-bloom, view-hash-lossy, ensurearrival-hijack, gen-jank-frame-drops, panel-covers-fly-target, setminmax-zoom-race, ceiling-by-centre-not-cursor, flyto-unclamped-target, marker-anchor-pixel-bias, route-faction-coords-off-town, midfly-mask-callback, eager-overlay-swap-on-fly (25). Only two of them depend on the medium.

---

### 1.2 Registration sources: what actually misaligns

#### 1.2.1 Anchors sit on the printed label, not the ring-dot · `anchor-on-label` · survived 2/2 (major, major) · R · VI yes

**Mechanism.** Every city layer is centred on `CITY_ANCHORS[k].atlasX/atlasY`. That anchor comes from `maps-site/data/city-anchors.json`, or from the two literals in `meshedWorldInit`. It drives all of these:

- `cityBox(a,w,h)`: overlay bounds, `worldPOICoord`, `buildWorldPOIs`
- `washMake`
- the `sampleCityMask` window (`ox=(atlasX−1.35·rW)·2`)
- `cityApproaches` (radius 2.5·FOOT_R)
- `nearestCity` (catchment 3·FOOT_R)
- the `flyIntoCity` destination

The clickable glyph, `flyToMarker` and the gazetteer use the `maps_markers.json` x,y, which sit on the ring-dot.

**Magnitude (recomputed by both checks).** 39 anchors sit more than 5 px from their own marker: 37 in the JSON plus the Summarch (19.9) and Penthelon (112.8) literals. The overall median is 1.0 and p90 is 35.4. Among the off-dot set the median is 26.4. The worst are:

| city | offset (atlas px) |
|---|---|
| Damoklir | 69.1 |
| Paga | 68.6 |
| Pontoron | 67.5 |
| Zhomphek | 64.5 |
| Philider | 58.0 |
| Permūr | 53.0 |
| Līrider | 42.7 |
| Hordon | 42.7 |
| Dargan | 42.4 |
| Lek Zagrok | 38.3 |
| Drāmūz | 36.3 (reverse case, see below) |

On screen that is ×9.85 at z7.3, or 77-681 px, and ×113.8 at the village cap of 10.83. By the checks' count, 16-17 printed dots lie outside their own anchor's 3·FOOT_R catchment, so zooming on the dot hits the Z_OPEN_MAX 8 wall ("uncharted").

**Corrections from the checks.**

- Tamaron and Lepon the Old do have markers (Tamaron at 7.8 px).
- Paga's anchor sits on *Penthelon's* label, and Paga's own marker is also off the print.
- Hordon belongs on the fix list.
- Moving x,y alone leaves `sea.side` and `sea.distPx` stale, both in `city-anchors.json` and in the `city-traits.json` overrides that `genCityCanvas` reads. They must be re-measured in the same edit.

**What the gap research adds and changes.**

**Verdicts.** The table now reads **45 move / 2 keep / 4 owner calls** over 138 anchors, replacing 37/1/2 (G0-1, 2/2). G1 audited the 98 anchors the 40-pair table never covered. It added 8 moves (Sokundo, Kanae, Shengo, Pomos, Ishin, Pish, Ītor Hāspira, Paril), 1 keep (Sjefenhold) and 2 owner calls (Ūmak-nūs-Ion, Kolens). The 45/2/4 split covers 51 anchors. The other 87 already sit on a ring.

The two keeps mean different things:

- **Drāmūz:** the anchor sits on the printed ◉ and on a Sun Road vertex (0.2 px). The marker, 35 px south in open water, is the record to fix. It is queued as a filigree R21 data item and has not been fixed yet.
- **Sjefenhold:** an uncharted canon place, kept with an explicit off-dot reason.

The four owner calls are Paga, Penthelon, Ūmak-nūs-Ion and Kolens. For Paga, both records are off the print, and the printed ○ is near (2074,809) (gap-anchor C2 **[D]**; the dissent confirmed the counts and objected to wording only).

**Penthelon.** It has no `city-anchors.json` row: its anchor is the `meshedWorldInit` literal. Moving it onto its ◉ (1942,755) drowns the plan. The cause is that `buildCityMask`'s r−b≤54 test reads the olive region tint as water: about 2% land at the ring against about 61% at the anchor 113 px north. The 0.9.7 off-dot reason is therefore stale. The move needs a different land/water source, such as the inked coastline or the filigree DEM, which is an owner decision because `sampleCityMask` is UG3-pinned (gap-anchor C3, 2/2).

**Derivation.** Derive the anchors with an **offline, deterministic tool** that carries an explicit exception table. Do not use a runtime "marker wins" rule. The divergence has several causes:

- anchors left stale after the marker re-snap
- the deliberate Penthelon move
- wrong markers, such as Drāmūz
- Hordon transcribed from Maeges' dot

(gap-anchor C1 **[D]**; the dissent rejected the "one general cause" story.)

**Order.** The census second pass has to land before or with that tool. Sokundo, Kanae and Shengo have no marker at all. Six trace records (Pomos, Ishin, Pish, Ītor Hāspira, Ūmak-nūs-Ion, Paril) have marker equal to anchor and are both off the print, so a by-name derivation would copy the error (G0-4, 2/2).

**Snap and lint by name, not by distance.** Paril sits exactly on a printed ring that by eye belongs to Mūmakon, and four other records would snap to a neighbour's ring. The holding check spells out "label-aware": the printed labels exist only as raster text, and OCR of Patrinaic diacritic names would be neither reliable nor deterministic. So both the snap and the lint work against one reviewed by-name table (G0-5 **[D]**; the dissent's own objection is cut off in the record). A 3-px anchor-to-marker lint only makes sense once that table exists. At HEAD it would fail at once.

**Point convention first.** `sampleCityMask` rounds the window origin from the anchor in 0.5-atlas-px steps. Three conventions are in use: ring centre, census-marker convention (ring −(1,0)) and anchor convention (ring −(1,1)). Each gives a different mask window, a different plan and a different `cityCanvasSha`. It also changes visible gates: Tamaron's sea approaches are [319.3, 1.6] at the ring centre and [317.7, 113.6, 3.5] at ring −(1,1). The convention ruling (see `marker-anchor-pixel-bias`, §1.2.7) must precede any B0 re-cut, or the re-cut has to be cut again (G0-3, 2/2).

**Targets for the hashed trio.** Sokundo's ○ is at (1478.0,1450.0), 5.81 px from its anchor. Kanae's ○ is at (1523.0,1490.0), 7.45 px off. Tamaron's ◉ is at (1659,1554). These are candidate targets pending the convention ruling (G0-2 **[D]**; the dissent reproduced the coordinates and its objection is cut off in the record). One earlier check reads Sokundo and Kanae differently: it found them off-ring or unstable under a local fit (see `marker-anchor-pixel-bias`, §1.2.7). A crop check before the re-cut settles which reading holds.

**Collateral of moving anchors.**

- Approach sets change for six pairs (Summarch, Tamaron, Lepon the Old, Harney, Drāmūz, Hordon), not five. Harney loses both land gates (gap-anchor C6 **[D]**).
- The Summarch route endpoint (0.0 px) and Sun Road vertices near Tamaron (1.7 px) and Lepon the Old (0.5 px) coincide with the old anchors. Re-trace those roads onto the printed ink. Never snap the polylines to anchors (gap-anchor C7 **[D]**; the dissent struck Drāmūz from that list and the claim that the roads were "drawn through" the anchors).
- 17 anchor-only names reach `gazetteer.json` through the anchors loop: Sokundo, Kanae, Mago, Aldorūs, Pharzeban and others, plus Lepon the Old through its alias. Regenerate with `tools/build-gazetteer.js` (gap-consumers C11 **[D]**).
- The planned living sea-mask done-when reads every anchor. Moving fixes 8 rows and breaks 1 (Ghōdar), and 16 fail at both positions in tinted regions. So the living tool must not use a colour threshold, and its check belongs in the rebaseline scope (gap-anchor C5 **[D]**).
- Living LV5/LV6 resolve from the Epēshu entry. Registration that lands before Living 1 costs no re-freeze (gap-consumers C7, 2/2).

**Fix (corrected).**

1. Data first: rewrite `city-anchors.json` onto the printed ring with the tool and table above.
2. Move the Summarch and Penthelon literals, which is a code edit to `meshedWorldInit` but not to any pinned function.
3. Re-measure the sea fields.
4. Regenerate the gazetteer.

No pinned function source changes, so UG3 `atlas_fn_sha` holds. But `genCityCanvas` output changes for every moved generated city. Tamaron, Sokundo and Kanae are exactly the `cityCanvasSha` trio and all are off-dot, so an owner-approved B0 rebaseline is needed, landed outside a Mobile 5-9 capture (gap-consumers C5 **[D]**).

#### 1.2.2 Summarch's hard-coded anchor · `summarch-anchor-literal` · survived 2/2 (major, major) · R · VI yes

**Mechanism.** `meshedWorldInit` sets `CITY_ANCHORS['Summarch']={atlasX:523,atlasY:58,kind:'town',harbor:false,sea:null}` under the comment "two extra anchors sourced from maps_markers (spec §A.2)". The comment is wrong. `maps_markers.json` and `gazetteer.json` put Summarch at (542,52), on the ◉. The ring centre is (542.0,50.0), in two forms of evidence:

- The pixel-centre ring detector finds it there on all three era sheets (G9-4 **[D]**).
- The marker-bias checks find every ◉ marker 2 px low, and (542,52) − (0,2) = (542,50).

An earlier tie-point fit gave (541.75,49.75). That is 0.25 px off on each axis, about half a z5 pixel, so it most likely reflects a different centroid convention. G0-3's ruling settles which to use. The literal (523,58) is the approximate `party-route.json` point, and it is also the `factions.json` Allhammers seat. `city-anchors.json` has no Summarch row.

**Magnitude.** The box from `cityBox` (town FOOT_R 14, 3072×2859 art) spans x 509-537 and y 44.97-71.03. The dot lies 4-5 px outside its east edge. The offset is 19.92 atlas px, or 1.42 footprint radii: 196 screen px at z7.3, about 290 at z8, and about 1.55k at the `flyIntoCity` target 10.28. That is off-screen on a 390-px phone.

**Effect.** The canon chart and its 9 world POIs draw west of the town, over the coast and the hatched realm.

**Fix.** Move the anchor to the ring, or move Summarch into `city-anchors.json` and delete the hard-code. This is a translation only: the Summarch fit itself is an owner question. Move the `factions.json` seat and the `party-route.json` (523,58) waypoint in the same edit. The second visit at (545,80) is offset on purpose. Canon cities are not hashed, so `cityCanvasSha` is unaffected.

#### 1.2.3 Hordon duplicates Maeges' dot · `hordon-maeges-duplicate` · survived 2/2 (major, moderate) · R + L · VI yes

**Data.** In `city-anchors.json`, Hordon {1139,1730} and Maeges {1139,1730} are the only coincident pair. Hordon's hand-measured ◉ marker is at (1124,1690), 42.7 px NW. `nearestCity` breaks ties strictly (`if(d<bd)`) in key order (Hordon 22, Maeges 127), so `worldCityUpdate` never selects Maeges: not for generation, prewarm, maxZoom or the overlay.

**Corrected end state** (both checks; the claim left out `ensureArrival`). On a cold `#chart=Maeges`, Descend or `ATLAS.descend('Maeges')`:

1. Maeges' village plan registers. maxZoom goes to 10.83 and the fly targets 10.33.
2. At landing `worldCityUpdate` picks Hordon. Hordon has no registry entry yet (its mask is async), so `setMaxZoom(8)` eases out by dz 2.33 (×5.0) and the Maeges plan is removed.
3. Hordon's plan registers when its mask lands. maxZoom rises to 10.19.
4. At 1.7 s `ensureArrival` sees z8 < 9.83. It hard-sets to 10.33, which is clamped to 10.19, onto **Hordon's** town plan sitting on Maeges' dot. If the mask is still pending, the view stays at 8 and the check retries at 3.4 s and 5.1 s.

On the warm path the plan swaps to Hordon's with a 0.14 ease. Either way a Maeges link ends on the wrong town, 42.7 atlas px from Hordon's own dot: 421 px at z7.3, 683 at z8, about 3.1k at 10.19.

**Fix.** Set Hordon's anchor to (1124,1690). The two footprints then stop overlapping, because 14 + 9 < 42.7 and each lies outside the other's 3r. Run it as the queued POLISH data item "Filigree data — Hordon/Maeges anchor" (R21, blocks F3-B) and regenerate the gazetteer. The trio is untouched. An optional `nearestCity` tie-break becomes unnecessary once the anchor moves.

#### 1.2.4 The two canon charts are placed by a box, not fitted · `canon-art-fit-by-box` · **[D]** 1/2 (major, minor) · R · VI yes

**Mechanism (both checks).** `cityBox` computes `r=footR(kind)`, `mx=max(w,h)`, `hw=r·w/mx`, `hh=r·h/mx`. That is an axis-aligned aspect-fit box centred on the anchor, with no fit data, control points or rotation anywhere.

For Epēshu (`registerCityOverlay('Epēshu','art/overlay-Epeshu.jpg',{prov:'canon',w:2863,h:3072,…})`, anchor 1236.1,1415, capital r 26):

- box x 1211.87-1260.33, y 1389-1441
- 0.01693 atlas px per chart px
- `cityMaxZ` 9.885, fly target 9.385

The chart draws water only on its west edge. The atlas puts Epēshu on a promontory with sea to the W, NW and N. The result: atlas water 43% of the box against 15% in the art (water IoU 0.37), and the chart's north wards sit over atlas sea.

**Magnitude.** The figure depends on the method. Quote the method with it and do not average across methods.

| method | rms (atlas px) | max (atlas px) | status |
|---|---|---|---|
| finder's mask-boundary classifier | 7.2 (mean 6.1) | 16.8 | not reproduced independently |
| three-coasts one-sided chamfer, chart to print | about 8.5 | about 21 | |
| G7 trace through the same box | 7.7 (islets filled; 7.56 raw) | | 1,780 of 5,424 traced buildings on print sea; the Forum 9.5 px west of the anchor dot (G7-9 **[D]**) |

At 7.7 rms that is about 76 screen px at z7.3 and 455 at 9.885.

The G7 trace is the clearest datum for the KEY DISTINCTION: the *vector* trace placed through today's `cityBox` lands exactly where the raster's pixels land.

**What gap research settles.**

- **The print mask is the registration target.** No DEM-to-atlas correction is needed. Near Epēshu the DEM 0 m contour and the print coast agree to about one DEM cell on the mainland: rms about 1.3-1.5 atlas px, mean offset vector 0.31-0.46 px. The best rigid shift (1.7-1.8 px) only lowers mainland rms from 1.50 to 1.02 (tc-1 **[D]**, tc-2 **[D]**). The tc-1 dissent limited the "one coast" reading to the DEM-to-print direction after islet filtering. Window-wide in the other direction the rms is far larger, largely from artefacts.
- **Translate plus scale, chosen by policy.** Six placements were tested, measured chart-to-print and chart-to-DEM (rms/max, atlas px):

| placement | to print | to DEM |
|---|---|---|
| 0.6x+3E | **3.58/6.66** | 3.57/6.84 |
| 0.6x+8E | 4.82/9.07 | 5.35/9.62 |
| 0.8x+3E | 4.69/13.0 | 3.71/10.93 |
| 0.8x+8E | 4.83/11.02 | 4.48/8.69 |
| 1.0x+3E | 7.21/19.39 | 5.79/17.25 |
| 1.0x+8E | 5.88/17.27 | 5.15/15.02 |

A free fit runs to the edge of its search box (scale 0.5, dy +9 to +10 S), so coast shape alone cannot choose the scale. Even the best placement leaves about 3.6 px rms, which is about 35 screen px at z7.3 and 211 at 9.885 (tc-4, 2/2).
- **Two criteria for the scale, and they conflict.** The print coast favours 0.6x+3E. The sim's hand-placed label spread favours about 1.0x (rms-radius ratio 1.12, or 0.94 without Fairy Arch). Its built-up extent favours 1.0x or more. Owner question OQ8 (prior Q4) must give both criteria and say that the atlas wins for placement, with the sim following later (G0-15 **[D]**). The dissent reproduced the numbers but objected to part of the framing; its reason is cut off in the record.
- **A non-rigid warp was measured and rejected.** A coast-band warp at 0.6x+3E halves the residual (3.58 to 1.72 rms). But it leaves the misregistered seam area unchanged, at 156-166 against 166 px² unwarped, because the frame displacement opens a third seam sign. It holds for this prototype on Epēshu only (G0-11, 2/2).

**Fix (corrected by the dissent).**

1. Store a per-city record in data (translate + uniform scale, fallback to the footprint box) and read it inside `cityBox`.
2. `cityMaxZ` and `nearestCity`'s radius must read the same scale. Otherwise the 1-texel-per-CSS-px zoom drifts by log2(1/scale), 0.32 at 0.8x.
3. `washMake` is UG3-pinned. It centres on the anchor and takes only hw/hh from `cityBox`, so a fitted *translation* does not move the wash. Either accept a wash a few px off-centre, or move the anchor itself.
4. Do not rotate through a re-encoded canvas. `registerCityOverlay`'s `toDataURL` would duplicate the multi-MB DM art.
5. POIs follow automatically through `cityBox`.
6. The fix is canon-only, so `cityCanvasSha` is unaffected.
7. Order: anchor fix, then this record, then the seam treatment (§1.2.5). It lands after the Mobile 5-9 lane releases `maps-site/index.html`.

**Inherited:** `pois-ride-the-art` **[D]** (minor, minor). World POIs share the `cityBox` transform, so they agree with the chart to about 0.003 atlas px but carry its offsets against the atlas. Several Epēshu POIs sit over atlas sea W and NW. The dissent struck the label-anchored-city range from this item, since those plans take their coast from the atlas mask, and struck the Lepon Island Fortress, which is placed by its own formula. No separate fix: the POIs follow any `cityBox` record.

#### 1.2.5 The canon seam · `canon-hard-edge-seam` · survived 2/2 (major, major) · R at the boundary · VI yes

**Mechanism.** The canon JPEGs are baseline 3-component with no alpha, and no CSS mask is applied to them. Only `genCityCanvas` plans get the destination-out vignette. The chart's rectangle therefore abuts the atlas: the base runs from 1 down to 0.12, and the f=1.7 wash is about 0.8 alpha at mid-edge but only about 0.38 at the corners. At Epēshu's north edge (y = 1389) the chart's sea ends near x 1214.5-1222 while the atlas sea runs to about x 1241-1254.

**Magnitude.** The coast jumps 20-31 atlas px: 197-305 screen px at z7.3, 0.84-1.3k at the 9.385 landing, 1.18-1.83k at cityMaxZ 9.885. Past z8 the hard rectangle edge stays at full strength. Only the coast jump fades.

The seam is **two-sided** (tc-5, 2/2). Today the chart puts 666 px² of land over atlas sea (26% of the box) and none the other way. At 0.6x+3E it is 91 px² land-over-sea and 75 px² sea-over-land. At 0.8x+3E it is 212 / 35. A static rebuild reproduced 91.9 / 74.4 px² (G0-12, 2/2).

**Fix (corrected by the G8 seam research).**

- **Shape.** Store an offline **print-land ring** in atlas px: islet-filled, a single ring, Douglas-Peucker 0.25-0.5, about 1 KB or less, within the mask's own 0.5-px pixel. Store it over a fixed window, the `sampleCityMask` patch at atlas 1201-1271 × 1380-1450, so it does not depend on the placement. At runtime convert it to a `%` `clip-path: polygon()` on the overlay `<img>`, set through the ImageOverlay `className` or a CSS rule, not through style.transform, width or height, which Leaflet rewrites (G8-1 **[D]**, G8-2 **[D]**).
  - Build the mask on a padded window and crop afterwards. A mask built on the box alone disagrees by about 11 px² at 0.6x (G8-3 **[D]**).
  - A % polygon follows Leaflet's resize and zoom transforms with no per-zoom JS. Unprefixed `clip-path` covers iOS Safari 15+ and Android Chrome. Support for evenodd is unverified, so use the single ring (G8-9 **[D]**; needs browser probe P14 under "Browser probes still needed").
- **Fill.** Clipping reveals the land-tone wash, not the base. The fill actually needed is a **sea tone over print sea**, above the wash (G8-4 **[D]**; the dissent corrected the premise: the wash is 0.94 alpha only out to about 0.85 of the box half-size, not over every box pixel). Carry it as an `L.imageOverlay` in the existing `pCityWash` pane with `zIndex 2`. That puts it above the default-1 wash even after `washRefresh`, with no `createPane` call and no `washMake` edit. An `L.polygon` there would sit under the re-appended wash (G8-5, 2/2).
- **Band 2** (chart sea over print land) needs its own owner decision. The chart's flat sea fill makes that band easy to key.
- **Rejected:**
  - Keeping the base opaque under the footprint: it is one global opacity, and only about 6% would show through the wash anyway (G8-6 **[D]**).
  - A runtime canvas mask-image: about a 30-screen-px blocky edge at 9.885, dependent on the browser's decoder, and a duplicate of `buildCityMask` logic (G8-7 **[D]**).
  - A PNG mask under `art/`: a new raster that trips the offline-manifest TILES_V check (G8-8 **[D]**).
  - A pre-warped or pre-feathered plate (G0-11, G0-13).
- **Gate.** Ship the clip and fill **with** Epēshu's registration record, never ahead of it. Today's unregistered box would clip away 665.8 px², most of the south-western quarter (G8-11 **[D]**). The seam bite is Epēshu-only among the canon charts.
- **Ownership.** No Living or Filigree product supplies this ring. The anchoring/registration track owns the data and its few runtime lines in `ensureCityOverlay` and `worldOpacityUpdate` (G8-10 **[D]**). The look still coordinates with filigree slice C.

**Correction to the prior dossier.** That dossier dropped "conflicts with A-U11's same-file rule" as unsourced. It is sourced: HEAD `docs/mobile/units.json`, id A-U11, acceptance "after load the full image is the same file … registerCityOverlay literal kept". It is restored here as a **sequencing rule**: until Mobile 6 (A-U11) has passed, any change to `art/overlay-Epeshu.jpg` collides with it. A new plate also needs a TILES_V bump, which re-versions all 3,364 rasters, about 36.7 MB. The CSS clip and fill cost 0 raster bytes and pass A-U11 (G0-13 **[D]**; the dissent verified the TILES_V counts, and its objection, cut off in the record, appears to concern the POI-displacement figures).

#### 1.2.6 Generated plans: gates, stubs, sea sides, rivers

These plans take their coast from the atlas mask, so their coast is registered to about 1 atlas px. The rest of their geometry is not.

- **`gates-ignore-atlas-roads` [D]** (major, major) · R · VI yes.
  - Mechanism: `buildApprPolys(route,res[13],res[12])` sees only the 12-point party route, 2 named ways and 4 sea lanes. Of 136 anchors, 11 get land approaches and 6 more get a hinterland gate. All the others aim gates at `gate0=R()·2π`.
  - The dissent corrected the counts (the claim said 13/138) and the visible jump: 125-390 screen px in the z6.8-7.8 band, not 2.0k px at a zoom where the base is at 0.12. It also rejected the cheap fix. `data/traced-roads.json` is 46 short, noisy fragments, "mostly lettering and islets", and must not be fed in. Wait for POLISH "Traced road network".
  - Gap research: classes are assigned per source file, so every party-route leg counts as land. Leg 8 (Sepos to White Sea, 74% sea) gives the village Pomos land gates at 266.6° and 107.6° with no printed road. Fix it with a per-leg medium field in `party-route.json` (gap-print vop-04, 2/2). Any road re-trace changes generated gates and therefore `cityCanvasSha`.
- **`stubs-unramped-and-gap` [D]** (major, minor) · R + P · VI yes.
  - Mechanism: `buildCityStubs` (invented plans only) takes the bearing at the 2.5r ring and draws the stub radially to 1.18r at a fixed .5/.6 opacity. `worldOpacityUpdate` never touches it, so stubs show over bare atlas from z6.5 to 6.8.
  - Gap to the drawn road (corrected): median **4.2** atlas px raw, not 2.2; max 14.0-14.5 (Sokundo); about 30 screen px median and up to 112-116 at z7. The spline is not the cause.
  - Fix: snap the outer end to the drawn way inside `buildCityStubs`, which is not pinned. Ramp the opacity per polyline, because `L.layerGroup` has no `setStyle`. A full fix still repaints the gates inside pinned `genCityCanvas`.
- **`trait-sea-side-vs-atlas` [D]** (major, minor) · R (data) · VI yes.
  - Mechanism: `sv` comes from `traits.sea.side` even when the mask has water. It shifts the centre 0.07·S away from that side, biases the quays, and draws the whole coast when there is no mask.
  - Corrected scope: only cities with `drawWater` (harbour, or a sea side within 25 px) are affected. Sokundo 132°, Tamaron 97°, plus Tarap, Mago and Kanae. Five of the nine claimed cities never draw water. Shengo's anchor sits on a label letter with a patch reading 0% water, yet it draws an eastern compass sea on dry land.
  - Fix: correct the sides as data. Do not null them: that adds a `pick()` draw. Decide Shengo's sea from the mask at its *moved* anchor. Sea approaches only steer the quay bearing and never create gates, so the convention question in G0-6 does not decide it (G0-6 **[D]**). Pomos loses both land gates when it moves, under every convention. This changes `cityCanvasSha` for Sokundo and Tamaron.
- **Rivers (new row, `plan-river-unregistered`; G0-7, 2/2)** · R · VI yes.
  - Finding: none of the 57 shift sources dealt with a printed river meeting a plan. 18 of 135 generated plans have a printed river within 2.5·FOOT_R: 13 cross the 1·FOOT_R box, 2 graze it, 3 are approach-only. 17 of those 18 draw no river at all. Epēshu's canon chart is a 19th case: the Lamor crosses its approach circle at 178.1°.
  - Effect: from z7.8 the plan is opaque over a 0.12 base, so it hides or contradicts the printed river. A vector plan with the same RV block misses the print the same way.
  - Hash cost: feeding atlas river geometry into the RV block changes `atlas_fn_sha.genCityCanvas`. While the change stays RV-guarded it leaves `cityCanvasSha` equal to B0, because none of the trio has a river. A fix in the mask path would move it (G0-9, 2/2).
  - Supply: no planned item supplies usable rivers. Filigree B-13 covers only the DEM window (5 river cities plus Epēshu), and the Living trace only feeds barges. This is new, unowned work. Re-run the crossing table after the anchors move: Aldrakhar, Iet Ford and Bash Dargis gain box ink and Stony Ford appears (G0-10 **[D]**; the dissent's objection is cut off in the record).
- **`generated-plan-centre-offset` [D]** (minor, minor). `genCityCanvas` deliberately pushes a water city's core 0.07·S inland: capital 3.6, town 2.0, village 1.3, ruin 1.12 atlas px. The mask coast stays registered. Accept it and record it. Shifting `cityBox` would misregister the coast.
- **`wash-ring-over-sea` [D]** (minor, minor) · tone, not a shift. `washMake`'s anchor-centred f=1.7 land-tone radial tints atlas sea beyond a coastal plan's own coast, at 0.5-0.94 alpha in a band 0.36-0.56·r wide. That is 150-240 screen px for a capital at z8. It fades in on the same ramp, so nothing jumps. Any fix edits pinned `washMake`.

#### 1.2.7 Mark data and sub-pixel conventions

- **`marker-anchor-pixel-bias` [D]** (major, minor) · R (sub-pixel) · VI yes.
  - Finding: `px()` treats integer atlas coordinates as pixel edges, which matches the tile pyramid. The glyph is centred exactly on its point, so the whole offset is in the data. The offset depends on the glyph type, not on the data source:
    - **○ markers** (all 121 census markers, plus the wiki markers Lepon and Clickerhall): the ring centre is marker + (1.00, 0.00).
    - **◉ markers** (Epēshu, Summarch, Penthelon, Hordon and about 11 census ◉): the ring centre is marker + (0, −2).
    - **Anchors** that sit on a ring (104 of 136): about (+1, +1).
  - Corrections from both checks:
    - Hordon is off by about 2 px, not 4.8-5. The 4.8 came from the fit locking onto a neighbouring glyph.
    - Ion Ephel is off by about 1-2.4 px.
    - Census y = anchor y + 1 for about 69-71 cities, not about 80.
    - Epēshu's anchor (1236.1,1415) is on its ring to within 0.1 px. Only its marker (1236,1417) is 2 px low. So the "59-90 screen px at Epēshu's 9.885-10" figure does not apply to Epēshu. It applies to a generic town anchor 1.0-1.4 px off: 32-45 px at z9 and 59-90 at z10.
  - Magnitude: ○ pins 4 px at z6 and 11 px at z7.49; ◉ pins 8 px and 22 px; anchors about 10-14 px at z7.3.
  - Fix:
    - Use one offline tool modelled on `tools/build-gazetteer.js`, then regenerate the gazetteer. Never add a runtime +1 in `px()`.
    - Census ○: a blanket x+1 is safe (sd about 0.05).
    - ◉ markers: hand-correct them or snap them to the fitted centroid.
    - Anchors: the dissent says snap only those within about 1.6 px of a fitted ring, not a blanket (+1,+1). Its local fit found Tamaron (+0.6-1.1, +3.4) and Kanae (+1.4-3.2, +7.4) off-ring and Sokundo unstable. The later G0-2 detector found clean ○ rings with their labels 5.8 and 7.45 px from Sokundo's and Kanae's anchors, and G0-1 rules them moves. The owner's point-convention ruling (G0-3) and a crop check settle which reading applies before the trio is re-cut.
- **`route-faction-coords-off-town` [D]** (major, minor) · R (mark data) · VI yes.
  - Finding: the Epēshu route pip and three faction seats share (1222,1404), 19.1 px from the marker and 17.9 px from the ring. The Summarch pips sit 19.9 and 28.2 px off. The Lepon seats sit 12.0 px from the marker. The 'Sepos' waypoint is 410.6 px from the Sepos marker.
  - Magnitude: up to 214 screen px for Epēshu at z7.49, and 316 for Summarch.
  - Fix: resolve seats and waypoints by place name through `MREF` at load, keep the EPESHU boot constant separate, and fix or rename the Sepos waypoint. This is data-only.

#### 1.2.8 Shipped vectors against the print: static misregistration, not zoom drift

These layers are already vectors. They show that a vector is exactly as registered as its source data. The offsets are fixed in atlas px, so on screen they grow with zoom but do not slide.

- **The Road that Walks with the Sun** (`named-ways.json`, 28 "trace approximate" vertices, drawn by `buildNamedWays`). It sits a median 31.3 atlas px from the nearest printed road dot in the ±100 px normal band (p90 64.6, max 98.2). For 45% of samples no dot lies in the band. About 44% of the line lies on print sea. That is about 125 screen px at z6 and 308 at z7.3. The fix is a data re-trace onto the printed ink, which is not `cityCanvasSha`-neutral (gap-print vop-01 **[D]**; the dissent confirmed the numbers and objected to their framing).
- **`crSpline` smoothing is innocent.** On land roads it moves the line at most about 6 px from the raw vertices. Only on wide sea bends is it larger (leg 8 up to 19.6 px), and the print draws no road there. That shift is far below the 31-98 px source error, so better rendering cannot fix this; only re-registering the vertices can (vop-02 **[D]**).
- **Sea lanes.** The North Coast Packet crosses the Paerāndas islets for 26 px raw, 27 px spline: 108 screen px at z6, 266 at z7.3. The South Lane's land run is only 12 px raw / 5 px spline, not the claimed 35. Lane endpoints sit up to 60 px from their anchors. Fix: move or split vertex [1320,1398] in `sea-lanes.json` (vop-06 **[D]**).
- **The mask's colour test** (r−b≤54) also fires on realm tints and grey-green land. 70 of 115 inland no-sea anchors read over 50% water. This is latent today, because only the 16 `drawWater` cities draw the mask coast. It is a trap for any registration step that trusts `mask.water` inland (vop-09, 2/2).

#### 1.2.9 Era sheets: one registration target, not three (G9)

- **The war and imperial z5 sheets are recoloured copies of the modern sheet.** 748 of 1,260 tile pairs are byte-identical, and the linework is unchanged outside the legend (G9-1, 2/2).
- **The printed geometry matches modern within 1 atlas px.** That covers ink inside every anchor's 2.5·FOOT_R circle (war max 0.71, imperial max 1.12 at Heron's House, where a river edge straddles the luma threshold), rings (≤0.19 px), and road dots (≤0.22 px). Rivers and roads at the flagged anchors also match. The checks counted 19 printed-river rows including Epēshu, and noted that Kolens has no ink in its circle (G9-2, G9-4, G9-5, G9-6, all **[D]** with the dissents adjusting counts, not the verdict).
- **Consequences.**
  - A per-city registration record and a modern-derived seam clip need no era fields.
  - Era mask differences come from tint and must never be read as coast moves (G9-3 **[D]**).
  - `sampleCityMask` always reads `tiles/5`, so tint-as-water cannot get worse in era mode. Keep any atlas-sampled mask modern-sourced (G9-7, 2/2).
  - The one era defect left is colour: the land-tone wash around Leponnia plans (about ΔG 29, ΔB 21), which belongs to the look owners (G9-8 **[D]**).

#### 1.2.10 What makes registration errors visible, and the contexts where they cannot be judged

- **`crossfade-double-image` [D]** (major, minor) · rendering that amplifies R · VI yes.
  - Mechanism: `baseOpacity` runs from 1 to 0.12 over z6.2-8, and `overlayOpacity` runs as z − 6.8 over 6.8-7.8. Both layers stay ≥30% visible from z7.1 to about 7.59 (z7.3: base 0.462, overlay 0.50), so any art offset reads as a slide of offset × 2^(z−4).
  - The dissent corrected three things. Only the canon charts show a *second* coast, because procedural coasts come from the same tiles. The "6.1 px" Epēshu figure was not reproduced. The "26 px" median belongs to the off-dot anchors, not to villages.
  - Fix registration first. Narrowing the ramp only hides the error. Even after registration Epēshu keeps about 3.6 px rms, so keep a crossfade rather than a hard cut.
- **`base-overzoom-ghost` [D]** (none, minor) · rendering · VI yes. Past z5 the base is a CSS-upscaled z5 layer. It is co-registered across levels and eras with 0 px offset, and sits at 0.12 under a 0.94 wash. The geometric error is nil, but alignment cannot be judged by eye at city zoom. This is why registration has to happen in data and cannot be tuned by eye.
- **`chart-frame-unregistered` [D]** (minor, minor) · R gap, mode switch. `enterChart` swaps to the chart's own pixel frame (`cpx` at `atZoom` 0 or maxz), with no transform to the atlas. It is a cut on click, not a zoom shift. It also makes chart POIs and world POIs two coordinate sets that agree only because the overlay JPGs are exact resizes of the pyramids. Region charts belong to POLISH "Region-chart zoom-through".

### 1.3 Lifecycle motion the reader did not ask for (independent of registration and medium)

All rows below are VI yes: a vector city under the same lifecycle behaves identically. Code is `maps-site/index.html` and the vendored `leaflet@1.9.4`.

| id | status (severities) | mechanism | when | magnitude (corrected) | fix (corrected) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| maxzoom-ease-down | survived 2/2 (major, major) | `worldCityUpdate` runs `setMaxZoom(allowed)` on every zoomend/moveend. Leaflet's `setMaxZoom` calls `setZoom` when z > cap, an animated 250 ms zoom about the **centre**, while `ensureCityOverlay(null)` drops the art | Panning, dragging or inertia at z>8 out of or between catchments; a fly from a deeper city; nearest-only holes | dz = cityMaxZ − 8: ruin 3.0 (×8), village 2.83, Summarch 2.78, town 2.19, Epēshu 1.885, generated capital 1.30. Prewarm never fires for neighbours, so the next city is capped at 8 | Never lower the cap below the current zoom: `setMaxZoom(Math.max(allowed,map.getZoom()))`, tightened only on zoom-out. Add catchment hysteresis and choose the city by min(d/3·footR). The checks rejected assigning `map.options.maxZoom` directly as unsafe |
| catchment-swap-pop | [D] (major, major) | One `CITYVIEW` overlay keyed to the centre-nearest anchor within 3·FOOT_R. `ensureCityOverlay` runs `removeLayer` on overlay, wash, stubs and POIs synchronously, with no fade or hysteresis. ### 1.4 Presentation and timing (nothing is misplaced; the eye reads it as shift)

| id | status | tag | finding (corrected) | fix |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| opacity-steps-on-animated-zoom | [D] (minor, moderate) | P | `_animateZoom` fires no 'zoom' until `_onZoomTransitionEnd` (250 ms), and the app listens only to zoom, zoomend and moveend. So buttons, keyboard, double-click, box-zoom and the `setMaxZoom` ease update opacity and create the plan *after* the scale. Steps are overlay 0.25 and base 0.122 per 0.25 click. The pane CSS transition is dead because `setOpacity` writes the img or tile container, never the pane. The dissent found the magnitude understated: larger animated steps up to dz 4 snap the fade completely, and the ×3 shift step belongs to the zoom control and keyboard, not double-click. After A-U7 a coarse-pointer press steps 0.5: overlay 0.5, base about 0.244, the crossfade in 2 presses, and a press during an animation loses 0.5 (gap-mobile C6, 2/2) | Compute target opacities on 'zoomanim' (e.zoom) |
| wheel-unanimated-steps | [D] (minor, minor) | P | The custom wheel handler (`step=clamp(−dy·k)`, k 0.0045, or 0.018 for ctrl-pinch) calls `setZoomAround(ll,target,{animate:false})`: dz 0.45 per 100-px notch (×1.37), crossfade in 2.2 notches. `wheelPxPerZoomLevel` and `wheelDebounceTime` are dead knobs, because `scrollWheelZoom` is disabled | A rAF tween with `_moveStart`/`_move`/`_moveEnd` and `_limitCenter`, clamped to the live max zoom. The dissent rejected `animate:true`, which drops events |
| tier-classes-zoomend-only | survived 2/2 (minor, minor) | P | `updTier` runs on zoomend only, while tier-d is live on 'zoom'. On pinch, flyTo and animated zooms, labels, lesser pins, shields, way labels and the tier-C 1.14 glyph scale pop in at the end (flyToMarker 0.9 s, flyIntoCity 1.4 s). Wheel is effectively live | `map.on('zoom zoomend',updTier)` |
| tier-thresholds-viewport-dpr | [D] (minor, minor) | P | Tier zooms come from fitZoom (map height) and `TIER_CEIL=RETINA?5:6`. HEAD figures (after A-U0): iPhone 13 fit 1.9, tier C 3.9667; 1x desktop tier C 4.7833; spread 0.82, or 1.22 with landscape (gap-mobile C2, 2/2). A resize re-tiers with no zoom | This is Mobile A-U2 (absolute tiers). Do not build it separately. A-U2 is recorded **failed** after 3 attempts, so the spread stays until it is re-run. Tiers are presentation: landing A-U2 fixes no registration |
| poi-band-gap | [D] (minor, minor) | P | Tier-d hides point marks live at 7.5. City POIs appear only at z≥8, through `setCityPOIVisible` inside `worldCityUpdate`. That leaves a 0.5-zoom band without point marks (plan, stubs and wash stay) and a POI pop at the end of flies and pinches. The dissent re-labelled this a visibility-timing gap, not a shift | Align the thresholds; drive the POIs from `worldOpacityUpdate` |
| uncharted-stale-class | survived 2/2 (minor, minor) | P | `worldOpacityUpdate` is registered before `worldCityUpdate`, and only the latter changes `CITYVIEW.name`. The gen callback never re-toggles. So `show-uncharted` (grain 0.6, watermark 0.92, 0.5 s fade) lags one event, indefinitely if the reader is idle. Narrower than claimed: zoom gestures on registered cities self-correct | Re-toggle at the end of `worldCityUpdate` and in the `ensureCityGen` callback |

The printed ring outgrows the glyph from about z5.4 (◉) / 5.6 (○). Glyph and ring share one atlas point, so this is a scale mismatch, not a registration error, but it amplifies the 1-2 px marker bias | A filigree ruling (R4/R7) decides |
