### 1.5 Medium-dependent sources, and what a vector city would and would not change

- **`svg-css-scale-bloom` [D]** (major, minor) · **M** · VI **no**.
  - Mechanism: Leaflet's Renderer only CSS-scales its container on 'zoom' and 'zoomanim', and re-projects paths at zoomend/moveend. ImageOverlay, TileLayer and markers re-place every frame. During flyTo and touch pinch, strokes, casings and dashes therefore balloon and drift against the raster, then snap. `flyIntoCity` adds the stubs before the fly.
  - Magnitude (corrected): invented-plan stubs reach about ×250 (targets 8.80-10.5). The claim's ×21 was paired with Epēshu, which has no stubs. Routes reach about ×30 before tier-d. Marker, waypoint and search flies only reach tier C (×3-5).
  - The dissent found two fixes wrong as written: a stock `L.canvas` renderer CSS-scales the same way, and wheel zoom is not exempt.
  - Fix: add the stubs on landing, or hide SVG panes while flying.
  - For a future vector city, the key caveat: draw it in a custom layer redrawn on every 'zoom' frame with stroke widths in screen px. Stock `L.svg`/`L.Canvas` paths would bloom.
- **`gen-jank-frame-drops` [D]** (major, minor) · M · VI no.
  - Mechanism: the synchronous 2048² `genCityCanvas` (several 2048² readbacks) plus `toDataURL('image/webp',0.85)` run in the z5 tile onload. Prewarm's `requestIdleCallback` only starts the fetches, and deep links generate twice. Long tasks can therefore land mid-gesture. Unmeasured.
  - The dissent: prewarming on 'zoom' events, or adding clock or performance tokens, would change the set of hashed invented URLs or break the clock-free rule (UG3). Keep the fix to scheduling.
  - A vector plan would still need generation, but not the encode and decode.

What the gap research says about the medium itself:

- **Placement.** A vector plan placed through `genCityCanvas`'s frame (a 2048 square, footprint scale, centred on the anchor) misaligns exactly as the raster does. The coast follows the atlas mask and the gate bearings follow `APPR_POLYS`; everything else is seeded (gap-vecbudget C7 **[D]**; the dissent added that roads, not only the coast, follow atlas data). The chart-conditioned Epēshu prototype through today's `cityBox` lands on the same 7.7 rms (G7-9 **[D]**).
- **Size.** One generated city is about 715 features / 2.6k vertices for a village (Kanae), 1,360 / 5.6k for a town (Tamaron), and 2,065 / 8.5k for a counterfactual capital (gap-vecbudget C1, 2/2). Only one plan is live at a time (C2 **[D]**). Merged by class into 11-30 SVG paths it is viable; otherwise use a per-pane canvas (C3 **[D]**). Leaflet's cost lands at zoomend (re-projection of *every* layer in the renderer) and at moveend, not during the animation (C4 **[D]**). The traced chart-conditioned Epēshu is 7,758 features / 29,360 vertices, about 3× the counterfactual and over the live ceiling without an LOD (G7-3, 2/2).
- **Crispness depends on DPR.** `cityMaxZ` reads the registered dimensions, so a sharper source raises the cap by exactly as much as its native zoom. The upscale at the cap therefore stays equal to DPR: 3 on iPhone 13 (G0-17, 2/2). The Epēshu raster goes soft from z8.3 on DPR 3, and `flyIntoCity` lands at 0.471 texels per device px on iPhone 13. Crispness gains from vector apply on phones well below 9.885 (G0-18 **[D]**). None of this moves the art.
- **Determinism.** The stubbed generator run is deterministic and clock-free. But `genCityCanvas` paints a canvas rather than returning geometry, so a vector consumer means forking or refactoring the generator, not reusing the pinned function for free (gap-vecbudget C8 **[D]**).
- **How the reference tools anchor.** They agree with the KEY DISTINCTION. swisstopo builds each WMTS grid from the LV95 projection and swaps only the Time dimension between years (RA-swisstopo-time **[D]**). Its vector base map is enabled only in the frame its renderer supports (RA-swisstopo-vector-base, 2/2). Its 2D-to-3D handoff transfers view state (RA-swisstopo-3d, 2/2). 3D Tiles parent-child transforms preserve registration that was authored in; they do not create it (RA-tokyo, 2/2). Leaflet's CRS.Simple guide derives image bounds from measured coordinates, and Allmaps fits control-point transforms (RA-tools **[D]**). The common thread is registration into one frame; none of them stays anchored because it is vector (RA-principle **[D]**; the dissent struck the universal "every reference" framing). The owner's other references are unverified briefs or aesthetic references, not anchoring precedents: Bay Atlas, Azlen's painted flyover, Lombard Street, the dragon living maps (RA-collison-bayatlas, RA-azlen, RA-lombard-httyd, all **[D]**).

---

### 1.6 Cross-cutting constraints for any fix

- **Hashes.**
  - Anchor, sea-side, road-bearing or mask-path changes for Tamaron, Sokundo or Kanae move UG3 `cityCanvasSha`. That needs an owner-approved B0 re-cut, landed after the point-convention ruling (G0-3) and outside a Mobile 5-9 capture.
  - Canon registration (Epēshu, Summarch), the seam clip on the overlay element, the sea fill in `pCityWash`, and the lifecycle fixes touch no pinned function source and no hashed URL.
  - An RV-guarded river feed moves `atlas_fn_sha.genCityCanvas` only (G0-9).
  - A clip on a *new* pane would break UG7. A clip inside `genCityCanvas`, `washMake` or `sampleCityMask` breaks UG3 (gap-consumers C5 **[D]**).
- **Rasters.** Any change to a plate under `art/`, or a new PNG, needs a TILES_V bump and collides with A-U11 until Mobile 6 passes. The CSS route avoids both (G0-13 **[D]**, G8-8 **[D]**).
- **Mobile overlap.**
  - A-U11 hot-swaps only an overlay URL inside the same `cityBox` bounds (gap-mobile C3, 2/2).
  - A-U7 changes the zoom-step magnitudes on touch (C6, 2/2).
  - A-U9 makes the panel offset dynamic (C7 **[D]**).
  - A-U2 is failed, not pending (C2, 2/2).
  - `maps-site/index.html` is held by the Mobile 5-9 atlas lane. Code edits queue after it, with Mobile 9 and Mobile 14 checked for the river feed.
- **Ownership.**
  - The registration record, the seam ring and fill, the plan-river consumer and an atlas vector city are all unowned today (G7-11 **[D]**, G8-10 **[D]**, G0-10 **[D]**). Filigree slice C owns the look. Living owns fly choreography and H2z.
  - Sim adoption of chart geometry would re-baseline the whole `#s=epeshu` world through the shared `W.rng.gen` stream. Keep it out of the first cut, as a separate owner-gated item (G7-13 **[D]**).
- **Recommended order** (from the checks):
  1. Owner rulings: anchor table, point convention, Penthelon, Paga, Epēshu scale policy.
  2. Census second pass and the offline anchor tool, then data moves, sea re-measure, gazetteer, `cityCanvasSha` re-cut.
  3. Summarch and Hordon (data plus seat and waypoint).
  4. Epēshu registration record, read by `cityBox`, `cityMaxZ` and `nearestCity`, together with the clip and sea fill.
  5. Lifecycle fixes: ease-down, hysteresis, `ensureArrival` token, `exitChart`, `#view`.
  6. Road re-trace and per-leg medium; river feed.
  7. Only then a vector medium, if the owner wants one.

---

### 1.7 Gap items with a JSON row but no finding above

The JSON has a row for every gap item. Those not discussed above mainly concern other sections of the dossier. Two of them still bear on this section:

- gap-anchor C5 and gap-consumers C7 couple anchor moves to the planned Living sea-mask gate and to LV5/LV6.
- G0-21 and G7-10 place a traced canon skeleton or a ported fabric as rendering-medium assets that need registration first. A ported fabric would sit beside slice C as merged Leaflet paths: LC7 bars WebGL in maps-site, and its at-rest POI discs conflict with R7.

---

### 1.8 Refuted (0/2): not findings

**Shift sources**

- `overlay-swap-decode-flash`: the remove-then-add blank frame exists only inside the narrow hot-swap path. It does not happen on every first visit, LRU revisit or neighbour switch, and it moves nothing.
- `descend-lateral-slide`: a marker click calls only `openPlace` and never flies. Tier C is about z4.2-4.9, not 5.5. The remaining error is anchor-on-label at arrival, and flying to the marker would strand the view off the plan.
- `downsample-shimmer`: no geometry moves. "1/8 scale" holds only for villages at z7.8. The half-resolution fix collides with A-U11.
- `tier-d-hard-cut`: all 15 tier-d panes already have `transition:opacity .2s`. An inline ramp would override the tier-a/b CSS.
- `refit-recentres-epeshu`: the queued `setMinZoom` animation restores the reader's own centre about 250 ms later. It is a flicker, not a lost position.
- `tooltip-fixed-offsets`: Leaflet's built-in 6-px tooltip margins were ignored. The real gaps are 6-10 px, with no overlap and no zoom dependence.
- `way-spline-off-trace`: the spline adds ≤5.8 px. The trace itself is 20-80 px off the printed road, a fixed atlas offset that grows on screen rather than slides (see vop-01).
- `mask-coast-quantization`: the mask boundary sits about 0.15-0.8 px landward of the stroke centre, and the blur does not move straight edges. Not a material shift.

**Gap items**

- gap-anchor C4: 18 of the 19 tint-misread cities draw no coast, so the colour test only shades their land and wash. The "reliable rows agree" calibration rests on one point.
- gap-anchor C8: Penthelon has no JSON row, Hordon and Clickerhall are anchor errors rather than exceptions, and 38 anchors exceed 3 px, so an offDot field cannot start with two entries.
- tc-7: the atlas never reaches 11.5 over Epēshu, H2z is a crossfade into a poster, and fitting the chart does reduce the residual.
- vop-03: the Stony Ford road identity and direction are unsupported, (545,80) is a deliberate offset, and Summarch does have an anchor.
- vop-05: the bearing errors match, but re-tracing named ways is not pin-safe (it changes all three hashed canvases), and the method overreaches.
- vop-08: the district rings are to be relabelled or retired per an owner-flagged POLISH ruling, not re-registered, and Azrāken lies over the strait.
- gap-consumers C2: "the only quantified error" is not shown, and an anchor move would also invalidate q08-c08.
- gap-consumers C3: the Q1 window moves under an anchor move too.
- gap-consumers C4: the DEM probe shows no window hex changing class.
- gap-consumers C6: the `cityCanvasSha` pin covers off-dot cities, so anchor moves are not free of mobile rebases.
- gap-consumers C8: the sim and atlas link by place name, not coordinate, so no sim edit is forced.
- gap-consumers C10: 523,58 is also the factions seat and a route waypoint.
- gap-mobile C1: A-U2 failed and has not landed.
- gap-mobile C4: the off-dot and sea-side faults, and the "no shared code" claim, are not in its sources.
- gap-mobile C5: only two of the four fixes move glyphs, and "only after G9" overstates the gates.
- gap-mobile C9: A-U10 is owner-gated and not in HEAD, and its deltas need the stage height.
- gap-mobile C10: D7 is not in HEAD, and the A-U11 remark was unsourced there (now sourced by G0-13).
- G0-8: Ion Ephel's wrong river and sea come from its anchor sitting on its label, 31 px from the dot (covered by anchor-on-label), not from a mask-code defect.
- G0-16: an equivalent-radius comparison does not prove that the chart fits inside the smallest sim wall, and "any scale implies a sim edit" overreaches.

---

## 2. Registration: anchors, tie points, transforms, the scale tension, warping

*Cross-references: the shift sources these fixes answer are section 1 (§1.2); the vector city that would inherit this registration is section 3 (§3.7); the reference tools behind the method are section 4 (§4.9); ownership, hashes and the landing window are section 5 (§5.6, §5.7). Owner questions OQ1-OQ11 and OQ16 decide the open policy points.*

This section covers **where city art and city geometry sit relative to the atlas**. That covers the coast, rivers, dotted roads and the printed ring-dots of PatrinorModern. All code anchors are in the HEAD export: `maps-site/index.html` for the atlas and `index.html` for the sim, unless a path says otherwise. Coordinates are atlas px (1 world px = 1 atlas image px at NZ=4, y down, no flip against the z5 tiles).

**The key distinction, which every finding below carries.** REGISTRATION decides where art and geometry sit relative to atlas features, and it is the only thing that fixes misalignment. The RENDERING MEDIUM (raster or vector) brings crispness past the stretched image, interactivity, and road vertices that can be shared with the atlas. **A vector city placed through the same `cityBox` misaligns exactly as the raster does.** Each finding is tagged **[reg]** (registration), **[medium]** (rendering medium) or **[both]**.

Status markers: unmarked = survived (2/2 checks held); **[D]** = disputed (1/2), with what the checks disputed (the "failing check" is the one that did not hold; where its objection is cut off in the check text this says so); refuted items are listed at the end only. Where a check corrected an item, the corrected version is the one given. Gap-round items supersede the earlier dossier where they differ. Ids from different research lenses collide (several `C1`s), so the lens is named as a prefix: `canon` (C01-C24), `reg` (R1-R16), `gap-anchor`, `gap-coasts`, `gap-print`, `gap-consumers`, `gap-mobile`, `gap-vecbudget`, `gap-refs`, `G0`, `G7`, `G8`, `G9`. The G9 era-sheet items were supplied twice, identically; they are counted once.

**Screen magnification.** All errors below are in atlas px. On screen they grow by 2^(z-4): ×9.85 at z7.3, ×16 at z8, ×59.08 at Epēshu's live ceiling z9.885, ×181 at Z_STREET 11.5.

---

### 2.1 Diagnosis: a fixed placement error, magnified by zoom

- **[reg] The overlay does not drift; it is placed wrong, and zoom magnifies it** (reg R1 **[D]**). The base tiles, the `pCity` overlay and the wash all go through `px()` = `map.unproject([x,y],NZ)` under `L.CRS.Simple`. A misfit is therefore a constant error in world px: 2 world px of misfit is 2 screen px at z4, 32 at z8 and about 300 at Z_STREET 11.5. Leaflet's own rounding is at most about ±0.5 layer px per corner. The failing check narrowed the claim to what the sources show (its objection is truncated in the check text); the holding check added that the error sits mainly in the two canon rasters, which `cityBox` aspect-fits into a FOOT_R box. Changing the medium fixes none of it.
- **[reg] `cityBox` is a placement guess, not a fitted transform** (reg R2). `cityBox(a,w,h)` centres a box on `a.atlasX/atlasY` with half-size `footR(a.kind)` (`FOOT_R={capital:26,town:14,village:9,ruin:8}`, default 9) on the longer side, aspect-fit to `max(w,h)`, rotation always zero. That is 3 degrees of freedom: x/y translation and one uniform scale. It is the "Linear" (QGIS) or "straight" (Allmaps) transform class, but with zero ground control points and so no error estimate. `overlay-Epeshu.jpg` is **2863 wide × 3072 tall** (the brief had it transposed) and `overlay-Summarch.jpg` is 3072×2859.
- **[reg] The frame is a size by city class, not by what the art depicts** (canon C04). Epēshu's box is 48.5×52.0 atlas px (1211.87,1389)-(1260.33,1441), 0.016927 atlas px per art px. Summarch's box is 28.0×26.1. No fit to coast or river is attempted for canon art. Generated plans do fit the land/water mask, but only inside that fixed frame. Every city-space consumer inherits this frame (see 2.11).
- **[reg] Registration exists only in `cityBox`/`CITY_ANCHORS`** (canon C03). The atlas uses the charts in two ways. (a) Modal chart mode: `enterChart(name)` reads DETAIL (`data/detail-charts.json`) and shows the chart in its own pixel frame, with POIs from `data/chart-pois.json`. For kind 'tiles' (Epēshu, Summarch) it uses atZoom=maxz; kind 'image' charts use atZoom 0 and an imageOverlay. (b) Meshed-world overlay: `registerCityOverlay('Epēshu'|'Summarch', art/overlay-*.jpg, {poiRef: chart w/h})` puts a separate, downscaled file (same aspect as the pyramid) into `cityBox` and reprojects chart-px POIs through it. The modal chart is exactly framed; the overlay is not. No file carries any per-chart geo-reference (control points, rotation or scale).
- **[reg] Generated plans are registered to the coast by construction, and only to the coast** (reg R16; gap-vecbudget C7 **[D]**). `sampleCityMask` samples an n=5.4·rW z5 patch starting at (atlasX−1.35·rW)·2. `genCityCanvas` (MK branch) crops exactly the central 4·rW z5 px (2·rW world px), the same square as `cityBox(a,S,S)`, so mask frame and box frame agree. The plan follows the atlas coast only when `drawWater` is set (harbour, or sea side with distPx ≤25) and the patch holds at least 2.5% water. Rivers (`traits.river`) and roads (`buildCityStubs`) are not registered to the print. Gap-vecbudget C7's failing check: the plan centre shifts 0.07·S away from the sea only when `drawWater` is true, and otherwise the plan is placed purely by the anchor and footprint scale. Either way, a vector plan emitted in this frame misaligns exactly as the raster does.
- **[reg] Where generated plans get their registration inputs** (canon C24 **[D]**). 136 of 138 overlays are `genCityCanvas` plans: the 136 `city-anchors.json` entries, plus the two code-added anchors Summarch and Penthelon, minus the canon Epēshu and Summarch. They are registered in `ensureCityGen`/`ensureCityGenNow` with prov 'invented' and w/h = the GEN_SIZE canvas. The holding check said the claim overstates how much registration the plans carry: canon charts are registered only through `cityBox`, and generated plans get only the coast from the mask, with rivers and roads unregistered to the print. The failing check's objection is truncated.
- **[medium] Leaflet's overlay can only express a straight transform** (reg R7 **[D]**). In Leaflet 1.9.4, `ImageOverlay._reset` reads only the NW and SE corners and sets width and height independently. Its positioning and `_animateZoom` write the element's whole transform (`translate3d` plus `scale`), so a rotate or matrix set on the `<img>` itself is overwritten. `_initImage` adopts only an IMG element; a canvas has to go through `toDataURL`, which the atlas already does. So x/y stretch is free, but rotation or shear needs a nested element or an offline pre-warp. The failing check confirmed the mechanism and its objection is truncated; the holding check verified it in the vendored build.
- **[medium] Per-frame cost during flies** (reg R10 **[D]**). Custom transformed elements must carry `leaflet-zoom-animated` (transform-origin 0 0, 0.25 s transition during `.leaflet-zoom-anim`). `flyTo` fires 'zoom' on every rAF frame, and `ImageOverlay._reset` runs on each. `flyIntoCity`, `ensureArrival`, the marker and journey fly helpers and touch pinch all hit this path. A CSS-transform overlay costs O(1) per frame. A canvas warp that re-samples pixels on 'zoom' costs per-pixel work every frame, which the mobile budget rules out. The atlas already uses the frame for `worldOpacityUpdate` (setOpacity only). The failing check restated the narrowest supported form (its objection is truncated); the holding check added the call-site list and the existing `worldOpacityUpdate` use.

### 2.2 What there is to register

- **[both] Only two city-scale drawings exist** (canon C01). They are the DM's Epēshu and Summarch charts. Each exists twice. One copy is a 256 px tile pyramid at z0-3 (`charts/epeshu` 3914×4200, `charts/summarch` 4245×3951). The other is a single downscaled overlay JPG: `art/overlay-Epeshu.jpg` 2863×3072 (2,171,695 B) and `art/overlay-Summarch.jpg` 3072×2859 (1,283,357 B), a uniform ~0.73 downscale. Both are registered with prov 'canon' in `meshedWorldInit`. A vector rebuild can only be traced from real DM geometry here, and the trace should come from the native z3 pyramid, then be registered with control points on coast, rivers and road exits.
- **[reg] Chart POIs live in chart px and are mapped two ways** (canon C17 **[D]**). `data/chart-pois.json` has 28 POIs: Epēshu 19 (x 246-3782, y 121-3835), Summarch 9. Every entry has a 'd'; only Winehall and The Seat carry a wikiSlug. In chart mode, `enterChart` → `buildPOILayer` places them with `map.unproject([x,y],atZoom)` in the chart's own frame, so footprint changes cannot move them there. In the meshed world they go through `cityBox` via `poiRef`, so they move with the footprint. The failing check corrected the wikiSlug count; the holding check added the two-mapping point.
- **[reg] The region charts are 1:1 crops of the atlas, not better sources** (canon C19 **[D]**, corrected). `chart-Horat.jpg` (529×283) is a 1:1 pixel crop of the 4390×2188 atlas with its top-left at about atlas (843,1577). The scale ratio is **1.00, not 0.961**. Līrider, Istīron, Hordon, Maeges and Molider agree to 1-2 px. Not every `chart-*.jpg` is a region crop (see the refuted canon C18). For region geography and inter-city roads nothing beats the atlas, so registration has to use the atlas itself as its only reference.
- **[reg] Place-card art is not a layout source** (canon C20). The 28 `loc-*.jpg` files (all referenced by `data/location-art.json`, 295×250 to 900×900, used through `artFor(name)`) are oblique paintings with no plan view or scale. They can at most inform mood or palette.
- **[reg] `city-traits.json` holds no geometry** (canon C16 **[D]**). It has 27 city entries plus `_readme` (village 14, town 9, ruin 3, capital 1), with flavour and generator steers. The failing check corrected one detail: provenance is not on every field. kind, harbor, sea and wall are bare values; prov sits on patron and inn. Epēshu: wall true, harbour SSW 10 px; Summarch: wall true, harbour false. The atlas already has atlas-px vector precedents (`data/named-ways.json` pts). A per-city registration file belongs beside these files, not inside them.
- **[reg] Epēshu has no canon size** (canon C11). `wiki-places.json` gives no population figure, wall length, area or width for Epēshu, only structure: White Sea west, hills north, a crumbling inner wall around the inner city, outer districts (Outwall, Eelwynd, Kohabron, Old Quarry Lot), Wood Quay north of Whitestreet split by the Necropolis of the Founders, Bangeph Rock in the bay, Mount Mastery over the harbour, the Sun Road through the Forum to Tamaron. A true scale for Epēshu is a ruling for the DM, not a derivation. The chart's layout is consistent with the text. One check added that the Lepon page also says the ruins lie "roughly fifty miles west of present-day Epēshu", a distance the claim had missed.
- **[reg] The anchor table** (canon C14). `data/city-anchors.json` has 136 entries (village 115, town 17, ruin 3, capital 1). 111 carry prov 'trace' (102 villages, 9 towns); 25 have no prov. 11 have harbor true; 117 have sea null. `meshedWorldInit` hard-codes Summarch (town) and Penthelon (village, harbour), giving 138 runtime anchors. Every anchor without canon art gets a seeded plan when the map is near it (`ensureCityGen` once zoom passes 6.5).
- **[reg] Footprint class does not track the printed glyph** (canon C15 **[D]**, corrected). The atlas prints 15 ◉ major cities (`maps_markers.json` mag:true), but only Epēshu is kind 'capital' (FOOT_R 26). Hordon, Cyrikon and Tamaron are 'town' (14). Summarch ('town', 14) and Penthelon ('village', 9) are **not in `city-anchors.json`**: they are hard-coded in `meshedWorldInit`. Eight to ten of the 15 ◉ cities have no anchor at all (the two checks say eight and ten). So a Māgosh-class Penthelon gets a hamlet's footprint. A data-driven size, or a per-city override, is needed.
- **[reg] The era sheets are one registration target, not three** (G9-1; G9-2, G9-4, G9-5, G9-6 **[D]**). `tiles-war` and `tiles-imperial` at z5 are the modern sheet with recoloured washes: 748 of 1260 tile pairs are byte-identical. The rest differ by realm tint (war repaints Leponnia and Ībūnor orange), the imperial sheet's removed Underwall shading, and the legend. Inside every anchor's 2.5·FOOT_R circle (139 points), the ink (luma <110) agrees with modern within 1 atlas px. War: max 0.71 px, rms ≤0.107. Imperial: max 1.12 px at Heron's House only, where thin river edges cross the luma threshold once shading is removed: a contrast change, not a displacement. Rings match within 0.19 px (war) and 0.11 px (imperial), road dots within about 0.22 px; 15 ◉ on every sheet; Summarch ◉ at (542.0,50.0) on all three. The failing checks on G9-5 and G9-6 corrected the counts (those on G9-2 and G9-4 reproduced the numbers; their objections are truncated): the 'printed-river' rows of `rivers-vs-plans.json` are **19** (18 generated plans plus Epēshu), not 18; Kolens has no ink in its circle at all; where ink exists, two points have none on some sheets. **Consequence:** a per-city registration record measured once on the modern sheet is valid in every era; `setEra` needs no hook.
- **[reg] Colour masks differ by era because of tint, not geometry** (G9-3 **[D]**: the failing check reproduced the medians, patch geometry and chamfer maxima, and its objection is truncated; G9-7 survived). The numpy port of `buildCityMask` (luma ≥110 weight, r−b ≤54 water, radius-2 box blur, ratio ≥0.5, border-connected water) gives a different mask per sheet: median water IoU vs modern on the `sampleCityMask` patch is 0.988 (war) and 0.291 (imperial). That must not be read as a coast move. In HEAD this cannot hurt: `sampleCityMask` always fetches `tv('tiles/5/'+tx+'/'+ty+'.jpg')`, `MASKQ`/`WASHC` are keyed by city only, and `setEra` swaps only `curBase` (plus base opacity and the radio inputs). Sampling era sheets instead would be mixed: war 70→74 of 115 inland no-sea anchors read >50% water, imperial 70→14. **Any seam clip must stay modern-sourced, and `mask.water` inland must not be trusted on any sheet.**
- **[reg] No per-era registration fields; the era defect is colour** (G9-8 **[D]**). The per-city record (anchor plus translate+scale) needs no era fields, and era mode does not need to hide `pCity`/`pCityWash`. A seam clip from the modern print mask fits war and imperial coasts within the ~1 px mask rim. What differs is tone: `washUrl` paints the wash at .94 alpha in the modern land hue (~(197,177,128) at Epēshu), and `genCityCanvas` uses the same hue for its surround, while the Leponnia war base is ~(197,148,107) and imperial ~(203,148,107) (ΔG≈29, ΔB≈21): a tan disc around Leponnia plans in era mode. That is colour work for filigree slice C, not registration. The failing check (medium) confirmed the code quotes and the hue numbers; its objection lies in the truncated remainder of the check text.

### 2.3 Anchors: the cheapest registration fix

- **[reg] Most anchors that disagree with their marker sit on their label, not their dot** (gap-anchor C1 **[D]**). Of 138 anchors, 121 match a `maps_markers.json` Settlements entry by folded name, and Lepon the Old matches "Lepon" through an alias **supplied by the probe** (the code has no alias map). 82 are within 3 px, 39 are farther; with Lepon the Old (6.96 px) that makes 40 pairs. The other 16 are Leponnia sim towns whose gazetteer coordinates are copied from the anchors. The 0.9.11/0.9.12 passes re-snapped markers to the printed dots and `city-anchors.json` was never updated: 38 of the 40 markers sit on their printed ○/◉, while the anchors mostly sit on the first letter of their label. The failing check reproduced the counts and noted the alias is the probe's, not the code's. The holding check corrected the proposal: **do not resolve anchors from markers at runtime or by a blanket marker-wins rule**; derive them in an offline deterministic tool with reviewed exceptions. A stale anchor misregisters raster and vector plans by the same amount; moving it fixes only the centre, not scale or orientation of DM art.
- **[reg] Verdicts on the 40 pairs: 37 move, 1 keep, 2 owner calls** (gap-anchor C2 **[D]**: the failing check verified the verdict counts and only questioned wording, the source calling Drāmūz's glyph "the printed dot" where the table says ◉; the holding check made the printed glyph the truth over both records). Keep: **Drāmūz**. Its anchor (1335,1733.8) is on the printed dot and 0.2 px from a Sun Road vertex; its marker (1344,1769) is 35 px S in open water and is the record in error. Resolving by name would put the city in the sea. Owner calls: **Paga** (marker also off-print, on the 'P' of its label; printed ○ ≈15 px NE near (2074,809), so both records need re-snapping) and **Penthelon**. **Hordon** moves: its anchor (1139,1730) equals the Maeges anchor and is 1.0 px from the Maeges marker, so it was transcribed from Maeges's dot.
- **[reg] Penthelon: a stale off-dot reason and a colour-test trap** (gap-anchor C3). The marker (1942,755) sits on the printed ◉ at the tip of an inked finger with a dotted road to it. The anchor (1962,644) is about 113 px north, on a tint boundary. The 0.9.7 'lake shore' reason is stale. Moving the anchor would make the plan mostly water, because `buildCityMask`'s r−b ≤54 test cannot tell the olive region tint (r−b 45-48) from lake water (47-50): the footprint at the ◉ reads about 2% land, the current anchor 61-62% only because it sits on an untinted band. In `genCityCanvas`, traits are harbor true, sea null, so at the marker `mask.bear` is null and the side falls back to a seeded pick. Moving Penthelon cleanly needs a land/water source other than the colour test (inked coast or the filigree DEM), which touches the UG3-pinned `sampleCityMask`: an owner decision.
- **[reg] The living sea-mask done-when is coupled to the anchors** (gap-anchor C5 **[D]**). Under an approximation of the unbuilt `tools/living-sea-mask.js` (8×8 atlas-px blocks, colour test), 14 of 38 in-scope pairs pass at the anchor and 21 at the marker; moving fixes 8 (Damoklir, Zhomphek, Līrider, Ion Ephel, Bēgroth Amar, Gakapē, Lendānak, Leprekhōr) and breaks 1 (Ghōdar, a tint case); 16 fail at both, all in tinted regions. The rule reads anchor x/y, harbour and `sea.distPx`, so decide anchors before the tool is written, re-measure `sea.side`/`distPx` for any harbour anchor that moves, and keep the tool off the colour threshold. The failing check restricted the numbers to the 38 in-scope rows under an approximated rule.
- **[reg] Approach bearings change for six of the 40 pairs** (gap-anchor C6 **[D]**, corrected from five). A re-implementation of `cityApproaches` (`segCircleHits` + `mergeAngles`, R=2.5·FOOT_R) changes the approach set for Summarch, Tamaron, Lepon the Old, Harney, Drāmūz and **Hordon** (which gains a sea approach at 297.2°). Tamaron: land shifts 12.0° and 7.8°; sea [308.9,119.0,4.8] → [323.4,1.1]. Harney loses both land approaches. Gates and quays move with the anchor only in seeded plans (`cityApproaches` is cached on `a._appr`; `genCityCanvas` is its only consumer). Summarch's change is invisible (canon art), and Drāmūz is a keep. Tamaron is one of the three plans `cityCanvasSha` pins, so moving it forces that rebaseline.
- **[reg] Polylines were drawn through the old anchors** (gap-anchor C7 **[D]**: the failing check verified the entries and distances, with its objection truncated; the holding check made four corrections, including that only anchors that actually move need polylines re-checked). `party-route.json` has two 'Summarch' entries, (523,58) and (545,80) (28 px S of the ◉). The Sun Road has vertices 0.2 px from Drāmūz, 0.5 px from Lepon the Old and 1.7 px from Tamaron. So any anchor that **actually moves** needs its nearby route and way points re-checked (`buildApprPolys` feeds gates and stubs); Drāmūz does not move. Moving Summarch's centre to the ◉ does not register the DM chart, whose own ◉ may not be at the image centre: canon art still needs control-point registration, which a vector redraw does not supply.
- **[reg] Summarch's anchor is 20 px off its own ring** (canon C13 **[D]**; Summarch ties). `CITY_ANCHORS['Summarch']={atlasX:523,atlasY:58,...}` in `meshedWorldInit` (commented "sourced from maps_markers") is ~20 px WSW of the printed ◉ at (541.75,49.75); `maps_markers.json` and `gazetteer.json` say (542,52). The 28 px footprint x 509-537 excludes the ring (which starts at x≈537) and mostly covers hatched sea west of the coast. The failing check verified the offsets and box (its objection is truncated). The holding check added that `data/factions.json` uses the same (523,58) for the Allhammers seat (used by 'show on the map'); with `data/party-route.json` (refuted gap-consumers C10) the fix is a **three-file data edit**, not one literal. Penthelon's anchor is 113 px from its marker by design (comment: 'wiki marker floats in the lake'), which gap-anchor C3 shows is stale.
- **[reg] The census second pass adds 8 moves** (G0-1). `anchors-off-print.json` audits the 98 anchors the 40-pair table never covered: 8 move (Sokundo, Kanae, Shengo, Pomos, Ishin, Pish, Ītor Hāspira, Paril), 1 keep (Sjefenhold, an uncharted canon place kept with an explicit offDot reason), 2 owner calls (Ūmak-nūs-Ion, Kolens). Totals: **45 move / 2 keep / 4 owner calls over 51 anchors**; the other 87 of 138 are already on a printed ring. The two keeps mean different things: Drāmūz = anchor right, marker to be fixed (a queued filigree R21 data item); Sjefenhold = off-dot on purpose.
- **[reg] Verified move targets for the hashed trio** (G0-2 **[D]**). Recomputed from HEAD z5 tiles (enclosed-hole detector, luma <100): Sokundo ○ (1478.0,1450.0), 5.81 px from its anchor (1477.7,1444.2); Kanae ○ (1523.0,1490.0), 7.45 px from (1521.5,1482.7). With Tamaron (◉ (1659,1554), marker (1659,1556)), all three `cityCanvasSha` cities in `tools/mobile-capture.js` have targets. The failing check reproduced both rings (its objection is truncated); the holding check framed them as candidate targets for a rebaseline that still needs owner approval.
- **[reg] The point convention must be ruled before any B0 cut** (G0-3). `sampleCityMask` rounds `(atlasX−1.35*rW)*2`, so ring centre, census marker (ring−(1,0)) and anchor convention (ring−(1,1)) each give a different mask window, a different `cityCanvasSha`, and different gates: Tamaron's sea set is [319.3,1.6] at the ring centre but [317.7,113.6,3.5] at ring−(1,1). Sokundo and Kanae sea fields were recorded at the old points and must be re-measured in the same edit or explicitly frozen, or a second hash change follows.
- **[reg] The census pass must come before the anchor tool** (G0-4). Sokundo, Kanae and Shengo have no `maps_markers.json` entry, so a by-name derivation cannot move them. Six prov 'trace' records (Pomos, Ishin, Pish, Ītor Hāspira, Ūmak-nūs-Ion, Paril) have marker within 1 px of anchor and both are off the print, so a by-name derivation would copy the error. Queue "Census second pass — orphan ○ dots & unmarkered towns" before (or with) the derivation tool, or give the tool hand overrides.
- **[reg] Snap and lint by name, not by distance** (G0-5 **[D]**). Paril sits 0-1.44 px from a printed ring that belongs, by eye, to Mūmakon; four more (Ishin, Pish, Ītor Hāspira, Ūmak-nūs-Ion) would snap to a neighbour's ring by nearest distance. A 3 px anchor-to-marker lint is not sufficient. The holding check: "label-aware" cannot mean OCR (labels are raster text with diacritics); it means one reviewed name-to-ring table that both the snap tool and the lint read.
- **[reg] Shengo is convention-sensitive; Pomos loses its gates** (G0-6 **[D]**). The South Lane passes 22.3 px from Shengo's ring centre and 22.9 px from ring−(1,1), against R=22.5; ring centre and census-marker conventions gain two sea approaches (213.2°, 187.5°), the anchor convention none. Pomos loses both land gates (356.6°, 197.6°) under every convention. The failing check: sea approaches never create a gate in `genCityCanvas` (they only steer the quay bearing when `drawWater&&WD&&sv&&seaAppr.length`), so decide Shengo's sea from the atlas mask at the moved anchor first.
- **[reg] Gazetteer regeneration after anchor moves** (gap-consumers C11 **[D]**). `tools/build-gazetteer.js` `add()` adds markers first and an anchor only when no marker has the same `fold()`ed name. The second check counts 119 anchors shadowed by a marker and **17** reaching `gazetteer.json` through the anchor path (Sokundo, Kanae, Mago, Aldorūs, Rēketis, Āglaz, Mīth Mirdānak, Pharzeban, Sardil, Perōna, Tarap, Thalbaen, Medem, Shengo, Kardunash, Rhup, Lepon the Old). The first check's count (about 29 of 33) disagrees and is not reconciled. Lepon the Old reaches it because its marker is named 'Lepon'. Regenerate the gazetteer after moving any of these.

### 2.4 Tie points and transforms

#### The transform menu and how tools report error

- **[reg] Minimum GCPs per transform** (reg R3 **[D]**): straight (translation + uniform scale) 2; Helmert/similarity 2; affine/polynomial 1 3; projective 4; polynomial 2 6; polynomial 3 10; TPS 3 in Allmaps (its TPS includes an affine part), "technically 10, usually more" in QGIS 3.34. Collinear points count as fewer. GDAL auto-fits at most order 2. Each is a small deterministic solve that fits inline. The holding check added that the render path caps which transforms are usable, since `L.imageOverlay` (used by `cityBox` and `enterChart`) only places an axis-aligned box.
- **[reg] Least squares vs exact interpolation** (reg R4 **[D]**). Helmert, affine and polynomial fits over-determined by extra GCPs give per-point residuals and average noise. TPS passes exactly through every GCP, so its residual RMS is always 0 and one bad click bends the art locally; smoothing turns it back toward a least-squares polynomial. With few, noisy GCPs, prefer Helmert or affine. The holding check scoped this to the two canon overlays (generated plans need no GCP fit); the failing check's objection is truncated.
- **[reg] Report residuals and held-out error** (reg R5). QGIS lists per-GCP residuals and RMS; Map Warper computes per-GCP and overall error; `gdalwarp -refine_gcps` drops outliers for polynomial fits. For TPS, held-out (leave-one-out) error is the only meaningful check; no reachable tool doc names it, so this is standard statistics applied, not a cited feature. **Proposal:** commit each canon city's GCPs (art px → atlas px) as data and have a node tool print per-GCP residuals, RMS (atlas px and screen px at z8, ×16) and leave-one-out misses as the acceptance gate. A vector city placed through `cityBox` needs exactly the same gate.
- **[reg] The atlas, not the art, limits tie supply** (reg R6 **[D]**). Epēshu's footprint is about 52 atlas px; only coarse atlas features can be matched (coast turns, harbour mouth, solid-line rivers, dotted roads, the ring). The original estimate (4-8 GCPs at ±1 px, pointing to Helmert) is superseded by the measured tie work below, which found far fewer. Allmaps' distortion measures (log2sigma for area, twoOmega for angle) can report how far a fit bends the art. The failing check's objection is truncated; the holding check limited it to the two canon overlays with GCPs stored as static data. (Separately, `traced-roads.json` is not loaded by the atlas; see reg R14.)
- **[reg] No axis flip** (reg R15). Art px and CRS.Simple world px both run y down, so a fit between them needs no flip. Convert to LatLng only through `px()` (under CRS.Simple, lat = −y/16, lng = x/16 at NZ=4). Leaflet lat/lng and north-up GDAL geotransforms bring the flip back and must stay out of the fit.
- **[reg] What the registration tools agree on** (gap-refs RA-tools **[D]**). The Leaflet 1.9.4 CRS.Simple guide derives image bounds by measuring pixel distances between known coordinates and extrapolating. Allmaps fits one control-point transform and applies it to rasters and vectors alike. `L.ImageOverlay` adds at most about 1 px of rounding. The Azgaar→Watabou link and TownGeneratorOS work in their own frames and are never registered to the world map. StreetView3DOverlay calls hand-tuned placement "not very precise". The failing check: the guide does not say "not from approximate bounds"; that was a gloss. **Port the method, not the libraries**, and only for the two canon overlays.

#### Epēshu ties (survived 2/2)

Anchor (1236.1,1415) on the printed ◉; art 2863×3072; box (1211.87,1389)-(1260.33,1441).

- Today: tie rms 8.0, max 12.95 (T1-T5 = 5.0, 12.9, 4.9, 3.8, 9.4); coast chamfer rms 9.30, max 19.96; water IoU 0.362.
- Five candidate ties: T1 west-shore knee / Necropolis knee; T2 eastmost water reach N of the centre; T3 east road (Sun Road exit); T4 south road; T5 ring centre / Epēshin Forum.
- **Correction (second check): strict identifiable ties = 0**, not 2. T1 pairs a ~10 px drafting promontory around the ring with a necropolis bump, usable at most as a partial x-only constraint. T5 is a symbol convention. T3/T4 are frame-edge exits (line constraints, not points). T2 is ambiguous by ~400 art px.

| model | rms | max | leave-one-out rms / max | verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| today (`cityBox`) | 8.0 | 12.95 | n/a | baseline |
| translation + uniform scale | 5.25 | 6.84 | 9.13 / 12.58 | s 0.825×, centre +4.6 px E; held-out worse than today |
| similarity | 5.03 | 6.15 | 9.89 / 15.53 | +5.4° rotation gains 0.2 px; rotation unsupported |
| affine | 3.83 | 6.51 | 19.91 / 38.42 | overfit; anisotropic 1.010× / 0.673× |
| scale on T1+T5 only | 22.7 | 42.95 | n/a | 1.83×: a ring-clearance artifact, not geography |
| TPS | n/a | n/a | n/a | skipped: 0 high-confidence ties (6 needed) |

- Coast-shape (water-mask IoU) matching is multimodal and flat: 0.32× (IoU 0.876, the 0.3× search bound, rot +12°), 0.55× (rot +33°, IoU 0.791) and 1.01× (rot 0, centre 18.5 px **S** of the anchor, IoU 0.737). 14 entries above 0.6× lie within 0.02 IoU of the best (0.61-1.04×, −18..+30°). Every mode puts the printed ring outside the drawn city. **The atlas is too simple at this scale to register the chart.**
- **Corrected recommendation:** translation + uniform scale only, turned into an explicit per-city override (centre dx,dy and scale) **chosen by stated policy, not fitted**. Policy: the printed ring falls inside the built-up area near the Forum; the art waterfront meets the atlas shore at the ring's latitude; the Sun Road exit stays on the atlas's eastern dotted road. **Over the policy range (0.83-1.0× with the centre 0-5 px E), tie rms is 5.3-8 px**, not "about 5": 5.25 is reached only at the fitted optimum (~0.83-0.9×, +4.6 px E). Recomputed: 0.83×/0E 6.97; 0.83×/+2.5E 5.65; 0.83×/+4.6E 5.25. Rotation, affine and TPS are rejected.
- The conflict in the NW sector (Eelwynd and the upper waterfront over atlas sea by ~12-15 px) cannot be removed by any similarity that keeps the ring inside the city. It must be handled at the seam (2.7). A vector or procedural layer generated **from** the atlas mask and road stubs would not have the NW conflict; the canon raster always will.
- Implied scale: the repo has no ground calibration for Epēshu. An unverified guess (house fills ~1 m per art px; Epēshu-Lepon about a day's walk) would put the true city near 0.2× today, kept out of the recommendation.

#### Summarch ties (survived 2/2)

Literal anchor (523,58); art 3072×2859 north-up with compass rose and a 0-500 m bar; box (509,44.97)-(537,71.03).

- Today: tie rms 27.82, max 32.47. The atlas shows at this spot only the ring, one dotted road leaving SSE, one solid river (an arc N of the ring, then S along its W side) and labels: no coast, lake, wall or harbour.
- **Correction (second check): identifiable ties = 2** under the tie-entry rule (3 if the river counts as one curve). The ring ↔ building-mass centroid is a valid point tie at symbol level (ring re-measured at (541.75,49.75), 10 atlas px across). The SSE road is a bearing tie: art ~36° E of S against an atlas chord of **31.5°** (not 34°). All three river point ties are frame edges or a near-collinear run.
- Fits: ring+road (2 points) is degenerate for every model. River-only translation+scale gives rms 2.49 at scale 1.004 (LOO 4.5-5.4); similarity puts the town centroid at (543.9,36.0), N of the ring across the river. All five ties: floor rms ~6.8, set by the ring-vs-river conflict, not noise.
- **Recommended: translation only** to the printed ring centre (541.75,49.75), a shift of (+18.75,−8.25) ≈ 20.4 px; keep FOOT_R 14 and north-up. Not a registration fix but an authoring choice: a north-up move to ~(548,34.5) lays the art river on the atlas arc (chamfer 0.64) but puts the town north of the river and 14 px from its ring. The DM drew the town on the river's north bank; the atlas puts the ring south/east of the river; canon text has the Stony River "girdling its southern flank". Reconciling them is for the owner, outside registration.
- A vectorised Summarch placed through `cityBox` at (523,58) would misalign by the same ~20-30 px; vector adds only crispness, interactivity and road vertices that could be welded to the SSE road bearing.

#### The hand trace from the chart-conditioned prototype

- **[reg] The trace coast is a coarse check, not a solver** (G7-4; G7-5 **[D]**). `prototypes/data/epeshu-trace.json` coast (24 hand vertices, densified to 2,693 points) against the print coast (numpy `buildCityMask` port, mainland, islets <400 px² filled), one-sided: today rms 7.70, p95 15.35, max 17.54, mean vector SE 134° (the direction the trace must move to meet the print); 0.6×+3E rms 3.43, max 6.18; 0.8×+3E rms 4.09, max 10.73. It duplicates the coasts work below and is not a new tie set. The trace sits 36.6 art px rms from the chart's own water-class coast (≈0.62 atlas px at 1.0×). A free translation of the open polyline slides 13-18 atlas px south onto an unrelated stretch of coast, so a one-sided chamfer fit of it is degenerate. Do not fit a transform to it; use it to check a policy placement, scored in both directions. The failing check reproduced the numbers but said the prose around them overreaches.
- **[reg] Road exits add at most 1-2 weak line constraints** (G7-6 **[D]**: the failing check reproduced every number from the throwaway scripts, and its objection is truncated). Recomputed printed dotted-road dots (vop method, 140 local dots): the printed roads cross each placed box at exactly 2 points (today E edge (1260.3,1416.9) heading 63.5° and S edge (1239.5,1441.0) heading 109.5°; screen angles, 0=E, 90=S). The trace has 4 frame exits (N×2, E×2) plus a short S; the print has 2. Use road exits as a weak heading check or tie-breaker, never as ties; the best (Marble Way S end) needs ~312 art px of extrapolation.
- **[reg] Trace POIs are derivative** (G7-8). The trace's 19 POIs are `chart-pois.json` converted to overlay px and rounded (residual mean 0.60, max 0.92 pyramid px). Four names drop macrons (Epeshin Forum, The Mamban, Thukin Amphitheatre, Temple of the Ekromatkar). Take POIs and spellings from `chart-pois.json`, joined on n. POIs move with whatever transform is adopted and add no registration tie.

### 2.5 Three coasts at Epēshu and the policy placements

Three coasts meet at Epēshu: the printed atlas (print water mask from the z5 tiles), the sim's DEM 0 m contour (`decodeEpeshuHF`, `const SEA_Y = 0` in `index.html`) and the DM chart's water class under `cityBox`. Static numpy measurements, one-sided nearest-point chamfer in atlas px. These figures are their own baseline; they agree only roughly with the tie tables (8.48 vs 9.3 today), see the refuted gap-coasts tc-9.

- **[reg] The Epēshu ◉ registration fact** (canon C12 **[D]**, corrected). The anchor (1236.1,1415) is on the centre of the printed double ring; the marker (1236,1417) is 2 px south. The ◉ sits on a **land promontory** (not a thin peninsula), with water W and NW and a ragged coast. The 48.5×52 box spills over atlas water. The chart's coast runs roughly N-S with sea along its whole western edge, so the coasts cannot coincide by translation and uniform scale alone. Both checks agree registration is needed whatever the medium.
- **[reg] Print and DEM are effectively one coast near Epēshu** (gap-coasts tc-1 **[D]**). DEM-to-print over the window [1060,1860)×[1240,2040): rms 1.33, p50 1.03, p95 2.31, max 6.79 (one-sided); within 60 px rms 1.51; near-Epēshu mainland symmetric rms 1.50, max 5.43. The failing check: the window figures are one-directional, and the reverse direction is large in the raw window because of sea lettering and islets, so "one coast" holds for the mainland near Epēshu, not window-wide. The print mask (what `buildCityMask`/`sampleCityMask` already produce) is the registration target and seam reference.
- **[reg] No DEM-to-atlas correction transform** (gap-coasts tc-2 **[D]**). Mean DEM→print vector 0.31 px at 150° (window), 0.46 px at 137° (60 px). Best rigid shift 1.73 px SSE (60 px) / 1.82 px S (window) only takes mainland rms 1.50 → 1.02. Grid conventions (800/768 vertex, cell-centre +0.5, 800/767) differ by ≤0.11 px rms. The failing check verified the numbers (objection truncated); the holding check corrected "all of the error is in the chart": a 1.3-1.5 px residual stays, as local noise; the fix goes into the chart's placement and the seam clip.
- **[reg] The DM chart is the outlier** (gap-coasts tc-3). Under today's `cityBox`: chart→print rms 8.48, p95 16.89, max 21.05, mean offset (4.34,4.39) SE; print→chart inside the box rms 14.93, max 33.62; chart→DEM rms 6.88, max 18.84. 666 px² of chart land over atlas sea and 0 px² of chart sea over atlas land. Screen: 84 / 501 / 1535 px rms at z7.3 / 9.885 / 11.5. A vector city placed through the same `cityBox` carries the same error.
- **[reg] Policy placements** (gap-coasts tc-4; seam areas tc-5). Scale relative to today's half-size; E = centre shift in atlas px.

| placement | chart→print rms / max | chart→DEM rms / max | land over sea / sea over land (px²) |
|---|---|---|---|
| today (1.0×, 0) | 8.48 / 21.05 | 6.88 / 18.84 | 666.5 / 0 |
| 0.6× +3E | **3.58 / 6.66** | **3.57 / 6.84** | 91.1 / 75.3 (10.0% / 8.3%) |
| 0.6× +8E | 4.82 / 9.07 | 5.35 / 9.62 | 39.3 / 106.8 (4.3% / 11.8%) |
| 0.8× +3E | 4.69 / 13.0 | 3.71 / 10.93 | 212.4 / 34.6 (13.2% / 2.2%) |
| 0.8× +8E | 4.83 / 11.02 | 4.48 / 8.69 | not measured |

A free translate+scale search runs to the edge of its box (scale 0.5, dy +9..+10 S), so **coast shape cannot choose the scale**: it stays a stated policy. Even the best leaves ~3.6 px rms, hundreds of screen px at z9.9, so the seam still needs a clip or fill. A check on tc-4 (which survived) added that the 0.6× "win" mostly reflects the coast preferring the smaller size.
- **[reg] Shrinking swaps one seam error for the other** (gap-coasts tc-5). Today the chart only puts land over sea; shrinking trades that for sea over land. The earlier "7-13%" counted one direction. A clip against atlas water fixes only land-over-sea; sea-over-land needs the chart's own sea (a flat RGB(119,153,136), keyable with tolerance) made transparent or filled.
- **[reg] Filigree B-38's "DEM shore up to 10 atlas px off" measured the DEM against the chart, not the atlas** (gap-coasts tc-6). `q08-c08` scanned rows at atlas y = 1389+cy/4200·52, the today's-`cityBox` frame. The DEM is not off the atlas; the chart is. Clipping relief to the print mask matches the DEM to ~1.4 px; clipping to a registered chart still trims relief ~3.6 px rms off the atlas coast, in both directions. Slice C should clip to the print mask.
- **[reg] The atlas never reaches z11.5 over Epēshu** (gap-coasts tc-8 **[D]**). `cityMaxZ` = min(Z_STREET, NZ+log2(3072/52)) = 9.885 for Epēshu; `worldCityUpdate` calls `setMaxZoom` with it whenever Epēshu is nearest. Correction from the holding check (the failing check verified the numbers; its objection is truncated): **11.5 is not a street-view handoff** (no handoff exists in code); `Z_STREET` is the global ceiling (initial map maxZoom, base tile maxZoom). Quote atlas shift budgets over Epēshu at z9.885 (×59.08).
- **[reg] The static seam baseline reproduces** (G0-12). An independent numpy rebuild at 0.6×+3E gives box [1224.56,1399.4]-[1253.64,1430.6], area 907.21, chart water 16.07%, land-over-sea 91.9 px², sea-over-land 74.4 px² against the file's 91.1 / 75.3 / 906.8 / 16.1%. Differences are grid quantization. The P13 static targets hold.

### 2.6 The scale tension: symbol, chart and true geography

- **[reg] The atlas prints its own scale** (canon C05). Bottom-left legend: 'Imperial (Cyrikoi) Miles' 0-600 (ticks 150/300/450/600) and 'Sārmathic River-Measures' 0-600. On the z5 tiles: 600 mi = 1318 z5 px = 659 atlas px → **0.910 mi per atlas px** (1.098 px per mile). One River-Measure ≈ 1.11 mi. The projection looks conic, so a single constant holds only near the legend latitude.
- **[reg] The code uses a different constant** (canon C06 **[D]**). `var MI_PER_NATIVE=600/723; // 600 Imperial (Cyrikon) mi ≈ 723 native px` (0.830 mi/px, about 9% off the printed bar), feeding `updScale`. Its origin is undocumented (only the comment and the CHANGELOG line "Cyrikon-miles scale bar"). The holding check: the facts are right, but this is not an anchoring question and the choice of constant is not really open. The failing check's objection is truncated (it concerns the grep for 723).
- **[reg] Canon prose confirms only the order of magnitude** (canon C08 **[D]**). The traced Sun Road (`named-ways.json`, 'Trace approximate') is 2053.9 px from Drāmūz to the (2600,1862) vertex = 1704 mi at 0.830 (568 leagues at 3 mi, 1.4% off canon's 560) or 1870 mi at 0.910. Lepon-Epēshu anchors are 89.75 px apart = 74 or 82 mi vs canon's "fifty miles" or 20 leagues. The atlas is authoritative, so its scale governs; prose cannot refine it beyond about ±25%. The holding check said the code's MI_PER_NATIVE governs as the atlas's scale and prose only confirms magnitude; the failing check's objection is truncated. Because the printed bar is on the authoritative plate too, this section keeps the choice of constant as an owner question.
- **[reg] Summarch's chart has a printed metre bar** (canon C09 **[D]**). Labelled 0m / 250 / 500 under the cartouche "Sun's March". On the z3 pyramid: ticks at x=138, 592.5, 1047.5, so 500 m = 909.5 px (1.819 px/m); the chart covers about 2334×2172 m; the wall ring is about 0.86 km across. On the overlay JPG the same bar is 658 px = 500 m. The failing check: **the bar is valid only inside the chart**. It sizes buildings, lanes and walls in a Summarch vector layer, and gives the street track true metres, but it must not be used to size the city in atlas px.
- **[reg] The insets are exaggerated symbols** (canon C10 **[D]**; Summarch implied scale). Summarch's 2.33×2.17 km chart fills a 28.0×26.1 px box, which at 0.910 mi/px is 41×38 km: a **~17.6× linear exaggeration** (~16× at 0.830). At true scale the whole plate is ~1.6-1.8 atlas px, smaller than its own printed ring (~10 px) and comparable to the river stroke (~2 px). Epēshu's box is 71×76 km at 0.910; its chart has no bar. The failing check kept the arithmetic (re-measuring the overlay bar at 658 px = 500 m) but said the applies_to_us conclusion and two phrases overreach. This section's reading: registration of the insets is symbolic (town on its mark, coast on the right side, roads on their printed bearings), not true-scale.
- **[reg] Two scale criteria conflict for Epēshu** (G0-15 **[D]**). The print coast favours 0.6×+3E (3.58 rms). The sim's hand-placed labels favour ~1.0× (rms-radius ratio 1.12 with 13 labels, 0.94 without Fairy Arch, median per-pair 1.01). The sim extent favours ≥1.0× (built-up equivalent radius equals the sim disc only at 1.53-1.63×). No tc-4 placement satisfies both. Per-name label rms cannot pick the scale (collapsing all POIs to the anchor already scores 221-226 m against 209-265 m), and direction disagreements (median 53°) are scale-independent. The failing check reproduced the sim-side criteria (objection truncated); the holding check said to keep the two criteria apart and that the coast fit is weak (the owner picks within roughly 0.6-0.8×): registration against the atlas decides placement, and the sim follows.
- **[both] One trace, two consumers, but not one fabric** (G7-12 **[D]**). One chart-conditioned trace can feed atlas and sim through one chain: art px → atlas (per-city override) → sim world (atlas = (1060,1240) + grid·800/768, 11.2647 m per atlas px). The seeded fabric cannot share one scale: 1 art px is 0.19 / 0.15 / 0.11 m at 1.0 / 0.8 / 0.6×. The prototype's lots (12×18 art px) become 2.3×3.4 m at 1.0×, while the sim's `planParcelsFor` lots are 8.5-11 × 7.5-10.5 m, a 4-7× linear mismatch. The holding check reframed it: the atlas fabric is a drawn symbolisation with no metric size, so the conflict is sim metric lots vs the chart's drawn density. It belongs with the registration scale question.
- **[medium] Raising resolution does not change the softness at the cap** (G0-17). `cityMaxZ` reads the registered dims, so a higher-res source (the 3914×4200 pyramid, a bigger GEN_SIZE) or a policy scale raises the cap exactly as much as native zoom: upscale at the cap stays equal to DPR (3 on an iPhone 13), and the soft band stays log2(DPR) wide. Epēshu cap 4+log2(3072/52) = 9.885; Summarch 4+log2(3072/28) = 10.778. Only decoupling the cap from the source changes crispness (R-a: serve the full plate while keeping the w/h literals, 2.194 at DPR 3, conflicts with A-U11; R-c: a DPR-aware cap). None of this moves where the art sits. Caveat: the invariant holds only while NZ+log2(max(w,h)/(2r)) stays below Z_STREET 11.5.

### 2.7 Warping and the seam

- **[reg] A coast-band warp was measured and does not pay** (G0-11). `epeshu-coast-warp.json` tested a non-rigid warp of the chart to the print coast at 0.6×+3E. It shrinks chart→print residual 3.58/6.66 → 1.72/3.60 rms/max (R6), but land-over-sea drops only 91.1 → 78.5/70.5 px², and the frame displacement opens a third seam sign: 49-67 px² of the box left uncovered. Left open, the total misregistered area is 156.2/165.8 vs 166.4 unwarped (no net gain); padded with flat sea it falls 36-38%. This is one prototype on Epēshu only (along-shore sigma 2 px, one-sided vectors), so "rejected" means this warp class on this chart. **Recommended class unchanged: policy translate+scale override, a runtime clip against atlas water, and a fill.**
- **[reg] Warping bends canon fabric** (G0-14). At 0.6×+3E nothing folds (det min 0.11), but Wood Quay moves 3.5 atlas px and the old harbour quarters 2.3-3.3 px. At 1.0× (the DM's own scale), R6 folds 3.6% of chart land and still leaves 388-471 px² of land over sea. Use these as evidence against a warp. The wall-ring strain figures rest on a 21-vertex wall traced by eye while sibling files say no separable wall is drawn on the Epēshu chart: hold them.
- **[medium] Delivery cost of a pre-warped plate** (G0-13 **[D]**). Any new or changed raster under `tiles/`, `tiles-war/`, `tiles-imperial/`, `charts/` or `art/` needs a bump of the one global `TILES_V` (`var TILES_V=1`; `tv()` adds `?t=`), which versions 3,364 files (~36.7 MB) so every returning device refetches. A new plate also collides with **A-U11**'s acceptance in `docs/mobile/units.json` ("after load the full image is the same file ... registerCityOverlay literal kept"). The earlier dossier dropped this conflict as unsourced; it is sourced. The failing check verified the TILES_V figures (its objection is truncated); the holding check restated the A-U11 conflict as a **sequencing rule** while Mobile 6 (which runs A-U11) is unchecked, not a permanent ban. A warped plate would also need its displacement field in `worldPOICoord` (7-10 land POIs >1 px off). A CSS clip/fill costs 0 raster bytes.
- **[reg] Clip shape: a stored print-land ring** (G8-1 **[D]**). A polygon of the print coast (islets <400 px² filled, one ring), stored in atlas px and converted to a percent `clip-path: polygon()` on the overlay `<img>`. Vertices / CSS bytes / JSON bytes / residual rms, max / XOR px², today's box: DP0.25 148 / 2007 / 2373 / 0.067, 0.247 / 3.25; DP0.5 39 / 524 / 629 / 0.167, 0.465 / 13.6; DP1 20 / 270 / 325 / 0.339, 0.976 / 29.4. DP 0.25-0.5 stays within the mask's own 0.5 px pixel at about 1 KB. Generate it offline with the same mask port; never hand-edit. The failing check reproduced every figure and noted the raw mask adds a small extra land ring (3.5 px²) at each placement (rest truncated); the holding check: clip mechanics only, registration is the prior decision, and the clip applies only to canon overlays.
- **[reg] Store the ring placement-independent** (G8-2 **[D]**: the failing check reproduced the figures but noted the first quoted source is a paraphrase of JSON fields). The print-land ring does not depend on placement; store it over a fixed window (for Epēshu the `sampleCityMask` patch, atlas x 1201-1271, y 1380-1450). Percent vertices relative to any registered box can fall outside 0-100%, harmlessly. An "agreement" polygon (chart class = print class) depends on placement and must be regenerated for every record (today 2 rings / 58 vertices; 0.6×+3E 3 / 35; 0.8×+3E 2 / 40): do not ship it.
- **[reg] Build the mask on a padded window** (G8-3 **[D]**: the failing check reproduced both the padded-window and box-only results by re-running the scripts, with its objection truncated). Around Epēshu the Leponnia tint does not read as water in a mask built over a padded window (0.0% border-connected print water on 9091 DEM-land pixels >5 px from sea), but a mask built from the box alone leaks (11 px² disagreement at 0.6×+3E; 64% of differing pixels within 2 px of the border). Take the mask from a padded window (the cached `MASKQ[key].m` of the pinned `sampleCityMask`, 70 atlas px for a capital) and crop afterwards. Do not edit `buildCityMask`/`sampleCityMask` (UG3 pins) and do not reuse this test for inland cities.
- **[reg] The clip reveals the wash, so the needed fill is sea, not land** (G8-4 **[D]**). `washMake` paints a radial land-tone gradient in `pCityWash` (z 449), directly under `pCity` (450), so clipping the chart reveals tan wash, not the base. At 0.6×+3E band 1 (chart land over print sea, 93.3 px²) clipped shows tan ΔE 14.1 from print sea [159,152,117]; band 2 (chart sea over print land, 73.9 px²) clipped shows tan ΔE 5.4 from print land [182,165,119]. Correction from the failing check: `washUrl` holds alpha .94 only out to gradient stop .5, i.e. **0.85 of the box half-extent**, then falls to .5; it is not .94 over every box pixel. So: clip the chart to print land, add a sea-toned fill over print sea in the box, and decide band 2 separately.
- **[medium] Where the fill goes** (G8-5). Use an `L.imageOverlay` in the existing `pCityWash` pane with `{pane:'pCityWash', zIndex:2, interactive:false}`: no `createPane` (UG7 stays green), no edit to `washMake`. Leaflet 1.9.4 ImageOverlay defaults to zIndex 1 and writes it onto the img, so zIndex 2 paints above the wash even after `washRefresh` re-appends it. An `L.polygon` would land in a per-pane `<svg>` with no z-index, which the re-appended wash would cover. Add, remove and fade it next to `CITYVIEW.wash` in `ensureCityOverlay` and `worldOpacityUpdate`.
- **[medium] Not a runtime raster mask** (G8-7 **[D]**). A canvas mask from the z5 tiles is 0.5 atlas px per pixel; at Epēshu's 9.885 each mask pixel spans ~30 screen px, so the edge goes blocky or soft. A % polygon is crisp at any zoom, is fixed data rather than dependent on the browser's JPEG decoder, and duplicates no pinned logic. The holding check kept the verdict on crispness alone and corrected two support details; the failing check's objection (on iOS mask-image support) is truncated.
- **[medium] Not a PNG mask asset** (G8-8 **[D]**: the failing check said the `--check` failure comes from the stale committed manifest, and the rest of its objection is truncated). A PNG alpha mask under `art/` works (palette 1-bit 5,685 B at art size, 937 B at 671×720) but is a new raster: `tools/build-offline-manifest.js --check` fails until `TILES_V` moves, and it touches A-U11's territory. The ~1 KB polygon JSON needs neither. If a soft edge is wanted, use an inline SVG data-URI `mask-image` built from the same polygon.
- **[medium] Browser support** (G8-9 **[D]**: the failing check noted that BCD's html_elements floors (Chrome 23, Safari 7) are the prefixed floors; the holding check said unprefixed `clip-path` is enough for iOS 15+). Per MDN BCD: `clip-path` unprefixed from Chrome 55 / Safari 9.1, `polygon()` from Chrome 37 / Safari 10.1, `path()` Chrome 88 / Safari 13.1; `shape()` (Safari 18.4) is too new for iOS 15. `polygon()`'s fill-rule argument has no BCD entry, so evenodd is unverified; the islet-filled single nonzero ring avoids it. Set it through the ImageOverlay className or a CSS rule, never through `style.transform`/width/height, which Leaflet rewrites. A browser probe is still needed (P14 under "Browser probes still needed").
- **[reg] Nobody else produces this product** (G8-10 **[D]**: the failing check restated the facts, and its objection is truncated). The Living sheet-wide sea mask is unbuilt, 1 mask px per 8 atlas px (the Epēshu box is 3.6×3.9 mask px at 0.6×: useless as a clip), loaded only under `living=1`, and blocked by Filigree 4 and Mobile 9/14. Filigree B-38 clips relief to the chart coast (the wrong direction), and slice C reads the pinned `sampleCityMask`. The anchoring/registration track owns the clip data, generated by its own deterministic offline tool.
- **[reg] Clip only with a registration record** (G8-11 **[D]**). Among canon charts the bite is Epēshu-only: Summarch's hard-coded anchor has harbor false, sea null (the failing check: the fields exist, set to false/null). Lepon the Old (ruin, sea E 2 px) is a generated plan the generator already handles. Today's unregistered Epēshu box has 665.8 px² of chart land over sea, so the clip alone would hide most of the south-western quarter: **ship clip, fill and the registration record as one unit, never the clip alone.**
- **[medium] Corner-placement plugins** (reg R9). Leaflet.DistortableImage (Public Lab, MapKnitter) places an image by four corners as a perspective transform (`matrix3d` from `general2DProjection`); its `_animateZoom` writes only the end-state matrix and lets Leaflet's 0.25 s CSS transition interpolate, so the image can wobble mid-animation (inferred). It brings a build, glfx, webgl-distort, leaflet-toolbar and exif-js. Not for the runtime; at most a one-off authoring aid outside the repo.
- **[medium] Allmaps** (reg R11 **[D]**). IIIF Georeference Annotations store GCPs as a GeoJSON FeatureCollection of WGS84 Points with `resourceCoords`, an optional transformation (polynomial order 1-3 or thinPlateSpline), and the mask as an SvgSelector on the annotation's target. Rendering is WebGL2 over a triangulated mask. The Leaflet plugin is ESM-only with bare imports, assumes WGS84 and Web Mercator, and needs IIIF tiles. Not a runtime dependency. Worth borrowing: the data shape (GCPs art px → atlas px, transformation type, mask polygon, committed per canon city) and its algorithms. The holding check: the "conflicts with fly-in animations" part is overstated (`flyTo` runs its own per-frame loop, not `zoomAnimation`).

### 2.8 The shipped vectors prove the point: medium does not register

- **[reg] The Sun Road vector is off the printed road at the source** (gap-print vop-01 **[D]**). `named-ways.json` 'Road that Walks with the Sun' (28 vertices, 'Trace approximate', drawn by `L.polyline` in `buildNamedWays`) sits a median 31.3 atlas px from the nearest printed road dot in a ±100 px normal band (p90 64.6, max 98.2); for 45% of samples no dot is in the band; the uncensored nearest-dot distance is median 39.9, max 215.7. East of Nhagi it cuts across Rubea 90-180 px inland of the printed coast road. This line is already a vector and drifts exactly as a raster would. The holding check: this is a static data error, **not the zoom "shift" the owner described**. The failing check verified the numbers; its objection is truncated.
- **[reg] Smoothing is innocent** (gap-print vop-02 **[D]**, corrected). `crSpline` (centripetal Catmull-Rom; seg 16 in `buildNamedWays`/`buildSeaLanes`, 18 per leg in `buildRoute`) moves land roads at most ~6 px from their raw vertices (Road that Walks median 0.9, max 5.8; East-West Road max 3.2). On wide sea bends it is larger: **leg 8 (Sepos to White Sea) median 6.1, max 19.6 px; the Gulf Crossing max 13.4**, where the print draws no road ink. Raw and spline offsets to the print agree within about 1 px at the median. Better vector rendering cannot fix a source-to-print error of 30-215 px; only re-registering the vertices can.
- **[reg] Wrong gate inputs come from data classes** (gap-print vop-04). `buildApprPolys` pushes every party-route leg as cls 'land' from raw pts. Leg 8 (74% sea) gives Pomos land approaches at 266.6° and 107.6° with no printed road dots in its annulus, so Pomos gets spurious gates. Leg 10 (99% sea) crosses Epēshu's circle at 314.1°, harmless only because Epēshu is canon art. Fix by classifying legs by the print sea mask or a per-leg medium field in `party-route.json`; a vector Pomos fed the same `APPR_POLYS` grows the same false gates.
- **[reg] Sea lanes cross land in two places** (gap-print vop-06 **[D]**, corrected). The North Coast Packet crosses land for 26 px raw / 27 px spline at x1311-1336, y≈1398 (the Paerāndas islets). The South Lane overrun into Kardunash is **12 px raw / 5 px spline beyond the port allowance**, not ~35 px. Gulf Crossing and Strait Run stay on water. Lane endpoints sit 13.7-60 px from their anchors. These lanes feed `seaAppr` quay bias for Sokundo, Kanae, Tamaron, Mago, Drāmūz and Kardunash. Fix only in `sea-lanes.json` (prov 'invented', so bending to the print is allowed).
- **[reg] Realm borders are the best-registered layer** (gap-print vop-07 **[D]**: the failing check verified the numbers, with its objection truncated; the holding check said one clause is unsupported by the data, also truncated). The print draws realms only as colour washes. Polygon rings sit a median 3.5-5.4 px from the printed wash edge for Cyrikon, Sārmathi, Alensic, Wall, Eastern Patrin and Pheropic (IoU 0.57-0.85). The same vector medium, traced against an unambiguous edge, registers within a few px: **the medium does not cause drift; registration does.** Umbadic and Regimen are low-confidence (green tint spreads past the polygon; Regimen is hatching).
- **[reg] The colour test reads tint as water inland** (gap-print vop-09). `buildCityMask`'s r−b ≤54 test fires on realm tints and Emphēros grey-green land: 70 of 115 inland anchors with no sea entry read >50% border-connected water. Impact today is small (only the 16 `drawWater` anchors use the mask coast, none >0.8; for the rest the mask sets only the land hue, which is itself biased). It is a latent trap for any step that trusts `mask.water` away from the coast. The functions are UG3-pinned, so this is flagged, not fixed.
- **[reg] Rivers are a third registration source the earlier dossier missed** (G0-7). The earlier dossier's line "registration error is concentrated in (1) the anchors and (2) the two DM charts" must be corrected. 18 of 135 generated plans have a printed river within 2.5·FOOT_R (13 cross the 1.0·FOOT_R box, 2 graze it, 3 approach-only); 17 of those draw no river at all; Epēshu's chart is a 19th case (the Lamor crosses its approach circle at 178.1°). All 19 come within 1.7·FOOT_R, under the wash. The shift audit's 57 sources had no river row. A vector plan with the same RV block misses the print the same way.
- **[reg] River fixes and the hashes** (G0-9). Feeding atlas river geometry into `genCityCanvas`'s RV block changes `atlas_fn_sha.genCityCanvas` (the function source is pinned). If it engages only for river cities, `cityCanvasSha` stays at B0: none of Tamaron, Sokundo, Kanae has `traits.river` or printed river ink (the comment "Every rng draw is RV-guarded → riverless cities stay byte-identical" sits above the RV block). A mask-path fix would move `cityCanvasSha` (all three are harbours). Queue it as its own atlas item after Mobile 9 and Mobile 14, with an `atlas_fn_sha` rebase.
- **[reg] No planned item supplies plan-usable rivers** (G0-10 **[D]**: the failing check narrowed it to "no planned item wires traced rivers into the city plans"). Filigree B-13's `rivers.json` covers only the DEM window (5 of the 18 river cities plus Epēshu) and gates on overlap with the print, not recall. 'Living later · trace the rivers' is sheet-wide, but its only consumer is barges and its done-when requires the atlas sha unchanged. A plan-river consumer is new, unowned work; re-run the crossing table after anchors move (Aldrakhar, Iet Ford and Bash Dargis gain box ink; Stony Ford appears).

### 2.9 How the reference tools stay anchored

- **[reg] Common principle** (gap-refs RA-principle **[D]**). Every reference that stays anchored does so because its detail is authored in, or registered upstream into, one frame: swisstopo's LV95 tile grid, the 3D Tiles transform chain, OSM-derived vector tiles whose decoder knows the frame. None stays anchored because it is vector. `L.svgOverlay` extends `L.ImageOverlay` and is positioned by the same bounds box. The atlas already has the frame (CRS.Simple atlas px at NZ=4, shared by `makeBase('tiles/',5)`, `makeBase('tiles-war/',5)`, `makeBase('tiles-imperial/',5)`); city art is not registered into it. The failing check narrowed the claim to four points the sources show.
- **[reg] swisstopo Journey Through Time** (RA-swisstopo-time **[D]**: the failing check confirmed the quotes, adding `constrainOnlyCenter: true` and the per-layer resolution slicing, with its objection truncated). Each WMTS layer builds its grid from the projection's resolution steps, tile origin and bounds on an LV95 view; the grid is cut short per layer at its maxResolution. Changing year swaps the WMTS Time dimension; the client never re-fits a sheet. Upstream control-point georeferencing of the old sheets is an inference (pages egress-blocked). Transfer: register each DM chart once, offline, into atlas px, store it, and only place or fade it at runtime. Era bases already follow this; city art does not yet.
- **[medium] swisstopo vector base follows the frame** (RA-swisstopo-vector-base). The vector light base map is drawn by MapLibre inside OpenLayers and mounted only in Web Mercator ("won't work on LV95 yet"). The frame comes first and the medium follows. For the atlas, a vector city must be authored in atlas px and drawn with Leaflet's own `L.polyline`/`L.polygon`, which already work in CRS.Simple; MapLibre would break the single-file rule.
- **[both] swisstopo identify is a coordinate lookup** (RA-swisstopo-buildings-overlays **[D]**: the failing check said identify is a backend request at a clicked coordinate, 10 features per click and 50 for a rectangle selection, with the rest truncated). `DEFAULT_FEATURE_IDENTIFICATION_TOLERANCE = 10` (px) and 10 features per click: a backend query at a coordinate, not a geometry hit-test. Transfer: inverse-project the tap to atlas px and query a footprint list placed by the same registration transform as the art, inside the atlas's one tap resolver (filigree FM4), with tolerance in CSS px. Neither fixes alignment by itself.
- **[reg] swisstopo 2D→3D hands off view state** (RA-swisstopo-3d). `CesiumCamera.vue` starts the camera at the 2D centre with height from resolution × canvas width, top-down; leaving 3D sets the 2D centre to the camera target. Transfer to the living track's H2/H2z: carry an atlas-px centre and scale through the existing Epēshu atlas↔sim transform (atlas = (1060,1240) + grid·800/768), valid only inside that window.
- **[reg] Tokyo streamed city** (RA-tokyo). The best candidate (`jeantimex/tokyo`, not confirmed as the post) uses one metric frame centred on the area with 256 m tiles; in 3D Tiles each tile's transform maps into its parent's frame. **The chain preserves registration authored in; it does not create it.** A city detail level should be a child of the atlas frame: atlas px plus one stored transform per city.
- **Owner's-brief references, unverified** (RA-collison-bayatlas **[D]**, RA-azlen **[D]**, RA-lombard-httyd **[D]**: the failing checks said these rest on the owner's brief only. For Bay Atlas, the Mapbox Vector Tile spec says a tile should not carry its own bounds or projection. For Azlen, the brief says nothing about anchoring. For Lombard, four searches found nothing). Collison's Bay Atlas stack (MapLibre + PMTiles + Mapzen terrain + OSM) is unverified (no repo found); if right, it stays anchored because all data share one vector-tile frame. Azlen's painted San Francisco flyover is one authored picture with nothing to register: an aesthetic reference for slice C, not a method. Neither the Lombard Street exhibits nor the How to Train Your Dragon maps have a verifiable source; the nearest verified exhibit pattern is a Mapbox storytelling chapter (camera pose plus layer toggles). None is a precedent for anchoring.

### 2.10 What the rendering medium costs (for contrast)

Every figure here is about the **medium**; none moves a city relative to the atlas.

- **[medium] Generated-plan vector budget, one city** (gap-vecbudget C1). Counts from running the verbatim generator against a recording stub with a synthetic half-sea mask (±5-15%). Capital Epēshu (counterfactual; canon art ships): 2,065 features / 8,462 vertices, GeoJSON 59,862 B gzip, delta-int at 0.25 px 21,535 B gzip. Town Tamaron: 1,360 / 5,560, 37,001 B gzip. Village Kanae: 715 / 2,581, 16,701 B gzip. Buildings are about 77-92% of features.
- **[medium] Map-wide** (gap-vecbudget C2 **[D]**). 136 generated cities (17 towns, 116 villages incl. Penthelon, 3 ruins): 98,084 features, 376,381 vertices, 2.46 MB GeoJSON gzip or 0.99 MB delta-int gzip. Plans are a pure function of the city name (`xmur3(name)` → `mulberry32`), so nothing has to ship. The failing check: only one overlay is live at a time, so the cost that matters is per-city build and draw time on the mobile frame budget.
- **[medium] One city live; merge by class** (gap-vecbudget C3 **[D]**). `worldCityUpdate` picks the nearest city and `ensureCityOverlay` removes the previous one: 500-2,100 features, 1.9k-8.5k vertices live. One SVG path per feature means 500-2,100 DOM paths; merging by class and style gives 11-30 paths. The failing check struck the claim that R4/R7 "allow" the merge: they bear on labels and rest-state, not hit targets; P11 decides SVG vs per-pane `L.canvas({pane:'pCity'})` with frame times.
- **[medium] Scale matters as much as count** (gap-vecbudget C5). Canvas px per atlas px: capital 39.4, town 73.1, village 113.8, ruin 128 (GEN_SIZE 2048 / 2·footR). A town building (rr(5,11) canvas px) is ~1-2 screen px at z7.8 and ~12-27 at Z_STREET 11.5. Leaflet's default `smoothFactor:1` and hairline strokes would collapse or alias geometry that the raster simply downsamples: a vector city needs LOD or a raster hand-off below about z8.
- **[medium] Texture is not geometry** (gap-vecbudget C6 **[D]**: the failing check said stipple dots on water are skipped, so drawn counts vary by seed). Ruin rubble (`rs<900`, drawn count varies by seed), scrub (`sc<520`), snow (`sn<420`), furrow hatching, the `destination-out` vignette and the 8× drawImage coast/river rims belong in raster or pattern fills. The wall is drawn as separate 0.02-rad two-point strokes (`wa+=0.02`, Tamaron 288, Epēshu 558) that merge into 4 and 10 runs.
- **[medium] Determinism** (gap-vecbudget C8 **[D]**). Running each named city twice gave identical op logs and identical R() draw counts (Tamaron half-sea 17,289; Epēshu 25,565); `Math.random`/`Date.now` replaced by throwing functions never fired. The failing check: `genCityCanvas(name,t,a,mask)` returns no plan, it paints imperatively, so a separate vector consumer is not free; extracting geometry means either editing the pinned function or duplicating its logic.
