- **[medium] Canon chart traces** (G0-19; G0-20). Measured: a skeleton trace fits the live budget (Epēshu 4,624 vertices / 24,015 B GeoJSON gzip; Summarch 931 / 5,326 B at DP 1). A full-building trace does not: Summarch 39,461 vertices (4.6×), 200,373 B gzip; Epēshu 177,225 vertices (20.9×), 903,456 B gzip, building IoU 0.883. These supersede the earlier refuted estimate. **Correction to the earlier dossier:** the "skeleton (coast, river, walls, gates, main streets)" is not extractable as stated. The real skeleton is coast/water plus Summarch's wall ring plus Epēshu's hinterland edge; lanes are unpainted ground and Epēshu's wall is drawn at outline width. Streets could come from the ground class's negative space (unmeasured). Epēshu building blobs: 23,184 at ≥12 px and 17,931 at ≥25 px, not ~17,323 at ≥12. All traced from JPEG tiles, not DM masters. A traced chart placed by `cityBox` misaligns exactly like the raster.
- **[both] Ownership of a trace** (G0-21 **[D]**: the failing check supported only narrower claims, e.g. pins stay untouched "unless a later change consumes it inside those functions"). A skeleton trace is a static offline asset: it touches no pinned function and leaves `cityCanvasSha` alone, but adds a download the Mobile delivery budget must admit. Its value is as registration targets and hit geometry, so it belongs with the anchoring/registration item; a full-building trace exceeds slice C and should be its own item. Dropping POI badges leaves ~540 building-equivalent voids in Epēshu. This is G4's inference, offered as a proposal for owner question OQ14 (prior Q12).
- **[medium] Chart-conditioned Epēshu budget** (G7-3). The prototype (`prototypes/epeshu-chart.html`, seed 'epeshu') emits 7,758 features / 29,360 vertices (GeoJSON 180,610 B gzip; delta-int q0.25 64,655 B gzip; 9 merged paths); non-degenerate 6,279 / 23,444. About 3.0× features and 2.8× vertices of the C1 counterfactual, and 2.8× the 8.5k-vertex live ceiling unless LOD is used. Its input trace is 5,478 B (1,873 B gzip).
- **[reg] The prototype puts houses in the sea** (G7-7). `build` blocks only the trace extent, its own coast strip (`COAST_STRIP=60`), plazas and masks; it never consults atlas water. Placed via `cityBox`: 1,780 of 5,424 building centroids on print sea today, 595 at 0.6×+3E, 1,068 at 0.8×+3E; 10 / 3 / 5 of 19 POIs on print sea. A registered port must transform the trace through the per-city override, then stamp the atlas water mask (from `sampleCityMask`'s output, not an edit to it) as blocked.
- **[medium] Until registered, the prototype is a medium asset** (G7-9 **[D]**). Through `cityBox`, every vertex lands where the raster pixel lands: 7.7 rms coast error, 1,780 buildings on sea, Forum (POI 7) at (1226.6,1415.6), 9.5 px W of the anchor. A building's median footprint 4.7×10.2 art px is ~4.7×10.2 screen px at 9.885 and ~1.0×2.3 at z7.8. Sequencing: registration override first, then vector fabric. The holding check fixed two arithmetic details; the failing check confirmed the core and its objection is truncated.
- **[medium] Portability** (G7-10 **[D]**: the failing check supported the LC7 and R4/R7 facts and flagged that `drawTrace` draws POI discs at rest; the holding check said "partly right"). The 2D trace, the raster-grid fabric (`growRibs`, `extractBlocks`, `makeParcels`, `footprint`) and the 2D draw port to the atlas as merged Leaflet paths, with four changes: a sibling stream such as `xmur3(name+'|vec')`; the repair bug fixed; atlas water blocked; and build cost respected (about 1.2 s per build in node v22, 1,177-1,375 ms over 5 runs: ship static output or build once off the gesture path). The 3D mode stays in the sim (LC7: no WebGL in maps-site). `drawTrace` draws POI discs at rest, which R7 forbids. A phone is unmeasured.
- **[both] Ownership is unmapped** (G7-11 **[D]**). No docs track claims the atlas vector city: filigree lists the prototypes only as 'not shipped' inventory and slice C's scope is four named functions; the street track is sim-only (ST1, ST2, ST12); living LC7 owns the crossfade into a Street poster. The failing check made three corrections to how ownership is stated (e.g. the prototypes also appear in `docs/filigree/research-dossier.md`); the holding check said to split the work. Registration comes first as a new atlas POLISH item beside slice C, independent of medium; the vector city follows.
- **[both] Sim adoption is a whole-world re-baseline** (G7-13 **[D]**: the failing check verified the quotes; its objection is truncated). `genTownPlans` draws every settlement's streets from the shared `W.rng.gen`; `GEN_STEPS` then runs `genBuildings`, `genHouses`, `genFlora` on it, and `genBuildings` draws per street segment. Any change in Epēshu's segment count shifts buildings, great houses, flora and the chronicle across the world for `#s=epeshu`. Keep the sim out of the first cut; treat sim adoption as a separate owner-gated item with a declared re-baseline.
- **[medium] Vectors transform vertex by vertex through the same registration** (reg R14 **[D]**: the failing check said two sub-claims lack cited evidence and one is slightly overstated; the holding check made the traced-roads correction below). Allmaps refines lines with midpoints under non-linear transforms; a vector city aligns only as well as its registration. Vectors add crispness past `cityMaxZ`, interactivity and road vertices that can be shared. `L.svgOverlay` inherits ImageOverlay's axis-aligned bounds; an inner `<g transform>` can carry an affine. **Correction:** `data/traced-roads.json` is not loaded by `maps-site/index.html`, so "snap road vertices to traced-roads at the gates" does not hold as written.

### 2.11 Collateral: who reads the placement

- **[reg] One record, five readers** (gap-consumers C9; reg R13 **[D]**). `cityBox` is read by `ensureCityOverlay` (imageOverlay bounds), `buildWorldPOIs` (hover halos), `worldPOICoord` (deep-link fly targets, used by `flyIntoCity`) and `washMake` (wash bounds). **`cityMaxZ` does not call `cityBox`**: it repeats the footR/aspect maths itself. `buildCityStubs` draws from `reg.stubs` already in world px. A registration override applied only to the overlay bounds would separate the art from the hover halos and undo the 'Epēshu roundel doubling' fix (POLISH). The override must live in one function that `cityBox`, `cityMaxZ`, `buildWorldPOIs`, `worldPOICoord` and `washMake` all read; `washMake` is hash-pinned, so geometry reaches it through `reg`/`a` fields, not by editing its body. Mobile A-U11 also requires the `registerCityOverlay` literal kept. R13's holding check added scope (its failing check verified the code facts; objection truncated): fit only the canon overlays; generated plans are registered by construction.
- **[reg] The hashed trio and anchor moves** (gap-consumers C5 **[D]**: the failing check confirmed the mechanism, including that canon cities are never hashed, with its objection truncated). Moving Tamaron's anchor shifts the `sampleCityMask` window, so the plan and its data URL change and the frozen UG3 `cityCanvasSha` fails on every profile; fixing it means re-cutting B0/R1. Canon overlays are never hashed, so a canon override does not trip it. A clip inside `genCityCanvas`, `washMake` or `sampleCityMask` breaks `atlas_fn_sha` and `cityCanvasSha`; on a new pane it breaks UG7. **Put the seam clip on the existing overlay element** (CSS), not in a pinned function or a new pane.
- **[reg] Living gates not yet frozen** (gap-consumers C7). Living LV5 and LV6 resolve from `city-anchors.json` Epēshu; the sea-mask done-when checks every anchor. A canon override and the clip change what LV5/LV6 capture; anchor moves change the sea-mask pass set. `docs/living/gates` does not exist yet, so registration landing before Living 1 costs no re-freeze.
- **[reg] A-U11 does not move bounds** (gap-mobile C3). A-U11 is the only mobile unit touching overlay art: a 720 px preview, then the same full plate, with the literal w/h kept, so bounds stay `cityBox(a,reg.w,reg.h)`. Its swap mechanism is unspecified; if it is a second `registerCityOverlay` call it runs `overlayCache.delete` and `ensureCityOverlay`, tearing down wash, stubs and POIs, and omitting opts would reset prov to 'invented'. The canon clip (keyed on prov 'canon'), any swap crossfade and the per-city override must attach where A-U11's swap passes.
- **[medium] Tier figures are HEAD values, not B0** (gap-mobile C2). A-U0 (in HEAD) moved phone fit 1.95 → 1.9 (iphone13) and 2.35 → 2.4 (pixel7); iPhone tier C is 3.9667, spread without landscape 0.82 (with landscape 1.22). A-U2 failed, so HEAD still uses the fitZoom-relative `tierZoom`; do not skip these figures.
- **[medium] Stretch past native** (canon C23 **[D]**). `baseOpacity` 1 → 0.12 over z6.2-8; `overlayOpacity` 0 → 1 over z6.8-7.8; base upscaled past maxNativeZoom 5 (4 on retina, via `makeBase`). Epēshu's overlay is native at `cityMaxZ` ≈ 9.885 (1 atlas px ≈ 59 screen px); the failing check added Z_OPEN_MAX 8.0 in open country and the holding check the retina maxNativeZoom 4; the failing check's objection is truncated. Crispness past the cap needs a vector or the full pyramid; neither alone fixes where the art sits.

### 2.12 What the evidence supports, in order

1. **Anchors (data, [reg])**: an offline, deterministic derivation with a reviewed name-to-ring table: 45 moves, keeps for Drāmūz and Sjefenhold, owner calls for Penthelon, Paga, Ūmak-nūs-Ion and Kolens. The census second pass comes first. Rule the point convention before any B0 recapture. Re-check polylines, `sea.side`/`distPx`, gazetteer and approach sets for every anchor that actually moves.
2. **Summarch (data, [reg])**: translation-only to (541.75,49.75) across `meshedWorldInit`, `factions.json` and `party-route.json`; the river disagreement is an authoring call.
3. **Epēshu (data + a few lines, [reg])**: a per-city translate+scale override, chosen by stated policy within ~0.6-1.0× and +3..+8 px E, read by all five placement consumers. Ship it with the seam treatment (a stored print-land ring as a % `clip-path` on the overlay `<img>`, plus a sea-toned `L.imageOverlay` fill in `pCityWash` at zIndex 2) as one unit. No warp, no rotation, no TPS.
4. **Rivers and roads (data, [reg])**: re-trace the Sun Road, East-West Road and sea lanes onto the print; classify route legs by medium; a plan-river consumer is new, unowned work.
5. **Medium ([medium])**: a vector city only after 1-3, registered through the same record; LOD below about z8; merged paths; sim adoption separate.

Every registration fix measured on the modern sheet holds in all three eras.

---

### Refuted items (not findings)

| item | why the checks refuted it |
|---|---|
| canon C02 | Both checks reproduced the pyramid tile counts and byte sizes (6/20/72/272 per level, 5.90 and 3.16 MB) and the overlay downscale; the failure lies in the truncated remainder of the check text, so the claim as a whole is not cited. |
| canon C07 | The quotes are verbatim in the 51-page snapshot, but the "inconsistent units" is an inference, not canon's own statement, and the figures do not bear on anchoring. |
| canon C18 | Inventory right, but "none carries city geometry" is wrong: the unreferenced `art/chart-Summarch.jpg` (644×597) is a complete low-res city plan (a duplicate of the Summarch chart). |
| canon C21 | Gazetteer counts and the missing footprint field are right; the settlement wiki-page part failed (truncated in the check text). |
| canon C22 | The sim mapping (11.25 m per atlas px, Epēshu and Lepon grid positions) is exact; the printed-bar figures and the 120-130× compression were wrong, and the scale gap does not cause misalignment. |
| reg R8 | Leaflet.ImageOverlay.Rotated's nested-div mechanism transfers, but it is a rendering/placement device, not a registration fix, and parts of the claim did not hold as stated. |
| reg R12 | Map Warper georeferences in EPSG:4326 (not a no-SRS pixel space) and emits alpha; the offline pre-warp route overreached and applies only to canon overlays (and see G0-13). |
| gap-anchor C4 | The 19 colour-test-unreliable names and mechanism hold as a caveat on offline numbers; the claimed rendering consequences for the generated plans were rejected. |
| gap-anchor C8 | An offDot field cannot start with "exactly two entries": Penthelon has no key in `city-anchors.json`, and a 3 px anchor-to-marker lint would fail at once. |
| gap-coasts tc-7 | The chart-vs-DEM gap is real, but as H2z is specified it does not show as a several-hundred-px coast jump at z11.5 (the atlas is capped at `cityMaxZ`). |
| gap-coasts tc-9 | The three-coasts figures and the dossier's only roughly agree (8.48 vs 9.3; 3.58 vs 3.7-3.9); they must not be quoted as one measurement. |
| gap-print vop-03 | The ~17 px offset is a lower bound (nearest dot may be another road), and "city-anchors.json has no Summarch entry" misleads: `meshedWorldInit` hard-codes it. |
| gap-print vop-05 | The bearing errors reproduce, but the conclusion (snapping pointless, detector needed inside the pinned `genCityCanvas`) is wrong: gates come from data. |
| gap-print vop-08 | District rings have no printed border, but the remedy (clip to land plus rivers) is wrong; the project already treats these as approximate traces of dotted lines. |
| gap-consumers C1 | Half right: a bible edit does re-open the Filigree 1 gate, but `city_grain` holds no registration numbers and a POLISH-only item is not free on the filigree gate. |
| gap-consumers C2 | `q08-c08` measured a DEM-vs-chart discrepancy through `cityBox`, not chart-vs-atlas, and any placement change stales it, including an anchor move, so the scenario split was wrong. |
| gap-consumers C3 | Scenario attribution overreached: a seam clip changes none of these numbers, and an anchor move shifts the Q1 window. |
| gap-consumers C4 | The probe shows no window hex changes class under the anchor moves; no bible amendment is needed. |
| gap-consumers C6 | Anchor moves leave marker-derived mobile ids and tap fixtures alone, but are not free of mobile rebases. |
| gap-consumers C8 | The coordinates match, but nothing reads that match at runtime: the apps cross-link by place name. |
| gap-consumers C10 | The literal 523,58 also appears in `factions.json` and `party-route.json`, so moving Summarch is not a one-literal change. |
| gap-mobile C1 | A-U2 is not pending: it failed after 3 attempts, and its captured tiers come from the rejected build. |
| gap-mobile C4 | Quotes are real; the sequencing conclusion was rejected (truncated in the check text). |
| gap-mobile C5 | The conflict with A-U3/A-U4 is a sequencing and rebase cost, not a rule that the fixes must wait for G9. |
| gap-mobile C9 | A-U10 is owner-gated and not in HEAD; the bounds-clamp effect gets worse in the band, not only at the edges. |
| gap-mobile C10 | Only raster byte changes need a TILES_V bump; data JSON edits do not, so "every registration item" overreached. |
| G0-8 | The Ion Ephel defect is anchor-on-label (anchor 31 px from its ring), a data-only fix, not a mask-code bug. |
| G0-16 | The area-equivalent radius cannot prove the whole chart fits inside the minimum wall, and the sim-edit claims overreached. |

---

## 3. The vector city: the existing prototypes, what the medium buys and costs, the canon question

*Cross-references: the shift sources that depend on the medium are section 1 (§1.5); the registration every vector city inherits is section 2 (§2.3-§2.7, §2.12); how the references treat vectors is section 4 (§4.2); who would own a vector city is section 5 (§5.2, §5.7). Owner questions OQ17-OQ21 decide its scope.*

The owner's idea is a vectorised, drawn, interactive city layer to replace the inserted city images. This section tests it against the code at HEAD, the two city prototypes already in the repo, the reference tools the owner shared, and the gap-round measurements. All code anchors are in the HEAD export: `maps-site/index.html` for the atlas and `index.html` for the sim, unless a path says otherwise. Coordinates are atlas px (1 world px = 1 atlas image px at NZ=4, y down, no flip against the z5 tiles).

**The key distinction, which every finding below carries.** REGISTRATION decides where art and geometry sit relative to atlas features (coast, rivers, dotted roads, printed ring-dots), and it is the only thing that fixes misalignment. The RENDERING MEDIUM (raster or vector) brings crispness past the stretched image, interactivity, and road vertices that can be shared with the atlas. **A vector city placed through the same `cityBox` misaligns exactly as the raster does.** Each bullet is tagged **[reg]**, **[medium]** or **[both]**.

**Status markers.** Unmarked = survived (2/2 checks held). **[D]** = disputed (1/2): the text says what the failing check disputed, and where its objection is cut off in the check text, says so rather than guessing. Refuted items (0/2) appear only in the closing list. Where a check corrected an item, the corrected version is the one given. Gap-round items supersede the earlier dossier where they differ.

**Ids.** Ids collide across research lenses (several `C1`s), so each is prefixed by its lens: `sim` (S1-S13, inventory sim), `vlod` (VL1-VL18, methods vector-lod), `vec` (V1-V18, methods vectorize), `gap-anchor`, `gap-coasts`, `gap-print`, `gap-consumers`, `gap-mobile`, `gap-vecbudget`, `gap-refs`, `G0`, `G7`, `G8`, `G9`. Several gap items (anchors, coasts, seam clip, era sheets) were also given to section 2; here they are cited only for what they impose on a vector city.

**Screen magnification.** Errors are in atlas px. On screen they grow by 2^(z-4) CSS px: ×9.85 at z7.3, ×16 at z8, ×59.08 at Epēshu's live ceiling z9.885, ×181 at Z_STREET 11.5. On phones, multiply by DPR for device px.

---

### 3.1 The distinction in evidence: vectors already ship, and they misalign too

- **[reg] The two canon cities skip registration entirely** (vec V2 **[D]**). `meshedWorldInit` calls `registerCityOverlay('Epēshu','art/overlay-Epeshu.jpg',{prov:'canon',w:2863,h:3072,...})` and the same for Summarch (3072×2859). `cityBox(a,w,h)` sets `r=footR(a.kind)`, aspect-fits the image into a 2r box and centres it on `a.atlasX/atlasY`. What the image depicts plays no part: no rotation, no scale fit, no fit to coast, river or roads. Leaflet's `L.svgOverlay` extends `L.ImageOverlay` and is positioned by the same bounds box (Leaflet 1.9.4 `SVGOverlay.js`). The failing check verified all of this; its objection is truncated. The holding check (major) states the fix: content-aware control points in the DM art (coast, Stony River and harbour mouth, road gates) matched to the same PatrinorModern features, with the art bent to the atlas, applied to either medium.
- **[reg] Measured at Epēshu, the chart is the coast outlier** (gap-coasts tc-3, 2/2). Under today's `cityBox` (capital FOOT_R 26, box [1211.87,1389]-[1260.33,1441]) the water class of `overlay-Epeshu.jpg` sits off the print coast by rms 8.48, p95 16.89, max 21.05 atlas px, mean offset (4.34,4.39) toward SE; print-to-chart inside the box is rms 14.93, max 33.62. That is 84 CSS px at z7.3 and 501 at z9.885. The chart lays about 666 px² of land over atlas sea and never the reverse. **A vector city whose coast comes from the chart carries the same 8.5 px rms error.**
- **[reg] The chart-conditioned prototype lands exactly where the raster does** (G7-9 **[D]**). Until a per-city transform exists, `prototypes/epeshu-chart.html` is a rendering-medium asset in overlay-JPG px. Through `cityBox` (0.016927 atlas px per art px) every vertex lands where the raster pixel lands: trace-to-print coast rms 7.7 atlas px (G7-4), 1,780 of 5,424 emitted buildings on print sea (G7-7), the Forum (POI 7, art 871,1570) at atlas (1226.6,1415.6), 9.5 px W of the anchor dot. The failing check confirmed the core; the holding check said the size arithmetic needs two fixes (truncated) — at minimum, read the per-building screen sizes as CSS px, since G0-18 shows phones need a DPR factor on top.
- **[reg] A generated plan placed the generator's way misaligns like the raster** (gap-vecbudget C7 **[D]**). `genCityCanvas` paints a GEN_SIZE 2048 square mapped at `scl=S/(2*footR(kind))`, the same footprint scale as `cityBox` for a square image, centred on the anchor. The failing check corrected one detail: the drawn plan's centre shifts 0.07·S away from the sea **only when `drawWater` is true**. Otherwise placement is the anchor and footprint scale alone. Only the coast follows the atlas, and only when the tile-sampled mask finds water.
- **[reg] The shipped road vector is off the print by 30+ px** (gap-print vop-01 **[D]**). The Road that Walks with the Sun is already an `L.polyline` (`buildNamedWays`, pane pWay, `crSpline(way.pts,16)`; `named-ways.json` 28 vertices, note "Trace approximate"). In the ±100 px normal band it sits a median 31.3 atlas px from the printed dotted road (p90 64.6, max 98.2), with no road ink in the band for 45% of samples; the uncensored nearest-dot distance is median 39.9, max 215.7. 45% of the line lies on print sea. The holding check (major) corrects the framing: this is a static misregistration in the data, not the zoom "shift" the owner describes, but it proves the point — a vector misaligns by the same atlas-px error a stretched raster would.
- **[medium] Smoothing is innocent; the source vertices are not** (gap-print vop-02 **[D]**). `crSpline` moves land roads at most ~6 px from their raw vertices (Road that Walks median 0.9, max 5.8; East-West Road max 3.2). The failing check corrected the sea legs: leg 8 (Sepos to White Sea) reaches median 6.1, max 19.6 px, and the Gulf Crossing 13.4, on wide sea bends where the print draws no road. Raw-vertex and spline offsets to the print agree within ~1 px at the median. Rendering refinement moves a line by ≤6 px on land; registration error is up to ~98 px in-band.
- **[reg] The same medium registers to a few px when traced against a clear printed edge** (gap-print vop-07 **[D]**). Realm rings (`buildRealms`, raw rings via `px()`, no spline, pane pRealm) sit a median 3.5-5.4 atlas px from the printed colour-wash edge for Cyrikon, Sārmathi, Alensic, Wall, Eastern Patrin and Pheropic (p90 10.8-22.5, max 20.9-59.9; IoU 0.57-0.85); Umbadic and Regimen are low-confidence. Both checks confirmed the numbers; the holding check said one clause of the claim is unsupported (truncated). The lesson stands: alignment follows how geometry was registered, not the medium.
- **[reg] Sea lanes also stray onto land, by small amounts** (gap-print vop-06 **[D]**). The Gulf Crossing and Strait Run stay on print water. The North Coast Packet crosses 26 px (raw) / 27 px (spline) of land at x1311-1336, y~1398 (Paerāndas islets). The failing check corrected the South Lane: only 12 px raw / 5 px spline lie beyond the port allowance, not ~35 px. These are data fixes in `sea-lanes.json` (prov "invented", so bending to the print is allowed); no rendering change helps.
- **[reg] Gates come from data, not pixels** (gap-print vop-04, 2/2). `buildApprPolys` classes every party-route leg 'land' from raw points. Leg 8 (74% sea) gives the procedural village Pomos land approaches at 266.6° and 107.6° with no printed road nearby, so Pomos gets false gates. A vector Pomos fed the same `APPR_POLYS` grows the same false gates. The fix is in `buildApprPolys` or a per-leg medium field in `party-route.json`, never in the renderer.
- **[reg] Leaflet's own placement adds ≤~1 px** (vlod VL10, 2/2). `ImageOverlay._reset` rounds both corners through `latLngToLayerPoint` (`project(...)._round()`): position within 0.5 px, size within 1 px, at any zoom; the overzoomed base is rounded the same way. The visible shift comes from the bounds themselves: `cityBox` centres on the anchor, sizes by a per-kind constant and cannot rotate.
- **[reg] Vectors carry no frame of their own** (vlod VL7, 2/2). The vector-tile spec stores integer coordinates in a tile-local `extent` (4096 is the spec's example, not a requirement) and says a tile SHOULD NOT carry its bounds or projection; the decoder must already know them. A plan authored in its own frame (MFCG, `genCityCanvas`'s 2048 canvas, a traced chart) must be transformed into atlas px, via `px(x,y)=map.unproject([x,y],NZ)`, before any layer draws it. The MVT format itself does not transfer (no decoder, no tile server, no build).
- **[reg] Leaflet's guidance for game maps is "measure, then extrapolate"** (vlod VL13 **[D]**). The CRS.Simple guide (v1.9.4 `crs-simple.md`) says a map has one CRS, think in map units, and shows "approximate" bounds failing before correcting them by measuring known coordinates and extrapolating ("not from approximate bounds" is the claim's gloss, per the failing check). `leaflet-imageoverlay-rotated` places an image by three corners (affine). `cityBox` is the approximate-bounds case, and the same box also places the wash, every POI (`worldPOICoord`, `buildWorldPOIs`) and the hover halos.
- **[reg] The fantasy-map precedents never register their cities** (vlod VL14, 2/2; vlod VL15 **[D]**). Azgaar FMG's `createWatabouCityLinks` (`burgs-generator.ts`) builds an MFCG URL from scalars only (name, population, size, seed, river, coast, farms, citadel, walls, plaza, temple, ...) plus `sea` as one direction value; no coordinates, scale or road geometry. `city-anchors.json` {kind, harbor, sea:{side,distPx}} feeding `genCityCanvas` through `cityTraits` has the same parameter-only shape. Watabou's TownGeneratorOS (`Model.hx` `buildPatches`) seeds Voronoi points on a spiral around (0,0); the failing check corrected that the central patch becomes the plaza only when `plazaNeeded` (a random bool) is true. MFCG's GeoJSON "world units around zero" is unverified. Any local-frame plan needs an explicit local-to-atlas transform; a vector export anchors nothing.
- **[reg] The DM charts are separate canvases with no registration metadata** (vlod VL16 **[D]**). The Inkarnate fact is unverified (one search summary, from an unrelated page; context only). What matters is in our code: `overlay-Epeshu.jpg`, `overlay-Summarch.jpg` and the `charts/*` pyramids are drawn in their own pixel frames, and registering them is our own tool step.

#### What the reference tools actually do (gap-refs)

| reference | what keeps it anchored | what transfers | status |
|---|---|---|---|
| common principle | detail authored in, or registered upstream into, one frame (swisstopo LV95 tile grid, 3D Tiles transform chain, vector tiles whose decoder knows the frame). None stays anchored *because* it is vector; `L.svgOverlay` is positioned like `L.ImageOverlay` | the atlas frame already exists (CRS.Simple atlas px, NZ=4; era pyramids share it via `makeBase`), but `cityBox` does not register city art into it | RA-principle **[D]**: failing check confirmed four narrow points (WMTS grid from the projection, 3D Tiles chain, svgOverlay placement, repo facts); objection truncated |
| swisstopo Journey Through Time (`ch.swisstopo.zeitreihen`) | each WMTS layer's grid comes from the LV95 View's resolutions, origin and extent, **cut per layer at its maxResolution** (so not literally "the same grid at every zoom"); a year change swaps only the WMTS Time dimension | register each DM chart once, offline, store the result, only place or fade at runtime. Era bases already follow it (`setEra` swaps `makeBase` layers on one grid); city art does not | RA-swisstopo-time **[D]**; vlod VL1 **[D]** (holding check: a shared grid only guarantees a shared frame); vlod VL3 **[D]**: the georeferencing of ~8,000 sheets, the WMTS id and the LV03→LV95 fix are **unverified** (search summary only) |
| swisstopo vector base (`ch.swisstopo.leichte-basiskarte_world.vt`) | MapLibre inside OpenLayers (`@geoblocks/ol-maplibre-layer`), mounted by `OpenLayersInternalLayer.vue` only when the projection is Web Mercator, because MapLibre cannot do LV95 | the medium follows the frame and never replaces it. Our frame is CRS.Simple; Leaflet's own `L.polyline`/`L.polygon` already draw in it. MapLibre would break the single-file, no-framework rule and cannot share CRS.Simple | RA-swisstopo-vector-base, 2/2; vlod VL2 **[D]** ("Mapbox vector tiles" wording dropped: the source does not say it) |
| swisstopo building register and overlays | overlays in a shared URL loaded but hidden (`,f`); identify is a backend request at the clicked coordinate (`DEFAULT_FEATURE_IDENTIFICATION_TOLERANCE` 10 px, `DEFAULT_FEATURE_COUNT_SINGLE_POINT` 10), not a client-side geometry hit-test; the WMS/WMTS form of the building layer is unverified | interactivity as a coordinate lookup against a footprint list placed by the **same registration transform as the art**, with tolerance in CSS px converted per zoom; fold it into filigree FM4's one tap resolver (one feature within 22 CSS px opens it). The building layer lines up because it is registered data in LV95, not because it is raster | RA-swisstopo-buildings-overlays **[D]**; vlod VL4 **[D]** (only the config constants verified) |
| swisstopo 2D→3D | `CesiumCamera.vue` `initCamera()` flies to the 2D centre, height `resolution*width/(2*tan(PI/6))`, pitch top-down; leaving 3D writes the camera target back as the 2D centre — view state, not a picture | for H2/H2z, carry an atlas-px centre and scale through the Epēshu atlas-sim transform (see 3.6). Separate from the `cityBox` overlay | RA-swisstopo-3d, 2/2 |
| Collison's Bay Atlas (owner's brief) | if the brief's stack (MapLibre + PMTiles + Mapzen terrain + OSM) is right, OSM data in one vector-tile grid; the stack is **unverified** (no repo found) | the one-frame principle, not the stack. "Density is a property of the medium" must not be read as "vectorising the city fixes the shift" | RA-collison-bayatlas **[D]** |
| registration tools | Leaflet guide (measure, extrapolate); `@allmaps/transform` 1.0.0-beta.53 (straight = translation+scale 2 GCPs, helmert 2, polynomial1 3, thinPlateSpline 3 "exact" at GCPs, same transform for rasters and geometries); `ImageOverlay` ≤~1 px rounding; Azgaar/Watabou and TownGeneratorOS never registered | port the method, not the libraries: an offline, deterministic fit from chart px to atlas px, stored as data per city with its residual, swapped in for `cityBox` where prov is 'canon'. Try translation+scale first; TPS bends canon art and needs an owner ruling | RA-tools **[D]** (the "not from approximate bounds" wording is a gloss) |

---

### 3.2 The three city generators that already exist

There are three places where city geometry is already made. None of them is registered beyond the coast.

#### (a) The atlas plan generator: `genCityCanvas`

- **[medium] Measured single-city vector budget** (gap-vecbudget C1, 2/2). From a recording-stub run of the verbatim generator on a synthetic half-sea mask (nearest-neighbour; expect 5-15% shift against the real tile-sampled coast): counterfactual capital Epēshu 1,864 buildings, 28 lanes, 9 primary roads, 558 wall strokes in 10 runs; 2,065 vector-worthy features, 8,462 vertices; GeoJSON 405,527 B (59,862 B gzip), delta-int at 0.25 px 25,514 B (21,535 B gzip). Town Tamaron 1,223 buildings, 1,360 features, 5,560 vertices, 266,903 B (37,001 B gzip), delta-int 16,783 B (14,062 B gzip). Village Kanae 549 buildings, 715 features, 2,581 vertices, 132,419 B (16,701 B gzip), delta-int 8,178 B (6,898 B gzip). Buildings are **about 77-92%** of features (corrected from 85-90%). Epēshu is a counterfactual: canon art ships, and `ensureCityGen` generates only when `!reg||reg.prov==='invented'`. These numbers are **medium cost only**; they say nothing about the canon charts (see 3.5) or about registration.
- **[medium] Map-wide** (gap-vecbudget C2 **[D]**). 136 cities would be generated (17 towns, 116 villages incl. Penthelon, 3 ruins; no capital): 85,297 buildings, 98,084 features, 376,381 vertices, 18.4 MB GeoJSON (2.46 MB gzip), 0.99 MB gzip as delta-int. Village median 690 features (max 861), town median 997 (max 1,493); the largest by features is the ruin Sjefenhold (1,570 features, 6,249 vertices). The failing check: this is not a shipping cost (only one overlay is live; the inputs already ship), and a plan is **not** a pure function of the name — it also reads traits, the anchor, the approach polylines and the tile-sampled mask — so the cost that matters is per-city build and draw time, not a precomputed total.
- **[medium] Scale matters as much as count** (gap-vecbudget C5, 2/2). Canvas px per atlas px is `GEN_SIZE/(2*footR)`: capital 39.4, town 73.1, village 113.8, ruin 128. Buildings are `rr(5,11)` canvas px, so a town building is ~0.95-2.1 CSS px at z7.8 and ~0.5-1 at z6.8 (the fade band), where Leaflet's default `smoothFactor:1` and hairline strokes would collapse or alias geometry that a raster simply downsamples. At Z_STREET 11.5 a vector town building would be 12-27 px, but no city reaches 11.5 today (each kind is capped at its `cityMaxZ`). A vector city needs level of detail or a raster hand-off below ~z8.
- **[medium] Some of the plan is texture, not geometry** (gap-vecbudget C6 **[D]**). The ruin rubble stipple (`rs<900`), arid scrub (`sc<520`), frost snow (`sn<420`), field furrow hatching, the `destination-out` edge vignette (needs a mask, not geometry) and the coast/river ink rims (8× `drawImage` stamps of mask canvases). The failing check: dots on water are skipped, so the drawn count varies by seed, and scrub/snow run only for their biomes. The wall is one `beginPath/stroke` per 0.02 rad step (Tamaron 288, Epēshu 558) that merge into 4 and 10 runs. Keep stipple and vignette raster or pattern-fill; merge wall runs. One-for-one conversion would inflate counts several times.
- **[medium] The generator is deterministic and clock-free, but exposes no geometry** (gap-vecbudget C8 **[D]**). Two runs per city gave identical op logs and `R()` draw counts (Tamaron half-sea 17,289 draws, all-land 16,195; Epēshu half-sea 25,565, all-land 24,409); throwing `Math.random`/`Date.now` never fired. Op-log hashes are not comparable to `cityCanvasSha`. The failing check (medium): `genCityCanvas(name,t,a,mask)` returns no plan; it creates its own canvas and paints as it computes. A "separate vector consumer" therefore means either a second function that re-implements the plan, or a refactor of the pinned function with an owner-approved rebaseline. Recording draw calls is a measurement method, not a shipping path.
- **[reg] Registration of generated plans** (cross-ref section 2). Only the coast is registered, and only for cities with `drawWater` and mask water (vec V1 was refuted for claiming more). Gates come from `cityApproaches` over the traced polylines, which are themselves off the print (3.1). 18 of 135 generated plans have a printed river within 2.5·FOOT_R and 17 of them draw no river at all (G0-7, 2/2). Rivers are a registration source alongside anchors, charts and roads, and a vector plan through the same RV block misses the print the same way.

#### (b) The chart-conditioned Epēshu prototypes (`prototypes/epeshu-chart.html`, `prototypes/parcels-roofs.html`)

These are the closest thing in the repo to the owner's "drawn, interactive city": a hand trace of the DM's Epēshu chart (`prototypes/data/epeshu-trace.json`: coast, primaries, extent, plazas, field masks, 19 POIs; 5,478 B, 1,873 B gzip) driving a seeded block/parcel/roof fabric, with a 2D draw and a three.js 3D mode.

- **[medium] Seeded and clock-free, but on the wrong RNG** (G7-1, 2/2). Neither file contains `Math.random` or `Date.now`; each seeds one `mulberry32` from an FNV-1a `hashSeed` of a text-box string (`epeshu-chart.html` default 'epeshu'; `parcels-roofs.html` 'allhammers' plus a second stream on `seed+'|b'`). `performance.now()` feeds only `stats.ms`. Two node runs of 'epeshu' gave the same vhash a14ea42e. In `epeshu-chart.html` one stream feeds both `growRibs` and the per-lot draws, so adding a rib changes every later lot. (The 'allhammers' vhash depends on the pitch setting; cite it only with pitch stated.) A port should seed a sibling stream `xmur3(name+'|vec')()` and feed the existing `mulberry32` in `maps-site/index.html` — calling the pinned functions leaves their source, UG3 `atlas_fn_sha` and the `genCityCanvas` stream untouched.
- **[medium] A quarter of the "roofed buildings" have zero area** (G7-2, 2/2). Run in node (script text from "use strict" to the 'view + 2D UI' banner, in a vm context, no stubs), the pipeline reproduces the CHANGELOG 'Four prototypes join' figures exactly (984 blocks, 1,641 ribs, 5,837 parcels = 4,358 accepted + 1,479 repaired, 5,424 buildings, 0 overlaps, 73,422 triangles). But 1,377 of 5,424 buildings have zero area: `makeParcels` stamps an accepted lot with `claimStampLot`, and a later repair calls `rebuildLot(prev,...)`, whose `claimMaxDepth` starts inside the lot's own stamp and returns 0; `footprint()` then falls back to a zero-area quad. Clearing each lot's own stamp before the re-measure drops zero-area buildings from 1,377 to 87, so the own-stamp is the dominant cause, not proven the only one. The 4,047 non-degenerate buildings still have 0 pairwise overlaps. `parcels-roofs.html` has the same defect (20 of 204). **Quote 4,047 buildings, not 5,424**, and fix the repair path before any port.
- **[medium] Measured feature budget of the chart-conditioned Epēshu** (G7-3, 2/2). As emitted (seed 'epeshu', GeoJSON in atlas px through today's `cityBox`, `toFixed(3)`; delta-int over art px): 7,758 features, 29,360 vertices; GeoJSON 1,448,411 B (180,610 B gzip); delta-int q0.25 87,677 B (64,655 B gzip), q1 77,515 B (47,794 B gzip); 9 merged paths. Non-degenerate only: 6,279 features, 23,444 vertices; GeoJSON 1,159,005 B (153,137 B gzip); delta-int q0.25 70,053 B (53,281 B gzip). That is about 3.0× the features, 2.8× the vertices and 2.6× the gzip of the generator's counterfactual capital, and 2.8× the 8.5k-vertex live ceiling — over budget unless an LOD is used (blocks below ~z8, buildings only near `cityMaxZ`).
- **[reg] The trace coast duplicates existing coast evidence; it is a check, not a solver** (G7-4, 2/2; G7-5 **[D]**). The 24-vertex trace coast (densified to 2,693 points) sits from the print coast at rms 7.70 / p95 15.35 / max 17.54 atlas px under today's `cityBox` (mean nearest vector, print minus trace, toward SE at 134°); 3.43 / 6.01 / 6.18 at 0.6x+3E; 4.09 / 9.00 / 10.73 at 0.8x+3E. It sits 36.6 art px rms (~0.62 atlas px at 1.0x) from the chart's own water-class coast. The failing check on G7-5 said the numbers reproduce but the prose overreaches: a one-sided chamfer fit of an open 24-vertex polyline is degenerate (a free translation slides 13-18 atlas px south onto a different stretch of coast), consistent with the full chart-class coast also failing to pick a scale. Score the coast tie both directions, and do not solve a transform from the trace coast alone.
- **[reg] Road exits give at most 1-2 weak bearing constraints** (G7-6 **[D]**). Recomputed printed dotted-road dots (ink luma<110, 8-connected, bbox 4-11 z5 px, fill>0.55, aspect≤1.6, chained within 8 atlas px; 140 local dots, all 135 reference dots matched) cross each placed box at exactly two points (today: E edge heading 63.5°, S edge 109.5°). Only four trace endpoints reach the art frame (Sun Road and Kohabron Road on E; Marble Way N end and Northern Exit on N), and the best candidate (Marble Way S end) needs ~312 art px of extrapolation. The chart shows 4 frame exits plus a short S; the print shows 2. Use exits only as a heading check or tie-breaker; the coast (and a river, if any) drives the fit. Both checks reproduced the numbers; the failing check's objection is truncated.
- **[reg] The prototype never consults atlas water, so placed through `cityBox` it builds in the sea** (G7-7, 2/2). `build` blocks only the trace extent, the trace's own coast strip (`stampSeg(... COAST_STRIP*2 ...)`, COAST_STRIP=60), plazas and masks. Building centroids on print sea: 1,780 today, 595 at 0.6x+3E, 1,068 at 0.8x+3E; traced built extent on print sea 34.6% (394 atlas px²), 11.2% (46), 20.8% (152); POIs on print sea 10 of 19 today, 3 at 0.6x+3E (Thūkīn, Eelwynd, Mount Mastery), 5 at 0.8x+3E. A registered port must (1) transform the trace through the per-city override and (2) stamp the atlas water mask into `G.mask` as blocked, reading `sampleCityMask`'s cached output without editing that pinned function.
- **[reg] The trace POIs are derivative** (G7-8, 2/2). All 19 match `maps-site/data/chart-pois.json` by number and macron-blind name; scaled back to pyramid px (fx=3914/2863, fy=4200/3072) the residual is mean 0.60, max 0.92 pyramid px — rounding. Four trace names drop macrons (Epeshin Forum, The Mamban, Thukin Amphitheatre, Temple of the Ekromatkar). Take POIs from `chart-pois.json` (canon spellings), join on `n`. They move with whatever transform is adopted and add no registration tie.
- **[medium] Portability** (G7-10 **[D]**). Portable to `maps-site/index.html` under the rules: the 2D trace, the raster-grid fabric (`growRibs`, `extractBlocks`, `makeParcels`, `footprint`) and the 2D draw as merged Leaflet paths (`fillRule:'nonzero'`). Four changes needed: sibling `xmur3(name+'|vec')` stream with the pinned `mulberry32`; the repair-bug fix; atlas-water blocking; and build cost — about 1.2 s per build in node v22 (1,177-1,375 ms over 5 runs; 1,006×1,114-cell grid), a long task against the end-of-gesture budget, so ship the output static (153-181 KB gzip GeoJSON, or 53-65 KB gzip delta-int) or build once off the gesture path and cache. A phone is unmeasured. Not portable: the 3D mode (LC7 in `docs/living/rulings.json`: no WebGL in maps-site). The failing check added a fifth change: `drawTrace` draws filled POI discs at rest, which filigree R7 (nothing at rest, halos on hover only) forbids, so the draw must change.
- **[both] Nobody owns this geometry** (G7-11 **[D]**). `docs/filigree/todo-inputs.json` city.today lists both prototypes as "block/parcel/roof grain, not shipped" (the failing check: they also appear in `docs/filigree/research-dossier.md`). Slice C's scope is four named functions (`registerCityOverlay`, `genCityCanvas`, `washMake`, `sampleCityMask`), none a vector emitter. The street track (ST1) is sim-only and reads `s.streets`/`s.buildings` as data; living (LC7) owns the crossfade into a Street poster, not city geometry; POLISH "Sim ↔ atlas continuity" covers deep-link vocabulary only. The holding check splits the work: registration first, as a new atlas POLISH item writing new code beside slice C; the vector fabric as a later, separate item.

#### (c) The sim's Epēshu streets: `planStreetsFor`

- **[reg] The sim's Epēshu street plan is invented** (sim S4 **[D]**). Epēshu takes the generic capital template: `s.R = 265` (both branches of `genSettlements`); `planStreetsFor(s, R)` with `R = W.rng.gen`, called from `genTownPlans`, draws bent, Chaikin-smoothed spokes from road entries to the centre, a ring at 0.52R with a sin-wobble, an outer ring at 0.82R and 10 radial lanes at angles from `rr(R,0,2π)`; `genBuildings` lays parcels along the segments. Nothing in `buildEpeshuMapData` feeds streets (cityLabels are name offsets only; `EPESHU_ART_URI` is the 1024² atlas-window crop). The holding check frames it as a REGISTRATION source-of-truth problem, not MEDIUM: no sim street sits where the chart's streets sit, and a vector layer drawn from sim streets would agree with the sim but not the chart. The failing check's objection is truncated.
- **[reg] The roads, rivers and bridges that shape the sim's Epēshu are the sim's own** (sim S5, 2/2). `genRoads` runs A* (`roadAstar`) on a `const G = 192` cost grid (~47 m cells), then `chaikin(chaikin(path))` and `resample(...,24)`; `registerRoadEntries` pushes each crossing at 0.92·s.R into `s.entries`, which set the spokes and gate edges. Custom-map rivers are walked downhill from the heightfield peaks (`genHydrology`); `W.bridges` are sim-road × sim-river intersections. The sim never reads `traced-roads.json` or `named-ways.json`. This does not cause the atlas shift; it matters only where the apps meet (H2z).

---

### 3.3 What the rendering medium buys

- **[medium] Crispness, and earlier on phones than the dossier said** (G0-18 **[D]**). The DM raster shows 1 overlay texel per **CSS** px at Epēshu's `cityMaxZ` 9.885, not per device px. On DPR 2 it goes soft from z8.885, on DPR 2.625 from z8.49, on DPR 3 from z8.3. Every `flyIntoCity` arrival (`cityMaxZ-0.5`) lands at 1.414/DPR texels per device px: 0.471 on an iPhone 13. The same holds for every generated GEN_SIZE 2048 plan. So a vector layer (or a higher-resolution raster) adds crispness on phones well below 9.885. The failing check confirmed the numbers; its objection is truncated.
- **[medium] Overzoom without stretched pixels** (vlod VL5 **[D]**). In MapLibre's style spec (`@maplibre/maplibre-gl-style-spec` 26.4.4), `["zoom"]` may only drive a top-level `step`/`interpolate`; layers have `minzoom`/`maxzoom`; a source's `maxzoom` lets deeper zooms re-rasterise the last tile's geometry. Leaflet 1.9.4 has no style interpolation; the equivalent is a pure function of fractional zoom, shaped like `baseOpacity(z)`/`overlayOpacity(z)`, run from `worldOpacityUpdate` on `zoom zoomend moveend` (the filigree note's recipe: one CSS var `--z` written on 'zoom'). Both checks confirmed the spec quotes; the failing check's objection is truncated.
- **[medium] A level-of-detail ladder** (vlod VL6 **[D]**). OpenMapTiles serves merged building blocks only at z13 (DBSCAN-clustered, buffer-unioned, `ST_SimplifyVW`) and individual polygons from z14; MapLibre's 3D-buildings example starts extrusions at z15; OSM Carto drops sub-pixel buildings. The failing check: the OSM Bright fade sub-claim overreaches slightly. Transferable only as a ladder shape: ward/block fills first, footprints later, a fade band between, fitted into our z6.2-8 base fade and z6.8-7.8 overlay fade. It is not an alignment fix.
- **[both] Interactivity** (vlod VL11, 2/2; vlod VL4 **[D]**). A path uses `options.renderer`, else a per-pane renderer, else the map's; `_getPaneRenderer` builds one per named pane (except overlayPane); `preferCanvas` only flips the default. Canvas hit-tests by a linear scan in draw order (hover throttled to 32 ms) with a pixel-bounds early-out. The atlas map (`L.map('map',{crs:L.CRS.Simple,...`) sets neither `preferCanvas` nor `renderer`, and its vectors go into named panes (pRealm, pDistrict, pLane, pWay, pCity, ...), so each already has its own SVG renderer; a city layer can take its own `L.canvas({pane:'pCity'})`. Alternatively keep a registered raster as the painted surface (slice C; R4 never label blocks) and make hover/click a world-px lookup against a footprint list placed by the same registration transform — the swisstopo pattern (only its config constants are verified). A coordinate lookup over a raster still placed by `cityBox` inherits the same error.
- **[both] Shared road vertices — only after registration** (sim S13, 2/2). A shared city geometry would let atlas roads, city gates and sim spokes share vertices. But the vertices must be right first: the traced polylines are 17-98 px off the print in places (3.1), so snapping gates to them is pointless until the road data is re-traced onto the ink.

### 3.4 What the rendering medium costs

#### Per frame, during the flights that already exist

- **[medium] During a flyTo, vectors are a CSS-scaled copy of the last frame** (vlod VL9, 2/2). Leaflet 1.9.4's Renderer listens to 'zoom'/'zoomanim' and only CSS-transforms its container (`_updateTransform`); paths re-project at zoomend. `flyTo`'s `frame()` calls `_move()` every rAF (firing 'zoom'); it never sets `_animatingZoom`, so the `Canvas._update` early-return concerns wheel/pinch/setZoom, and for a flyTo the operative fact is redraw only at moveend, clipped to the renderer padding (default 0.1 of the view). Inference: a Canvas city blurs under the 2^dz scale, SVG strokes grow then snap at moveend, and on zoom-out content beyond the padding is missing until moveend. `flyIntoCity`/`ensureArrival` fly several zoom levels (to `max(z, cityMaxZ-0.5)`, ~z9.4-10.3), so a vector city that fades in mid-descent (as `ensureCityOverlay` adds the overlay eagerly) would look worse than the raster unless hidden while flying or redrawn per 'zoom'.
- **[medium] The cost lands at the end of each gesture** (gap-vecbudget C4 **[D]**). On zoomend `_onZoomEnd` calls `_project()` on **every layer the renderer holds**, not only on-screen ones (the failing check's correction: clipping comes later, so cost scales with all loaded vertices); on moveend `_update` fires `_updatePaths` (`_clipPoints`, `_simplifyPoints`, `_updatePath`). With `zoomSnap:0`, `zoomDelta:.25` and `wheelDebounceTime` 20, every wheel or pinch ends in one full re-projection together with `worldCityUpdate`. Measure that end-of-gesture long task, not only animation frames.
- **[medium] Clip and simplify run per renderer update, not per frame** (vec V12 **[D]**). `Polyline._update` runs `_clipPoints` (`LineUtil.clipSegment`; `PolyUtil.clipPolygon` for polygons) and `_simplifyPoints` (Douglas-Peucker at `smoothFactor`) on moveend/viewreset/zoomend; `noClip` skips clipping. SVG makes one path element per layer inside a shared `<g>`; Canvas draws all paths into one `<canvas>`. Every atlas vector today, the `buildCityStubs` polylines included, is an SVG path. About 17k separate `L.Polygon`s in SVG is not phone-safe.
- **[medium] One city at a time, merged by class** (gap-vecbudget C3 **[D]**). `worldCityUpdate` passes the nearest city to `ensureCityOverlay`, which removes the previous overlay, wash, stubs and POI layer, so only one plan is live: ~500-2,100 features or 1.9k-8.5k vertices for a generated plan (and 6-8k features / 23-29k vertices for the chart-conditioned Epēshu, G7-3). One SVG path per feature means 500-2,100 DOM paths; merging by class and style (`L.polygon` multipolygon, 3-level arrays, `fillRule:'nonzero'` because the default `evenodd` punches holes where footprints touch) gives 11-30 paths at the same vertex count. The failing check: the clause that rulings R4/R7 *allow* the merge overreaches — the merge is possible because nothing currently requires per-block hit targets, not because the rulings grant it. Per-building hover would push to a per-pane `L.canvas`.
- **[medium] Element budgets are rules of thumb only** (vlod VL12 **[D]**). "SVG slows past ~3,000-5,000 elements; Canvas handles 50,000+ at 60 fps" is **unverified** (both sources egress-blocked); directional only, never measured on iphone13/pixel7.
- **[medium] No atlas vector-cost data exists** (vlod VL17, 2/2). `docs/research/web-performance.md` lists source-confirmed Leaflet knobs (`updateWhenIdle`, `keepBuffer`, `updateWhenZooming:false`, `detectRetina`) and recommends `preferCanvas:true` for traced roads/rivers/regions, unapplied in `maps-site/index.html`. The mobile track records SwiftShader `fps_median` as information only, never gated. The only budget is generic (~10-15 ms per rAF callback, half at 120 Hz). Do **not** turn on a map-wide `preferCanvas`; choose per layer, and bring a measurement (path count, redraw ms during a `flyIntoCity` descent and the z6.8-7.8 crossfade) on the mobile profiles.

#### Bytes and feature counts of the canon charts

- **[medium] Both canon charts are flat-colour digital art** (vec V8, 2/2). On `art/overlay-Epeshu.jpg` (2863×3072), a building rule (R-G>18, G-B>18, 150<R<235) with 4-connected `scipy.ndimage.label` finds 6.1% building pixels, 17,323 blobs ≥12 px and 2,793 ≥40 px (8-connectivity: 17,723 / 2,864; largest blob 118 px). On `overlay-Summarch.jpg`: 9.7%, 3,769 and 3,682 (1,911 ≥200 px; largest 4,536 px, The Seat compound merged around badge 5). Dark outlines already split touching buildings; no ML or GPU is needed.
- **[medium] The traced-chart budget, measured** (G0-19, 2/2; G0-20, 2/2). G4 (`out/dm-chart-vector-budget.json`) traced the **pyramid tiles** (`charts/epeshu/3`, 3914×4200; `charts/summarch/3`) rather than the downscaled overlay. The likely cause of the count difference from V8 is the source (the pyramid has ~1.87× the pixels, so "≥12 px" means a different size); the check's own account is truncated, so this is an inference. G4 counts 23,184 Epēshu blobs ≥12 px and 17,931 ≥25; Summarch ~3,770 agrees. Both counts stand with their sources. A **skeleton trace** fits the live budget (≤8.5k vertices): Epēshu 4,624 vertices / 24,015 B GeoJSON gzip / 5,824 B delta-int gzip at DP 1; Summarch 931 / 5,326 / 2,133. A **full-building trace** does not: Summarch 4,325 features / 39,461 vertices (4.6×) / 200,373 B gzip; Epēshu 27,763 features / 177,225 vertices (20.9×) / 903,456 B gzip, building IoU 0.883 against the classified JPEG. These replace the refuted vec V13 figures. Two qualifiers from the checks: all figures come from the JPEG tiles, not DM masters; and the "skeleton" that fits is only coast/water, Summarch's wall ring and Epēshu's hinterland edge — **no street network**, because lanes are unpainted ground (MFCG style), and no separable Epēshu wall (drawn at outline width). Streets could still come from the negative space of the ground class (Epēshu 5,089 polygons / 105,656 vertices at DP 1) or a centreline graph via thinning, unmeasured.
- **[medium] Classic vectorization pipelines are overkill here** (vec V9 **[D]**; vec V10, 2/2). The soduco Paris-atlas benchmark (deep edge filter, watershed closed-shape extraction, raster-to-vector) needs PyTorch on a CUDA GPU and solves noisy scans; our charts are clean flat colour. Keep at most classical Meyer watershed as optional offline tooling for splitting merged footprints. mapKurator (text spotting and OSM linking on the Rumsey corpus) does not apply: Nīmlad has no OSM entities, and the 19 + 9 chart POIs are already in `chart-pois.json`. What a tracer needs is the reverse: mask the badges, legend and compass rose and in-fill under them. Dropping the POI badges leaves voids of about 540 building-equivalents in Epēshu (G0-21).
- **[medium] Tracers, offline only** (vec V6 **[D]**). Potrace (GPL) traces a bitmap into SVG, PDF, EPS, DXF, GeoJSON and more from PBM/PGM/PPM/BMP with a black/white cutoff (`-k`, default 0.5); `mkbitmap` pre-processes grey/colour input. Splitting colour art into per-class masks is our inference. AutoTrace adds colour reduction and centerline mode (quality on our thin roads untested). Never shipped or ported (the npm potrace port is also GPL-2); a `tools/` node script writes committed, deterministic data under `maps-site/data/`. Neither binary is installed here.
- **[medium] No new dependency** (vlod VL8, 2/2). geojson-vt slices GeoJSON into tiles in-browser (defaults tolerance 3, extent 4096); Leaflet.VectorGrid's Slicer draws them (default `rendererFactory: L.svg.tile`); VectorGrid's last npm release is 1.3.0 (2017-08-28). The atlas vendors only Leaflet (`maps-site/vendor/leaflet@1.9.4`, pinned by an SRI attribute on its `<script>` tag and separately by `vendor/SHA256SUMS`). One city is hundreds to low thousands of polygons in ~50 world px; a hand-rolled per-band simplifier and a flat int array with an inline decoder suffice.

#### Determinism and the mobile pins

- **[medium] UG3 pins and how they actually bind** (vlod VL18 **[D]**; vec V15 **[D]**). `tools/mobile-capture.js` hashes the source of `xmur3`, `mulberry32`, `genCityCanvas`, `washMake`, `sampleCityMask` (`atlas_fn_sha`) and `cityCanvasSha` over the invented `data:` URLs after descending into Tamaron, Sokundo and Kanae. `docs/mobile/gates.md` UG3 text asks equality across all five profiles, but the capture tool and the mobile README require equality **within each DPR class** (the 1x desktop differs, probably from canvas text hinting) — the operative check, per the VL18 failing check. UG3 also requires the `maps-site/index.html` clock-token count to stay at 1 narrow / 2 wide. A seeded vector plan can be hashed as geometry (rounded coordinates) instead of canvas bytes, which removes the DPR leak, but this is determinism, not registration. Filigree's README "Reuse map" row "painted city, pooled edges" gives slice C ownership of the four pinned functions. A runtime vector emitter must be a **new sibling function** on its own `xmur3(name+'|vec')` stream, and (holding check) wait until Mobile 9 and Mobile 14 are checked with no mobile run live; offline tracing (committed JSON regenerated by a `tools/` script, like `build-gazetteer.js`) touches no pin.
- **[medium] What does and does not move the hashes** (G0-9, 2/2; G0-21 **[D]**). A static skeleton trace of the canon charts touches no pinned function and leaves `cityCanvasSha` alone (canon overlays are never hashed), but adds a download the Mobile delivery budget must admit. Feeding atlas river geometry into `genCityCanvas`'s RV block changes `atlas_fn_sha.genCityCanvas` (source pinned) but, if RV-guarded, leaves `cityCanvasSha` equal to B0 (none of Tamaron, Sokundo, Kanae has a river); a mask-path change moves `cityCanvasSha`. The G0-21 failing check narrowed it to what `dm-chart-vector-budget.json` says: the asset is untouched "unless a later change consumes it inside those functions"; ownership advice there is labelled "Inference" — a proposal for owner question OQ14 (prior Q12), not a ruling.

---

### 3.5 The canon question: DM art, traced, generated, or chart-conditioned

#### The scale problem every option meets

- **[reg] Canon art and the footprint differ in scale by roughly 10-16×** (vec V3, 2/2). Summarch's own scale bar has major ticks at about art x 99, 429-430 and 756-759 (0/250/500 m), so 500 m ≈ 660 px and the 3072 px art is ~2.3 km wide. Summarch is kind 'town', so `cityBox` shows it 28 atlas px wide: ~23 Cyrikon mi at `MI_PER_NATIVE` = 600/723. Epēshu's box is ~48.5×52 (52 tall), ~43 mi. Narrowed by both checks: the ratio is measured only for Summarch (Epēshu inferred from box size), and it assumes a Cyrikon mile of 1-1.6 km (not in canon) and that the bar's "m" means metres. Consequence: no similarity or affine transform can land the art's coast and river on the atlas at both true scale and display scale. The city insets are symbols; registration can only be symbolic (drawn town on the printed mark, coast on the right side, roads leaving on printed bearings), and the art bends at the seam, never the atlas.
- **[reg] The scale choice has two criteria that conflict** (G0-15 **[D]**). Owner question OQ8 (prior Q4) was framed by the coast alone. The print coast favours 0.6x+3E (3.58 rms vs 5.88-7.21 at 1.0x/0.8x), but that fit is weak (the free fit runs to the edge of its search box; 0.6x is the bottom of the policy range). The sim's hand-placed labels favour about 1.0x (rms-radius ratio 1.12 with all 13 labels, 0.94 without Fairy Arch, median per-pair 1.01), and the sim extent favours ≥1.0x (built-up r_eq equals the sim disc only at 1.53-1.63x). Per-name label rms cannot choose (collapsing all POIs to the anchor already scores 221-226 m against 209-265 m), and direction disagreements (median 53°, 4 pairs >90°) survive any scale. The holding check: state both criteria, keep them apart; **the atlas wins for placement, the sim follows**; owner picks within ~0.6-0.8x.
- **[medium] One trace can feed both apps; one fabric cannot** (G7-12 **[D]**). The trace and the transform chain (art px → atlas via a per-city override → sim world via `atlas = (1060,1240) + grid·800/768`, 11.2647 m per atlas px) serve both consumers. The seeded fabric does not share a scale: at 1.0x one art px is 0.1907 m (0.1525 at 0.8x, 0.1144 at 0.6x), so the prototype's lots (LOTW 12 × LOTD 18 art px) come out 2.3×3.4 m (1.4×2.1 at 0.6x) and its median footprint 0.9×1.95 m, against `planParcelsFor` lots of 8.5-11 × 7.5-10.5 m: about 4-7× linear. The chart's own roofs (median sqrt-area 6.08 chart px) are also ~0.85 m at `cityBox` scale. The holding check rewords the conclusion: the atlas fabric is a drawn symbolisation with no metric scale, so the conflict is the sim's metric lot size against the chart's drawn density; it does **not** force a choice between sub-metre sim houses and ~270 atlas houses.

#### The options

| option | fidelity to DM art | registration | effort | bytes (Epēshu) | status of evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| (1) DM raster as is, through `cityBox` | full | none (8.48 rms coast error, tc-3) | zero | 2,171,695 B JPG; pyramid separate | today |
| (2) same raster, registered (translation + policy scale, CSS clip + sea fill at the seam) | full | fixed by data, either medium | hours of tie-picking per city | unchanged | section 2; vec V18 **[D]** |
| (3) skeleton trace (coast/water, Summarch wall, Epēshu hinterland edge), warped per vertex | shape exact where traced; no streets | inherits the fitted transform | a data job | 24,015 B gzip GeoJSON / 5,824 B delta-int | G0-19, G0-20 |
| (4) full-building trace | IoU 0.883 vs the JPEG | inherits the transform | its own project | 903,456 B gzip; 177k vertices | G0-19 |
| (5) chart-conditioned seeded fabric (prototype) | trace exact; fabric seeded | inherits the transform; must block atlas water | port + 4-5 fixes | 153-181 KB gzip / 53-65 KB delta-int | G7-3, G7-10 |
| (6) procedural vectors in the base frame | none (fine for invented cities) | coast only, by construction | sibling emitter | 21.5 KB delta-int gzip (counterfactual capital) | gap-vecbudget C1, C8 |

- **[both] Recommended order** (vec V18 **[D]**). Register the canon art first (it removes the shift whatever the medium), then trace, then a vector sibling emitter for invented cities after the mobile atlas lane closes. Both checks support the order; the holding check stated it as: (a) REGISTRATION first — offline fit on `art/overlay-Epeshu.jpg` and `art/overlay-Summarch.jpg` against atlas coast, river mouths and road entries, committed as a deterministic asset; the rest truncated. The "about a day per city" and "0.2-0.6 MB" figures in the original synthesis are superseded by G0-19 and G7-3.
- **[reg] A coast-band warp was tested and rejected as the seam fix** (G0-11, 2/2; G0-12, 2/2; G0-14, 2/2). At 0.6x+3E a non-rigid coast warp (along-shore sigma 2 atlas px, taper sigma R/2) shrinks the chart-to-print residual from 3.58/6.66 to 1.72/3.60 rms/max at R6, but land-over-sea drops only from 91.1 to 78.5/70.5 px², and the warp exposes a third seam sign (49-67 px² of uncovered box). Left open, total misregistered area is 156.2/165.8 against 166.4 unwarped (no net gain at R12); padded with flat sea it falls 36-38%. The static baseline reproduces (land-over-sea 91.9, sea-over-land 74.4, box 907.21 px², chart water 16.07%). The warp bends canon fabric: Wood Quay moves 3.5 atlas px (det 2.92), old harbour quarters 2.3-3.3; at 1.0x, R6 folds 3.6% of land and still leaves 388-471 px² of land over sea. Wall-strain numbers rest on a 21-vertex wall traced by eye that two sibling files say is not separable; hold them. The class that remains: policy translate+scale override, runtime CSS clip against atlas water, and a sea fill (3.7). A vector city inherits this choice; vectorising does not make the warp cheaper in fidelity terms, only in compute.
- **[reg] Per-vertex warping is cheap for vectors** (vec V4 **[D]**). `@allmaps/transform`'s README supports building a transform from GCPs and applying it to points, lines and polygons, forward and backward. Use it as an offline method: the repo has no `package.json` and its tools import only node built-ins, so implement least-squares Helmert/affine in a dependency-free `tools/` script whose output is committed atlas-px JSON. A raster needs resampling; a vector needs only the transform per vertex.
- **[reg] TPS does not fit our case** (vec V5 **[D]**). QGIS Georeferencer's thin-plate spline is for deformed hand-made maps and "technically requires a minimum of 10 GCPs" (docs confirmed only via search snippet). Inside Epēshu's ~52×52 atlas px box there are roughly 3-6 identifiable features (coast/harbour, river, road exits), so TPS is unsupportable; QGIS is at most an offline aid for picking points against the z5 tiles (atlas px = z5 px / 2, no y-flip).
- **[both] Ask the DM before tracing anything** (vec V17 **[D]**). Watabou's MFCG gained GeoJSON export in devlog 0.7.3 and a URL `export` parameter (png, svg, json); the developer called it not final. Both canon charts look like MFCG output (Summarch "Sun's March": framed legend, 0/250/500 m bar, numbered ward discs, towered wall, hatched fields, compass; Epēshu: same legend box, numbered discs, building texture). Authorship is an inference. If the DM has the seed/permalink or an export, the canon vectors exist exactly and need only the registration warp.

### 3.6 One geometry for the atlas and the sim?

- **[reg] The atlas↔sim transform exists only as literals** (sim S1, 2/2). The sim world is a 9000 m square on a 768-cell grid; world x = (gridX/767 − 0.5)·9000. For seed `epeshu` the grid is the atlas window [1060,1240]..[1860,2040], so atlasX ≈ 1060 + gridX·800/768 (likewise Y), about 11.265 m per atlas px (`gridToWorld` divides by N−1, while `tools/filigree-dem.js` uses SPAN/N). The check resolved the half-px ambiguity: the cell-lower-edge convention reproduces `city-anchors.json` within ~0.1 px (Epēshu 169,168 → 1236.04,1415.0 vs 1236.1,1415). No function in `index.html` does the conversion; it lives in the comment above `EPESHU_HF_URI` and in `tools/filigree-dem.js`. Procedural seeds have no atlas frame. This matters only for shared geometry and H2z; it does not fix the atlas-internal shift.
- **[reg] Sizes roughly agree, unlinked** (sim S8, 2/2). Capital R 265 m ≈ 23.5 atlas px vs FOOT_R 26; Sokundo (pop 780) ≈ 163 m ≈ 14.5 px vs 14; Kanae (pop 320) ≈ 71 m ≈ 6.3 px vs 9 (FOOT_R ~43% larger). No shared constant links `s.R` to `FOOT_R`; the m/px figure is derived, not a constant in either file.
- **[reg] Same-named places sit 100-400 m apart between the apps** (sim S6, 2/2). Converting chart POIs through `cityBox` (box.x0 + (p.x/poiRef.w)·box.fw; capital box ~546×586 m) and comparing with the sim's hand-placed `cityLabels` dx/dz: Forum chart (−107,7) vs sim (0,−15); Wood Quay (−132,−44) vs (−95,−150); Marble Quarter (−71,−89) vs (120,−105); Outwall (13,−100) north vs (25,200) south; Old Quarry Lot (−75,242) south vs (180,−175) NE; Eelwynd (−77,−228) NW vs (−190,150) SW; Kohabron (171,−108) vs (210,25). Both checks hedge: the metres assume ~11.25 m/px and the arbitrary `cityBox`, so they show disagreement, not who is right; "hand approximations" is an inference. The fix runs one way only — DM chart → registered to the atlas → written into the sim — never sim → chart.
- **[reg] The atlas chart's coast does not follow the atlas** (sim S7 **[D]**). Inside `cityBox` the chart shows a sea strip down its west edge; the atlas (and the sim heightfield) has the coast running NE-SW through the box with sea W/NW. No control point ties a charted coast, quay or road to the atlas. The holding check (major) refined it: the mismatch is more than an offset (chart shore ~N-S vs atlas NE-SW, much less sea in the chart), so the fix starts with a similarity transform; tc-3 later measured it (8.48 rms). A vector city through the same `cityBox` misaligns identically. The failing check found no warp code, consistent with the claim; its objection is truncated.
- **[reg] The H2z hand-off registers at one point only, and today aims at the sim label** (sim S10 **[D]**). Street frozen views are framed by `ANNALS.goto(x, z, R, true)` with yaw 0; `updateCamera` puts the camera on the +z (south) side looking north, pitch `lerp(0.30, 1.42, smooth(tFromAlt(R)))` — about 0.386 at R=30 per the holding check (units cut off in the check text; if radians, ~22°), not 17°. A perspective poster matches a north-up plan only at its focus and heading. SV1/SV3/SV8 focus on sim `cityLabels` ('Epēshīn Forum', 'Whitestreet', 'Wood Quay'), ~100 m from the chart's same-named POIs (S6): ~9.6 atlas px, ~150 CSS px at z8. The original "~1,700 px at Z_STREET 11.5" does not apply: over Epēshu the atlas is capped at `cityMaxZ` 9.885, and H2z's target is a perspective poster (gap-coasts tc-7, refuted on exactly this point). H2z is itself unbuilt (gated on Street 3/4, `street=1` and LC7).
- **[reg] Deep links cannot reach Epēshu's districts** (sim S11, 2/2). `applyGotoFromHash` matches only `W.settlements` by `s.name` and `CUSTOM_MAP_DATA.extraLandmarks` by `l.name`, then `// unknown name: ignore`; it never reads `s.cityLabels`, so `#goto=Epēshīn Forum` does nothing. Names also differ: 'Marble Quarter' vs 'the Marble Quarter', 'Thūkīn Amphitheatre' vs 'the Thūkīn Amphitheater', 'The Fairy Arch' vs 'Fairy Arch', 'Temple of Thobrauk Tegendes' vs templeName 'the Blue Temple of Thobrauk'. The holding check: this is identity/deep-link vocabulary, not registration — it does nothing for the zoom shift, but is a prerequisite for the H2z landmark exhibit. A shared geometry needs one id/name table per place, carrying atlas-px coordinates.
- **[both] The street track has no street code yet** (sim S12 **[D]**). HEAD `index.html` has zero STREET or ST-HOOK occurrences. In `docs/street/README.md`: ST1 keeps the track sim-only and hands the atlas↔sim link to "Sim ↔ atlas continuity" (POLISH, unchecked); ST2 says ground interpolates the 11.72 m grid with no new relief; ST12 puts the Blue Temple on Wood Quay's northern edge (Wood Quay being the sim's hand label, not the chart POI); ST4 keys randomness via `makeStream(W.seed+':st:'+…)`. A shared, registered city geometry is outside ST1 and needs its own item or a ruling change. (The original "the ground already follows the atlas" rested on the refuted sim S3; the supported fact is tc-1: the DEM 0 m contour sits rms 1.33 atlas px from the print coast, one-directional, within the window.)
- **[both] What a shared geometry requires** (sim S13, 2/2). (a) One coded atlas-px ↔ sim-metre transform in both apps. (b) Shared inputs as well as code: one atlas-derived land/water mask, one road-entry source (atlas traced roads, not the sim's 192² A*), one river source, one footprint radius, fixed settlement positions. (c) A per-city keyed stream, e.g. `xmur3(seed+':city:'+name)`, instead of sequential `W.rng.gen`. (d) For Epēshu, canon input: the DM chart warped to the atlas. Both checks reorder it: REGISTRATION — (d) for the two canon cities plus the atlas-side part of (b) for procedural ones — fixes the atlas in either medium on its own; cross-app items come later. The claim that moving `planStreetsFor` off the global stream changes the whole epeshu fingerprint is an inference; verified is that `genTownPlans`, `genBuildings`, `genHouses` and `genFlora` share one sequential `W.rng.gen` with `genTownPlans` first.
- **[both] Sim adoption is a whole-world rebaseline** (G7-13 **[D]**). `genTownPlans` draws every settlement's streets from `W.rng.gen`; `GEN_STEPS` then runs `genBuildings` (draws per lot along `s.segs`), `genHouses` and `genFlora` on the same stream. Any change in Epēshu's segment count shifts buildings, great houses, notables and flora across the world, and the chronicle that follows. Draw parity is impossible (downstream draws scale with segment length); a sibling stream for the chart branch removes the `planStreetsFor` draws but not `genBuildings`'. Keep the sim out of the first cut: atlas vector city first (atlas-only, sibling stream, beside the raster, registered); sim adoption a separate owner-gated LC17-class item with a declared rebaseline, waiting for Street 4 and coordinated with the street track and the Mobile sim lane. Both checks agree on the conclusion; the failing check's objection is truncated.

### 3.7 Registration preconditions a vector city inherits (cross-ref section 2)

These were measured for registration; they bind any city layer, raster or vector, because the vector would be placed by the same anchors, masks and polylines.

- **[reg] Anchors.** `maps_markers.json` matches 121 of 138 anchors by folded name, plus Lepon the Old ↔ marker "Lepon" through an alias the probe supplied (the code has no alias map); 82 within 3 px, 39 farther (40 with Lepon) (gap-anchor C1 **[D]**). Fix in an offline deterministic tool, not at runtime and not as a blanket marker-wins rule. The verdict table now stands at 45 move / 2 keep / 4 owner calls over 51 anchors; the other 87 sit on a printed ring (G0-1, 2/2; Drāmūz's marker is *to be* fixed). Sokundo ○ (1478.0,1450.0) and Kanae ○ (1523.0,1490.0) reproduce from the z5 tiles as candidate targets for the `cityCanvasSha` trio (G0-2 **[D]**). The point convention (ring centre vs ring−(1,0) vs ring−(1,1)) changes the `sampleCityMask` window (`Math.round((atlasX-1.35*rW)*2)`) and so the hash and Tamaron's sea set; rule on it before any B0 recapture (G0-3, 2/2). Sokundo, Kanae and Shengo have no marker, and six prov 'trace' records have marker ≈ anchor, both off print, so the census second pass must land before or with the anchor tool (G0-4, 2/2). Moving Tamaron invalidates `cityCanvasSha` on every profile and needs a B0/R1 re-cut; canon overlays are never hashed; a seam clip belongs as CSS on the existing overlay element (gap-consumers C5 **[D]**). Moving an anchor changes gates for six of 40 pairs (Summarch, Tamaron, Lepon the Old, Harney, Drāmūz, Hordon — six, not five), visible only in invented plans; Summarch is canon so its bearing changes have no effect (gap-anchor C6 **[D]**). Polylines drawn through stale anchors must be re-checked for anchors that move (not Drāmūz, kept) (gap-anchor C7 **[D]**). Shengo's sea: sea approaches never create gates — they only steer the quay bearing when `drawWater&&WD&&sv&&seaAppr.length` — so decide Shengo's sea first from the mask at the moved anchor (G0-6 **[D]**; Pomos loses both land gates under every convention). Penthelon's marker is on its ◉, but the `buildCityMask` test (luma≥110 and r−b≤54 = water) reads the olive tint (r−b 45-48) as lake water (47-50), so the ◉ footprint reads ~2% land; moving it needs another land/water source, which touches the pinned `sampleCityMask` (gap-anchor C3, 2/2). The same test fires on realm tints for 70 of 115 inland anchors (>50% water); today it only biases land hue, but any future inland clip or snap must not trust `mask.water` away from the coast (gap-print vop-09, 2/2).
- **[reg] Who reads the placement.** `cityBox` is read by `ensureCityOverlay`, `buildWorldPOIs`, `worldPOICoord` and `washMake`; `cityMaxZ` repeats the footR/aspect maths itself. An override applied to the overlay bounds alone would separate the art from its hover halos, undoing the "Epēshu roundel doubling" fix. **Any override (and any vector city) must go through one function all of these read, with `cityMaxZ` updated too** (gap-consumers C9, 2/2). Seventeen anchors reach `gazetteer.json` through the anchor path of `tools/build-gazetteer.js` (Sokundo, Kanae, Mago, Aldorūs, Rēketis, Āglaz, Mīth Mirdānak, Pharzeban, Sardil, Perōna, Tarap, Thalbaen, Medem, Shengo, Kardunash, Rhup, Lepon the Old); moving any of them requires regenerating the gazetteer (gap-consumers C11 **[D]**; the failing check counted ~29 of 33 differently, the holding check named these 17). Panel-offset logic for fly targets must take the sheet side from measured geometry, not a device class (gap-mobile C8 **[D]**; the +211 px landscape figure is not supported by the quotes).
- **[reg] Coasts.** The DEM 0 m contour and the print coast agree to rms 1.33 atlas px window-wide (one-directional DEM→print; p95 2.31, max 6.79), 1.50 symmetric for the near-Epēshu mainland (gap-coasts tc-1 **[D]**: "effectively one coast" does not hold window-wide; the print mask is the target). There is no dominant DEM-vs-print offset worth a correction transform (mean vector 0.31 px; best rigid shift 1.7-1.8 px only lowers mainland rms 1.50 → 1.02), but a ~1.3-1.5 px local residual remains — not "all of the error" is in the chart (gap-coasts tc-2 **[D]**).
- **[reg] Rivers.** No planned item delivers rivers that plans can use: filigree B-13's `rivers.json` covers only the DEM window (5 of 18 river cities plus Epēshu) and gates on overlap, and "Living later · trace the rivers" serves only barges. A plan-river consumer is new, unowned work, queued after the anchor fix and re-run at the moved anchors (Aldrakhar, Iet Ford, Bash Dargis gain box ink; Stony Ford appears) (G0-10 **[D]**).
- **[reg] The seam clip is itself a small vector product** (G8). It is the one place where vector geometry *is* the registration fix, because it is generated from the print, not from the art. The islet-filled print-land ring inside the Epēshu box, stored in atlas px beside `city-anchors.json` and generated offline, is 74 vertices / 984 B CSS `polygon()` at DP 0.25 for 0.6x+3E (148 / 2,007 B today), within the mask's own 0.5 px (G8-1 **[D]**; apply only to the canon overlays). Build its mask on a padded window and crop afterwards — best, read the cached `MASKQ[key].m` from the pinned `sampleCityMask` (70 atlas px window for a capital) without re-sampling (G8-3 **[D]**). Because `washMake` paints a land-tone radial gradient (alpha .94 only out to gradient stop .5, i.e. ~0.85 of the box half-size, then falling), clipping reveals tan wash, so the needed fill is a **sea** fill over print sea (G8-4 **[D]**). Carry it as an `L.imageOverlay` in pane pCityWash with `zIndex:2` (an `L.polygon` would sit in a z-index:auto `<svg>` under the re-appended wash), so no `createPane` and no `washMake` edit (G8-5, 2/2). An opaque base under the footprint is impossible without a per-footprint mask (G8-6 **[D]**). A runtime canvas mask is blocky at ×59 and duplicates pinned logic (G8-7 **[D]**); a PNG mask under `art/` forces a TILES_V bump and manifest regeneration (G8-8 **[D]**; inline SVG data-URI mask is the soft-edge fallback). Neither Living's 1:8 sheet sea mask nor filigree B-38 (which clips relief to the *chart* coast, the opposite direction) supplies it, so the anchoring track owns it (G8-10 **[D]**). Ship clip, fill and registration record as one unit, Epēshu only for now (G8-11 **[D]**; Summarch's harbor/sea fields exist, set to false/null).
- **[reg] Era sheets add nothing to register** (G9). Ink geometry inside every anchor's 2.5·FOOT_R circle is identical to modern within 1 atlas px on war (max 0.71) and imperial (max 1.12, Heron's House, a luma-110 threshold straddle, not a displacement) (G9-2 **[D]**; G9-6 **[D]**: 137 of 139 points have ink). Rivers and dotted roads at the flagged anchors match within 1 px (G9-5 **[D]**: 19 printed-river rows, not 18; Kolens has no ink). The colour mask differs per sheet because r−b≤54 reads tint, not ink; no bug today since `sampleCityMask` always fetches `tiles/5` (G9-3 **[D]**). So one registration record, and one vector city, measured on modern serve all three eras; the remaining era work is colour (the tan wash disc in Leponnia, ΔG≈29 / ΔB≈21 on war/imperial), which belongs to slice C and the pinned functions (G9-8 **[D]**).

### 3.8 What the evidence supports, in order

1. **Register first, in data, medium-blind.** Anchor fixes (offline tool, census pass first, convention ruled, `cityCanvasSha` rebaseline approved), then a per-city record for the two canon charts (translation + policy scale, owner picks ~0.6-0.8x for Epēshu), read by one function that `cityBox`'s consumers and `cityMaxZ` all share, then the print-derived seam clip + sea fill. This removes the shift the owner sees whatever the medium. A vector city before this step misaligns exactly like today's raster.
2. **Get crispness on phones without vectors if needed.** G0-18 shows softness from z8.3 on DPR 3; a higher-resolution or tiled registered raster addresses it inside the existing medium.
