3. **Skeleton trace as registration and hit geometry.** Coast/water (both), Summarch's wall ring, Epēshu's hinterland edge: ~24 KB gzip, offline, no pins touched. Use it as the coast tie and as a lookup list for hover/tap through the same transform (R7: halos on hover only; R4: no block labels).
4. **Only then a vector fabric, as its own owner-gated item.** Either the DM's own MFCG export if it exists (ask first), or the chart-conditioned prototype ported with its four-plus-one fixes (sibling stream, repair bug, atlas-water blocking, off-gesture build, no POI discs at rest), drawn merged by class, with an LOD below ~z8 and hidden or replaced during flies. Measure the end-of-gesture task on phones before committing (6-8k features, 23-29k vertices for Epēshu).
5. **Procedural cities** get a sibling vector emitter only after the mobile atlas lane closes; it must re-implement or refactor `genCityCanvas` (which exposes no geometry) and draw from `xmur3(name+'|vec')`.
6. **Keep the sim out** of the first cut. If adopted later, the trace and transform can be shared; the fabric's scale cannot, and the change is a whole-world `#s=epeshu` rebaseline.

### Refuted items (not findings)

| item | why refuted |
|---|---|
| sim S2 | Shengo/Rhup offsets reproduce (17 of 19 within 0.5 px), but the conclusion that the sim is the stale side is wrong for Shengo: its atlas anchor is itself off the print (G0-1 lists Shengo as a move) |
| sim S3 | the heightfield is hand-authored from the atlas window, but the described sea placement was wrong (sea lies SW/WSW, land due S for 143 cells) and "agrees at ~1 px" overstated; tc-1 is the measured comparison |
| sim S9 | separate generators and seeds confirmed, but the "~5.6 m per z5 px" figure was wrong and the conclusion is neither medium nor registration for the owner's ask |
| vec V1 | "invented plans already anchored by construction" holds only for the coast of coastal plans with `drawWater` and mask water; streets, walls, rivers and gates are seeded or bearing-only |
| vec V7 | VTracer's colour pipeline and compact stacked output are in the README, but the remaining stated details were not supported, and tracing through `cityBox` does not fix drift |
| vec V11 | effort estimate (10 s per footprint, 48 h) unsourced; the surviving point (trace only the skeleton; ask the DM for Watabou sources first) is carried by G0-19/G0-20 and vec V17 |
| vec V13 | traced-chart byte figures reproduced only within ~10% for Epēshu and not for Summarch; superseded by G0-19 and G7-3 |
| vec V14 | literature seen only via search summaries; and coast-conditioned "registration by construction" already exists in `sampleCityMask`/`genCityCanvas` |
| vec V16 | tracing gives exact shapes, not placement; traced footprints are aligned to the chart, not the atlas |
| gap-coasts tc-7 | over Epēshu the atlas is capped at 9.885 and the H2z target is a perspective Street poster, so there is no coast jump at 11.5 to measure |
| gap-coasts tc-9 | the patch check compares the numpy port with itself, not with the browser; figures are close to the dossier's but neither equal nor independent |
| gap-print vop-03 | the "Summarch to Stony Ford" road and the Undaukhōr crossing are not in the data, and Summarch does have an anchor (the `meshedWorldInit` literal) |
| gap-print vop-05 | the nearest printed bearing may belong to another road; and a re-trace is not hash-neutral, because `genCityCanvas` consumes `cityApproaches` |
| gap-print vop-08 | measurements sound, but the remedy contradicts the POLISH ruling that district rings were traced from dotted roads and are to be relabelled or retired with the road network |
| gap-consumers C1 | a .md-only bible edit does not trip Job 2, and a POLISH-only item is invisible to Job 2 and does not gate Job 3 |
| gap-consumers C8 | the sim grid matches the anchors for 17 of 19 only by coincidence of numbers; no runtime consumer reads it, and LC7/continuity are name-keyed |
| gap-consumers C10 | the literal 523,58 also lives in `factions.json` (Allhammers seat, read by 'show on the map') and `party-route.json` |
| gap-mobile C4 | gate mechanics right, but "off-dot with wrong sea sides" and "shares no code with any A-U unit" were not shown by the cited sources |
| gap-mobile C10 | only raster byte changes need a TILES_V bump; "any data JSON edit" is too broad, and the A-U11 same-file conflict was unsourced |
| G0-8 | Ion Ephel's defect is a data error, not a mask-code bug: its anchor (1339,2116) sits on the printed label, ~31 px from the ring (1311,2130) |
| G0-16 | the arithmetic holds (R = 23.52 atlas px; smallest wall 0.5R = 11.76 px), but r_eq is an area-equivalent radius, so "the whole built-up chart fits inside the smallest wall" is not shown |

## 4. How the owner's references keep zoom anchored

*Cross-references: the placement errors these references expose are measured in section 1 (§1.2) and section 2 (§2.4-§2.5); the medium-side lessons are section 3 (§3.1, §3.3); the H2z hand-off and its owner are section 5 (§5.2). Probes R1-R3 test the claims here.*

This section asks one question of every reference the owner shared (Patrick Collison's and Azlen Elza's posts, map.geo.admin.ch / swisstopo, the Tokyo streamed city, the Lombard Street exhibits, the How to Train Your Dragon living maps) and of the registration tools behind them: **what keeps their detail in place when the view zooms, and what of that transfers to the atlas?** Code anchors are in the HEAD export: `maps-site/index.html` for the atlas, `index.html` for the sim, and the vendored `maps-site/vendor/leaflet@1.9.4/dist/leaflet.js`, unless a path says otherwise. Coordinates are atlas px (1 world px = 1 atlas image px at NZ=4, y down, no flip against the z5 tiles).

> **KEY DISTINCTION.** REGISTRATION (where art or geometry sits relative to the atlas coast, rivers and roads) is what fixes misalignment. The RENDERING MEDIUM (raster or vector) brings crispness past the stretched image, interactivity and road vertices that can be shared with the atlas. **A vector city placed through the same `cityBox` misaligns exactly as the raster does.** Every finding below is tagged **[reg]** (registration), **[medium]** (rendering medium), **[both]**, or **[view]** (camera motion: neither registration nor medium).

**Status markers.** Unmarked = survived (2/2 checks held). **[D]** = disputed (1/2), followed by what the failing check disputed. Refuted items appear only in the closing list, with the reason. Where a check corrected an item, the corrected version is the one stated. Gap-round items supersede the earlier dossier where they differ. Internal vote bookkeeping that some claims carried ("VL1 … disputed 1/2", "C12 survived 2/2" and the like) is dropped: several checks failed partly because those labels could not be traced. Ids collide across research lenses, so each is cited with its lens: `refs C1-C17` (methods refs), `gap-refs RA-*` (reference-anchoring answer), `gap-coasts tc-4/tc-6` (three coasts at Epēshu), `G7-6` (chart-conditioned Epēshu prototype).

**Screen magnification.** Errors here are in atlas px. On screen they grow by 2^(z-4): ×9.85 at z7.3 (inside the overlay fade band 6.8-7.8), ×59.08 at Epēshu's live ceiling cityMaxZ 9.885, ×181 at Z_STREET 11.5.

---

### 4.1 Summary by reference

| reference | what keeps it anchored | what transfers to the atlas | items |
|---|---|---|---|
| swisstopo Journey Through Time | the client builds each WMTS layer's tile grid from the LV95 projection's resolutions, origin and extent (cut per layer at its maxResolution); a year change only swaps the WMTS `Time` dimension. Upstream georeferencing of the old sheets is inferred, not read | register each DM chart once, offline, store the result as data; at runtime only place, fade and cap zoom. Era bases already work this way; city art does not | refs C5 **[D]**, gap-refs RA-swisstopo-time **[D]**, refs C6 **[D]** |
| swisstopo vector base | MapLibre inside OpenLayers, mounted only under Web Mercator: the medium follows the frame | a vector city must live in atlas px (CRS.Simple) as Leaflet paths; MapLibre cannot share CRS.Simple | gap-refs RA-swisstopo-vector-base |
| swisstopo overlays, building identify, compare | overlays loaded but hidden in the layer list; identify is a backend request at a clicked coordinate; a swipe (`compareRatio`) compares layers | interactivity by coordinate lookup through the same registration transform as the art; a debug-only swipe audits the residual. Neither fixes alignment | gap-refs RA-swisstopo-buildings-overlays **[D]**, refs C13 **[D]** |
| swisstopo 2D→3D | hands over view state (centre, height from resolution), not a picture | the living track's H2/H2z link carries an atlas-px centre and a scale through the Epēshu atlas-sim transform | refs C12, gap-refs RA-swisstopo-3d |
| Tokyo streamed city (candidate jeantimex/tokyo, not confirmed as the post) and 3D Tiles | one metric frame; in 3D Tiles each tile's transform maps into its parent's frame, child content inside the parent's volume | a transform chain preserves registration authored in; it never creates it. A city detail level = atlas px plus one fitted transform | refs C11 **[D]**, gap-refs RA-tokyo |
| Collison's Bay Atlas (owner's brief) | stack unverified; if OSM vector tiles, the frame comes from shared source coordinates, not from the vector format | the one-frame principle only; "density is a property of the medium" is rejected | gap-refs RA-collison-bayatlas **[D]** |
| Azlen Elza's flyover (owner's brief) | one authored picture; "nothing to register" is the project's inference | an aesthetic reference for slice C; no alignment method | gap-refs RA-azlen **[D]** |
| Lombard Street exhibits, HTTYD maps | no source found | not precedents; the nearest verified exhibit pattern (camera pose + layer toggles) frames rather than anchors | refs C16 **[D]**, gap-refs RA-lombard-httyd **[D]** |
| StreetView3DOverlay, js-three | hand-tuned placement, self-described as "not very precise" | never hand-nudge bounds; fit control points and report the residual | refs C14 **[D]** |
| Registration tools (Leaflet CRS.Simple guide, Allmaps, Rotated/Distortable overlays) | bounds measured from known points; one fitted control-point transform | port the method (offline, deterministic, stored with its residual), not the libraries | refs C7 **[D]**, refs C8, gap-refs RA-tools **[D]** |

---

### 4.2 The common principle: one frame, then the medium

- **[both] Every anchored reference we could read keeps its detail in one frame; none stays anchored because it is vector** (gap-refs RA-principle **[D]**). Four sourced mechanisms support this. (1) swisstopo's WMTS layer builds its grid from the projection's own tile origin, resolutions and bounds (LV95), so its raster detail is registered upstream into one frame. (2) In 3D Tiles, `tile.transform` maps a tile's local coordinates into its parent's. (3) Leaflet 1.9.4's `L.SVGOverlay` extends `L.ImageOverlay` and is placed over specific bounds, exactly like a raster. (4) swisstopo mounts its vector layers only under Web Mercator, because MapLibre does not support other projections. *Disputed:* the failing check accepted these four points but rejected the universal wording ("every reference", "none … because it is vector") as the project's reasoned conclusion, not a sourced fact; the OSM-vector-tile example and the "shared road vertices" benefit carry no citation; and the swisstopo gate covers all VECTOR-type layers, not specifically a base map. Read it as a design conclusion backed by four examples.
  - *What transfers:* the atlas already has the frame. CRS.Simple atlas px at NZ=4 is shared by all three era pyramids (`makeBase('tiles/',5)`, `makeBase('tiles-war/',5)`, `makeBase('tiles-imperial/',5)` in `BASES`). City art is not registered into it: `cityBox(a,w,h)` centres a `FOOT_R[kind]` box (capital 26, town 14, village 9, ruin 8 world px) on the anchor and reads nothing the art depicts. The holding check adds: one affine fit may not suffice for hand-drawn art (allow a piecewise or thin-plate warp if residuals stay above about 1 world px, subject to an owner ruling, see 4.9); vector geometry should be `L.polyline`/`L.polygon` in atlas latlngs or canvas strokes transformed into atlas px, never an `L.svgOverlay`; procedural cities need the identity transform.
- **[medium] The swisstopo vector base follows the frame; it never replaces it** (gap-refs RA-swisstopo-vector-base). The web-mapviewer renders VECTOR layers (class `GeoAdminVectorLayer`, "MapLibre layer") through MapLibre inside OpenLayers via `@geoblocks/ol-maplibre-layer` (`OpenLayersVectorLayer.vue`). `OpenLayersInternalLayer.vue` mounts them only when `projection.epsg === WEBMERCATOR.epsg`, with the comment that they "won't work on LV95 yet, as MapLibre doesn't support other projection systems yet". The light base map style id `ch.swisstopo.leichte-basiskarte_world.vt` is `VECTOR_LIGHT_BASE_MAP_STYLE_ID` in `vectortiles.config.js`; that it is mounted as a VECTOR layer was inferred from the style URL, not checked directly. "The medium follows the frame" is the claim's inference from the gate.
  - *What transfers (corrected):* a vector city must be authored in atlas px and drawn with Leaflet's own paths, which `maps-site/index.html` already uses in CRS.Simple for routes, lanes and trails; on phones through one shared `L.canvas` renderer, not one SVG node per building. Being in atlas px is necessary but not sufficient: vector geometry placed through `cityBox` misaligns exactly as the raster does. MapLibre is out, but **not** because of the single-file rule (Leaflet itself is vendored). The reasons are that MapLibre has no arbitrary pixel CRS, and that it would add a second map engine and WebGL context against the mobile budget and duplicate the pane and zoom machinery.
- **[medium] A vector overlay is positioned by the same bounds box as a raster** (refs C9). Leaflet 1.9.4 `SVGOverlay` extends `ImageOverlay` and overrides only `_initImage`, so it inherits `_reset` (positions and sizes the element to the projected bounds) and `_animateZoom` (scales it from `_bounds`). An SVG or vectorised export of `overlay-Epeshu.jpg`, `overlay-Summarch.jpg` or a `genCityCanvas` plan given `cityBox` bounds keeps the same offset; only the stretching blur goes away. Correction from the checks: "exactly" needs one caveat. An SVG with a `viewBox` and the default `preserveAspectRatio` is letterboxed inside the box, where a raster is stretched; set `preserveAspectRatio="none"` or match aspects for identical placement. Leaflet vector layers (`L.polygon`, `L.polyline`) in atlas-px latlngs are positioned per vertex and do not go through a bounds box at all. Vectors help alignment only once their coordinates are registered: either atlas-px paths, or one `svgOverlay` whose `viewBox` is in atlas px and whose bounds equal that extent, after the DM art has been bent to the atlas by a control-point transform.

### 4.3 Leaflet itself does not drift

- **[reg] Leaflet pins an `L.imageOverlay` to the base within about one screen px** (refs C1 **[D]**). In the vendored `leaflet.js`, `ImageOverlay._reset` rounds both corners through `map.latLngToLayerPoint` (`project(...)._round()` minus the pixel origin) and runs on `zoom` and `viewreset`; `_animateZoom` uses `_latLngBoundsToNewLayerBounds`; `GridLayer._setZoomTransform` ends in `.round()`. The base (`makeBase`, an `L.tileLayer` with tileSize 256 and `detectRetina`) and every city overlay and wash (`ensureCityOverlay`, `washMake`: `L.imageOverlay(…, L.latLngBounds(px(…),px(…)))` in panes `pCity` and `pCityWash`, which carry only a z-index) all go through `map.project()`. Each is rounded independently, so they can differ by roughly 1 screen px, i.e. 2^-(z-4) atlas px (about 0.01 atlas px at z11). The error never accumulates. *Corrected:* it is **not** limited to the 250 ms zoom animation. It applies at rest too (through `_reset`, and at `zoomSnap 0` tiles are CSS-scaled from an integer tile zoom with a rounded translate) and on every `flyTo` frame. *Disputed:* the failing check could verify only the vendored minified copy (unpkg returned 403 for the npm tarball), and the 2^-(z-4) bound and "the shift is not Leaflet" are derived, not quoted. Both checks agree on the conclusion: Leaflet cannot produce a shift of the size the owner describes; the shift comes from where `cityBox` places the art.

### 4.4 What the references expose about today's placement

- **[reg] Today's city footprint is a fit, not a registration** (refs C2). `cityBox(a,w,h)` ("footprint square in world px; the image (w×h) is aspect-fit into a 2r side box") takes only the anchor, the kind and the image size; no coast, gate, river or road in the art is matched to the atlas. `registerCityOverlay` stores only url, w, h, prov, poiRef, pois and stubs: no control points, no bounds. Chart POIs reach the world through the same box (`worldPOICoord`, `buildWorldPOIs`), so they inherit any offset. Corrected numbers: Epēshu's art is **2863 wide × 3072 tall**, so the capital footprint (r=26) is **48.46 × 52** atlas px (x 1211.87-1260.33, y 1389-1441, matching `docs/filigree/research/q08.json`), and `cityMaxZ` = 4+log2(3072/52) ≈ **9.885**. The procedural plans are registered to the **coast only**, by construction: `sampleCityMask` reads the z5 tiles in a 1.35·FOOT_R margin box (a 5.4r patch) around the anchor and `genCityCanvas` draws its central 4r onto the square `cityBox` spans; roads and gates are not registered this way (a code reading, not a quote).
  - *Where the fix goes:* a per-city registration record kept as data (beside `city-anchors.json` or a new `city-registration.json`), read **inside** `cityBox` and `cityMaxZ` (or one helper both call), never only at the overlay call site: `cityBox` feeds the overlay bounds, the wash, `worldPOICoord`, `buildWorldPOIs` and the zoom cap, and any caller left out keeps the old offset. Cities without a record keep today's fit, so procedural output and `cityCanvasSha` stay byte-identical. Do not edit `genCityCanvas`, `washMake`, `sampleCityMask`, `xmur3` or `mulberry32` (UG3 atlas_fn_sha).
- **[reg] Even the best policy placement leaves a residual the medium cannot cure** (gap-coasts tc-4). Translate-plus-uniform-scale placements of the Epēshu chart (scale relative to today's `cityBox` half-size, centre shifted east in atlas px), scored chart-to-print / chart-to-DEM as rms/max atlas px (`out/three-coasts-epeshu.json`, `dm_art_placements`):

  | placement | to print (rms/max) | to DEM (rms/max) |
  |---|---|---|
  | today (1.0x, 0E) | 8.48 / 21.05 | 6.88 / 18.84 |
  | 0.6x+3E | **3.58 / 6.66** | **3.57 / 6.84** |
  | 0.6x+8E | 4.82 / 9.07 | 5.35 / 9.62 |
  | 0.8x+3E | 4.69 / 13.0 | 3.71 / 10.93 |
  | 0.8x+8E | 4.83 / 11.02 | 4.48 / 8.69 |
  | 1.0x+3E | 7.21 / 19.39 | 5.79 / 17.25 |
  | 1.0x+8E | 5.88 / 17.27 | 5.15 / 15.02 |

  0.6x+3E is best only among these six. In screen px its rms is 35.3/211.5/648.0 to print and 35.2/210.9/646.2 to the DEM at z7.3/9.885/11.5. A free translate+scale search (s 0.5-1.1, dx -6..12, dy -10..10) lands at s=0.5 (the lower edge of the box) against both references (print 2.04 rms at dx -3, dy +10; DEM 1.69 at dx -4, dy +9), so coast shape alone cannot choose the scale; the 0.6x "win" mostly shows the coast preferring the smallest box on offer. The pick stays a stated policy (an owner call). The ~3.6 px rms that remains is a chart-versus-atlas difference: a vector city placed by the same box carries it unchanged. It errs in both directions (at 0.6x+3E, 91 px² of art land over atlas sea, 75 px² of art sea over atlas land), so a clip against atlas water fixes only the first; the second needs the chart's water class made transparent or replaced by atlas sea. Only the mainland coast was scored; roads and rivers were not. `washMake` is pinned and centres on the anchor, so it does not follow a translated fit.
- **[reg] The DEM is not off the atlas; the chart is** (gap-coasts tc-6). Filigree B-38's "DEM shore runs up to 10 atlas px off" came from q08-c08, which scanned in today's `cityBox` frame (atlas y = 1389+cy/4200·52), so it measured the DEM against the `cityBox`-placed chart. Within about 60 atlas px of Epēshu the DEM coast sits 1.3-1.5 atlas px rms from the print-mask coast (p95 2.3-2.7, mainland max 5.4). Slice C should therefore clip relief to the **atlas print mask** (`buildCityMask`/`sampleCityMask` water class), not to the chart's coast, which even registered at 0.6x+3E is 3.6 px rms off. Caveats from the checks: the ~1.4 px is a DEM-coast-to-print-coast distance, not a measured clip result; it uses the raw DEM decode, not the `genHydrology`-carved W.H; the art-fit numbers use `overlay-Epeshu.jpg` while q08-c08 used the 4200-px mosaic. A print-mask clip is right only if slice C also makes the painted chart obey the print coast; otherwise clip contours to print-mask land ∩ chart land. This is filigree's call, as a proposed B-38 amendment.
- **[reg] Road exits are weak constraints, not ties** (G7-6 **[D]**: the failing check reproduced every number from the throwaway scripts; its objection is truncated in the text available here). Recomputed printed dotted-road dots (ink luminance < 110, 8-connected, bbox 4-11 z5 px, fill > 0.55, aspect ≤ 1.6, chained within 8 atlas px; 140 local dots, all 135 reference dots matched). The printed roads cross each placed box at exactly 2 points: today E edge (1260.3,1416.9) heading 63.5° and S edge (1239.5,1441.0) heading 109.5°; at 0.6x+3E (1253.6,1408.7) 35.8° and (1240.6,1430.6) 87.1°; at 0.8x+3E (1258.5,1413.5) 54.8° and (1240.7,1435.8) 93.0° (screen angles, 0=E, 90=S, undirected). Only four trace endpoints reach the art frame (Sun Road and Kohabron Road on E; Marble Way N end and Northern Exit on N); the chart has 4 frame exits plus a short S, the print 2. The road trace adds at most 1-2 placement-dependent bearing constraints; the best candidate (Marble Way S end) needs about 312 art px of extrapolation. Drive the Epēshu fit by the coast (and river, if any); use road exits only as a heading check or tie-breaker.

### 4.5 swisstopo: registered sheets, a fixed grid, a time dimension

- **[reg] Journey Through Time stays anchored on the client because the grid is fixed and only `Time` changes** (refs C5 **[D]**, gap-refs RA-swisstopo-time **[D]**). Read in `geoadmin/web-mapviewer` (develop): `useViewBasedOnProjection.composable.js` builds the LV95 View with `resolutions: constants.LV95_RESOLUTIONS`, `extent: LV95.bounds.flatten` and `constrainOnlyCenter: true`. `OpenLayersWMTSLayer.vue` builds a `WMTSTileGrid` from `projection.getResolutionSteps()`, `getTileOrigin()` and the bounds, sliced to `maxResolutionIndex` from the layer's `maxResolution`, so the grid is cut short per layer, not literally the same at every zoom. A year change only calls `getSource().updateDimensions({Time})`, and the tile URL carries a `{Time}` placeholder. `GeoAdminWMTSLayer.class.js` throws unless `maxResolution` is one of `SWISSTOPO_TILEGRID_RESOLUTIONS`; `CesiumWMTSLayer.vue` takes its URL from the same `getWmtsXyzUrl` helper with `addTimestamp: true`. *Disputed:* both failing checks accept these client facts and reject three things as inference: that the old sheets were registered upstream by control points (the swisstopo pages are egress-blocked; "the most up-to-date combination of map sheets" is a search snippet, which hints at server-side compositing per date rather than proving per-epoch registration); that `ch.swisstopo.zeitreihen@year=1864` uses this component (neither file names it); and "the client never re-fits a sheet" as a statement about the whole app.
  - *What transfers (corrected by the holding checks):* the principle (register once offline, store, then only place, fade and cap zoom) transfers; the practice exists today only for the era bases (`setEra` swaps three `makeBase` tile layers on the shared `tileBounds` grid; these are three layers, not a Time dimension, and whether the era images were themselves registered to the modern atlas was not verified). The city overlays are still placed by the runtime `cityBox`/FOOT_R guess. Do **not** put city art "on the atlas pyramid's grid": that grid is z0-5 at 1 world px = 1 atlas px, and a 2863-3072 px image covers only about 52 world px; the analogue is atlas-px bounds stored as data, or the same 4608×2304 frame extended to deeper levels. A stored per-city max zoom is the analogue of swisstopo's per-layer `maxResolution` cut, so `cityMaxZ` should read it. Zero runtime cost holds only for scale plus translation, because `L.imageOverlay` is axis-aligned; rotation, shear or nonlinear fit needs an offline pre-warp (art bent to the atlas). Every consumer reads the one stored transform: overlay bounds, `worldPOICoord`/`buildWorldPOIs`, the wash bounds, `cityMaxZ` and, later, the H2z hand-off.
- **[reg] Historical sheets are registered with control points, and survey accuracy bounds the result** (refs C6 **[D]**, low confidence). *Disputed:* the only evidence is a WebSearch summary; the primary sources (swisstopo Siegfried pages, Heitzler et al., e-Perimetron 13(2)) and every mirror tried are egress-blocked, and a fresh search did not reproduce the figure. Narrowest statement: an unconfirmed secondary summary says the 1888 Siegfried 1:50,000 guidelines allowed an average deviation over ten points of 0.7 mm (35 m at 1:50,000; the arithmetic is consistent), and that per-sheet georeferencing quality is critical for sheet comparison. The transform type swisstopo uses is unknown. *What transfers:* the method only, and only for the two DM canon charts (Epēshu, Summarch). Pick control points where the table checks the fit (coastline, river mouths, gates where dotted roads arrive), fit a low-order transform (similarity, or affine with at least 4 well-spread points), and report the leftover error per point in atlas px, judged at the zooms where it shows (one atlas px ≈ 7-14 screen px in the fade band, ≈181 at Z_STREET). The Siegfried figures are precedent, never a target.

### 4.6 Interactivity, comparison and the swipe audit

- **[both] swisstopo keeps overlays in reach and identifies by coordinate** (gap-refs RA-swisstopo-buildings-overlays **[D]**). The shared link opens on the bare colour base with all six overlays loaded but hidden (`,f`): they wait unchecked in the layer list ("overlays in reach, not on the base drawing", `docs/filigree/research-dossier.md` §1). In `map.config.js`, `DEFAULT_FEATURE_IDENTIFICATION_TOLERANCE = 10` ("In pixels") and `DEFAULT_FEATURE_COUNT_SINGLE_POINT = 10` ("how many features we will request our backends when identifying features under a single coordinate (mouse click)"; 50 for a rectangle): identify is a backend request at a clicked coordinate, not a client-side geometry hit-test. *Disputed:* that the 10 px constant is passed to the backend is inferred from its name; the claims about a blocked ckan host, the unverified WMS/WMTS form, and the internal vote labels are unsupported and dropped; the building register is "served by WMTS and REST" at medium-low confidence in the filigree dossier. *What transfers:* inverse-project the tap to atlas px and query a seeded or canon footprint list placed by the **same registration transform as the art**, folded into filigree's one tap resolver (FM4: one feature within 22 CSS px opens it, two or more open the chooser). The tolerance is in CSS px converted per zoom, never a fixed atlas-px radius. No per-building marker or DOM node; R4 (identify cards, never block labels) and R7/FM5 (nothing at rest, tap primary) hold. Today the lookup would inherit `cityBox`'s offset. Do not copy swisstopo's preloaded hidden overlays: the atlas builds city overlays lazily for the mobile budget.
- **[both] Comparison is a swipe between layers; the atlas fade is hand-built** (refs C13 **[D]**). swisstopo compares with `compareRatio` (0.0-1.0, active only strictly between 0 and 1, dropped from the URL when inactive) and a year slider that picks "the best sheet combination for a year"; each layer carries visibility and opacity. Leaflet fades per tile within one layer, never between two sheets (dossier finding 37), so the atlas hand-builds `baseOpacity` (1→0.12 over z6.2-8) and `overlayOpacity` (z6.8-7.8). *Disputed:* a crossfade between Swiss sheets was not read in the geoadmin code ("or a fade" is unverified); the time-slider layers should not be cited (filigree finding 10); NLS's and David Rumsey's transparency sliders (findings 29, 31) are secondhand with split lens confidence, and "georeferenced sheets" is an inference. *What transfers:* a **debug-only** swipe or opacity override (behind a URL flag, never at rest, per R7) at a fixed zoom of about 7-8, over the in-world overlay against the base it covers, is the cheapest way to see registration error. It must force both opacities to 1 (the base is 0.12 at city zoom), clip the overlay with CSS rather than touch `washMake`/`genCityCanvas`/`sampleCityMask`, and land after the Mobile 5-9 lane. It cannot audit the modal chart: `enterChart` removes `curBase` and draws in chart pixel space. The swipe measures error by eye; it does not fix it, and the fade only hides it.

### 4.7 Hand-offs and nested detail: 2D→3D and streamed tiles

- **[reg] A 2D→3D hand-off stays anchored by transferring view state, not a picture** (refs C12; gap-refs RA-swisstopo-3d). In `CesiumCamera.vue`, `initCamera()` (only when no camera position is already stored, e.g. from the URL) flies to the 2D centre at height `calculateHeight(resolution, canvas.clientWidth)` = resolution·width/(2·tan(π/6)) (`cameraUtils.js`), pitch −π/2, heading the negated 2D rotation. On unmount, `setCenterToCameraTarget` picks a ray through the canvas centre against the globe and, if it hits, dispatches `setCenter`; only the centre returns, not zoom. Cesium's own `SceneTransitioner.morphFrom2DTo3D` is staged (duration/3 per stage) with eased lerps, not one linear lerp; it has no counterpart here, because the atlas and sim are separate pages and engines.
  - *What transfers (living track, H2/H2z, Epēshu window only):* the atlas-sim transform already exists in the `EPESHU_HF` comment of `index.html`: atlas px = (1060,1240) + grid·800/768, `gridToWorld(i)` = (i/(N−1) − 0.5)·W_SIZE with N=768, W_SIZE=9000 (about 11.7 m per atlas px); Epēshu grid (169,168) → (1236,1415) matches `city-anchors.json`. It is valid only for atlas px [1060,1240]..[1860,2040]; no `worldToAtlas` function exists in either file yet. Today `applyGotoFromHash` takes only a name and flies to a fixed altitude 600, and the atlas `setHash` writes only `#place=`/`#chart=`. The link should carry an atlas-px centre plus a scale: at atlas zoom z one CSS px = 2^(4−z) atlas px; convert through 800/768 to metres and set the camera height from res·canvasWidth/(2·tan(fov/2)), starting top-down (the sim's orbit camera is pitched, so the geoadmin formula holds only for the top-down start). The reverse needs no ray-cast: `CAM.focus` is already the ground target. The H2z poster must be rendered from the camera this mapping gives, and a perspective poster can be anchored only at the focus point, not registered full-frame. The two problems meet at the H2z seam: crossfading from the `cityBox`-placed overlay would show the overlay's own error, so crossfade from the base or register the overlay first. View keys are not generation inputs, so determinism and the mobile pins are untouched; like all of H2 this waits for `street=1` (Street 3/4).
- **[reg] Streamed cities stay anchored because content is authored into one frame; nesting preserves that, it does not create it** (refs C11 **[D]**; gap-refs RA-tokyo). The candidate repo `github.com/jeantimex/tokyo` (not confirmed as the owner's post) states a single world frame (metres, x east, y up, z south, origin at the area's centre) and 256 m tiles. The 3D Tiles spec says a tile's `transform` maps its local coordinates to its parent's, and each bounding volume completely encloses its content, child content inside the parent's volume; REPLACE renders children instead of the parent, ADD renders both. *Disputed (C11):* these are containment conventions a tileset author must satisfy; they do not by themselves guarantee a refinement swap cannot move content (hence 3D Tiles carries a geometric error), and the two sources describe different mechanisms (one flat frame vs a composed transform chain). RA-tokyo states the corrected form and survived 2/2. *What transfers:* a city detail level should be a child of the atlas frame: atlas px plus **one stored transform per city**. `cityBox` is already a parent-relative transform, but both its translation (centred on the dot, assumed) and its scale (FOOT_R × aspect, assumed) are not fitted, and it has no rotation. Procedural plans are children of the atlas frame for their coast; the DM canon art is the "separately fitted picture" that needs control points. Streaming, octrees and bounding-volume machinery do not apply to a two-level Leaflet raster.

### 4.8 The owner's other references, and the drift precedents

- **[both] Collison's Bay Atlas** (gap-refs RA-collison-bayatlas **[D]**, owner's brief, low confidence). From the brief: the 29 Sep 2026 post wants to "wallow in cartographic filigree", and Bay Atlas draws the Sonoma coast in a style after swisstopo. The attributed stack (MapLibre + PMTiles + Mapzen terrain + OSM) is unverified; no repo was found. The Mapbox Vector Tile spec 2.1 §3 says a tile "SHOULD NOT contain information about its bounds and projection"; the decoder is assumed to know them. *Disputed:* that is a property of the format, not evidence about Bay Atlas; nothing read supports "it stays anchored" or "its density is a property of the medium". *What transfers:* only the principle. In an OSM-style stack layers line up because all data shares one source coordinate system, not because it is vector. "Density is a property of the medium" is **rejected**: Swiss and OSM density comes from rich data, and reading it as "vectorising the city fixes the shift" would misdirect the build.
- **[medium] Azlen Elza's flyover** (gap-refs RA-azlen **[D]**, owner's brief, low confidence). The brief records the 1 Oct 2026 post (date decoded from the post id) as a thirteen-second painted flyover of San Francisco with no chrome or pins, "a camera move over a painted sheet, not a live map". *Disputed:* that it is "anchored trivially, nothing to register" is this project's inference; the brief says nothing about anchoring, and the flyover could be generated or composited. *What transfers:* an aesthetic (block grain, park voids, ocher arterials, fog; filigree slice C under R4 and R7), no alignment method. The atlas holds at least two pictures (the PatrinorModern sheet and the `cityBox`-placed city art, plus the chart pyramids and the H2z poster); they behave as one sheet only once the city is painted in, or warped into, atlas px.
- **[both] Lombard Street exhibits and How to Train Your Dragon maps** (refs C16 **[D]**, gap-refs RA-lombard-httyd **[D]**). Four searches found no three.js or 3D Lombard Street exhibit; `docs/living/research-dossier.md` §1 calls it owner-recalled and unverified, "never a precedent". HTTYD is recorded as a mood with no map source (`docs/research/living-chart.md`). *Disputed:* the "repo research" label and the "two more web searches … only generic 3D Tiles demos" note are unverifiable and dropped; a "C16 disputed" cross-reference the claim carried collides with an unrelated id in `docs/living/todo-inputs.json` and is dropped. The nearest verified exhibit pattern is a Mapbox storytelling chapter: camera pose plus layer toggles plus a callback (living-chart.md maps it to an exhibit = centre + zoom + rows). *What transfers:* a narrative pattern only. No exhibit code exists yet (`grep exhibit` finds nothing in `maps-site/index.html`). A target inside a city (for example a chart POI) is chart px pushed through `worldPOICoord` → `cityBox`, the same path `flyIntoCity`'s `poiN` uses, so it inherits the city layer's offset literally. World-scale motion (caravans, ships) sits in atlas px directly and is already registered; it diverges only from the city art at the city edge. Fix registration first and exhibits become correct for free, raster or vector.
- **[reg] Detail over imagery without real registration is imprecise** (refs C14 **[D]**). The `rbejar/StreetView3DOverlay` README says its panorama parameters "have been established by trial-and-error", that the model's "position and orientation … is not very precise", and that panorama distortion "is not uniform". The `googlemaps/js-three` README says GPU math uses float32 ("about 7 decimal digits") and that the anchor (world-space origin) should be set near the content. *Disputed:* "drifts" is the dossier's word (the source says imprecise); js-three's anchor is "chosen near the scene", not fixed. *What transfers:* never hand-nudge bounds; fit control points and report the RMS residual in atlas px. The precision point applies only to a WebGL layer (H1, owner-gated): Leaflet 1.9.4 draws overlays, SVG and Canvas relative to `pixelOrigin` in float64, so a 2D canvas detail layer needs no local origin even at Z_STREET 11.5 (1 atlas px ≈ 181 screen px, project arithmetic, world ≈ 834k px wide). Precision does not cause today's shift; registration does.

### 4.9 Registration tools: port the method, not the libraries

- **[reg] The transform ladder** (refs C7 **[D]**). `@allmaps/transform` 1.0.0-beta.53 lists: straight (2 GCPs) translation+scale; helmert (2) adds rotation, preserving shapes and angles; polynomial/polynomial1 (3) adds shear, preserving lines and parallelism; polynomial2 (6) and polynomial3 (10) add "bending flexibility"; thinPlateSpline (3) is "rubber sheeting" with an affine part, "exact" at GCPs. `@allmaps/leaflet` 1.0.0-beta.55 renders georeferenced IIIF images with WebGL (WebGL2WarpedMap) and its example sets `zoomAnimationThreshold: 1` because multi-level zoom animations are not supported. *Disputed:* the ordering by amount of bend is the claim's own; "EPSG:4326/3857" is dropped (neither README names 3857; 4326 is only a default output); the layer takes Georeference Annotations (IIIF). *What transfers:* the method, as an offline node tool beside `tools/build-gazetteer.js`, fitting DM-chart px → atlas px from control points on atlas features. Try (a) straight / scale+translate first: this needs only new axis-aligned bounds, no resampling; (b) Helmert only if the fitted rotation is not negligible, written as a pre-warped PNG or WebP **with alpha** (rotation leaves empty corners; not JPG); (c) TPS or polynomial2/3 bend canon art and need an owner ruling. Corrected: `registerCityOverlay` does **not** stay unchanged; it needs a bounds override that `cityBox` and `cityMaxZ` honour, and the canon POIs (reprojected through `poiRef`) must take the same transform. Allmaps itself is out: WebGL2, a georeferenced CRS, IIIF, a beta dependency chain and a build, against CRS.Simple and the custom wheel handler (`map.scrollWheelZoom.disable()` plus the `'wheel'` listener).
- **[reg] Leaflet-native corner placement exists without WebGL** (refs C8). `leaflet-imageoverlay-rotated` 0.2.1 (2019): `L.ImageOverlay.Rotated` is "defined by three points (top-left, top-right and bottom-left corners)", "rotated and skewed", "tested only with Leaflet 1.0.0-beta and 1.0.0-rc1" ("affine" is an inference from three points). `leaflet-distortableimage` 0.21.9 (2022): "perspectival distortions … using CSS3 transformations in the DOM", "over 100 images smoothly, even on a smartphone" (self-reported), four corners via `getCorners()`; `matrix3d` comes from the package source (`src/util/DomUtil.js`), not the README. *What transfers, cheapest first (holding check):* (1) a per-city registered axis-aligned box solved from 2+ control points, which a plain `L.imageOverlay` already displays with separate x/y scale and offset, with no edit to pinned sources; (2) only if residuals show rotation or shear, a three-corner affine, preferably pre-warped offline, or a small in-file `ImageOverlay` subclass (~40 lines) that overrides both `_reset` and `_animateZoom` (Leaflet writes the transform itself via `DomUtil.setTransform`), with the same affine applied to POIs, wash bounds and road stubs through one shared image-px→atlas function. Take neither plugin: Rotated is untested past the 1.0 betas and DistortableImage is an editing UI with handles and dependencies; a four-corner perspective adds nothing for top-down art. Leaflet is self-hosted, so the objection is plugin age and weight, not a CDN rule.
- **[reg] The tools agree on measured, fitted placement** (gap-refs RA-tools **[D]**). Supported: Leaflet 1.9.4's CRS.Simple tutorial finds bounds by "measuring how many pixels there are between the 0 and 1000 coordinates, and extrapolating"; the Allmaps transform table above; `latLngToLayerPoint` rounding plus `ImageOverlay._reset` gives roughly ≤0.5 px position and ≤1 px size error at rest; StreetView3DOverlay's hand-tuned placement is "not very precise". *Disputed and dropped:* "not from approximate bounds" (a gloss); "rasters and vectors alike" (raster warping is the separate `@allmaps/render` package); and the sentence that the Azgaar→Watabou link and TownGeneratorOS "are never registered to the world map" (the cited `Model.hx` line is only a random start angle for the patch spiral). *What transfers:* an offline, deterministic fit from overlay px to atlas px for `art/overlay-Epeshu.jpg` and `art/overlay-Summarch.jpg`, stored per city with its residual, swapped in for `cityBox` only where `registerCityOverlay` entries have `prov:'canon'`. Procedural cities need no fit (refitting them would shift the bounds the mobile pins capture). Scale+uniform-translate first; Helmert's rotation cannot be shown by `L.imageOverlay` (custom layer or offline-rotated image, owner to agree); TPS needs an owner ruling. Every `cityBox` consumer for a canon city (POIs via `poiRef`, `pCityWash` bounds, `cityMaxZ`, the seam fade) must read the same transform, or art and pins separate.

### 4.10 Detail by zoom, and motion that is not registration

- **[medium] Past z5, detail must come from authored city content; the stretched base cannot add it** (refs C15 **[D]**). A Leaflet GridLayer renders one integer tile level, CSS-scaled to fractional zoom; `makeBase` caps `maxNativeZoom` at 5 (4 on retina) and stretches to Z_STREET 11.5 while `baseOpacity` eases it 1→0.12 over z6.2-8. *Disputed:* "in the references" is unevidenced (threshold-plus-ease is the project's own ruling R6, B-28 and the cartographic-filigree "appear effect", ≤0.25 zoom or 250 ms); "never blend levels" is too absolute (new tiles fade in over 200 ms with parents retained, findings 37-38, though there is no continuous interpolation). *Corrected scope:* drop the sheet-wide z6/z7 storage figure (≈36/145 MB per era) as a cost: the atlas has no detail past z4 (NZ=4 is 1:1 with the 4390×2188 PNG; z5 is already a "2x LANCZOS + unsharp" upsample), so a sheet-wide z6+ raster would only interpolate. City-scale authored content already exists (the DM overlays, native to about z9.9; the `charts/epeshu` and `charts/summarch` pyramids; `genCityCanvas`). What is missing is registration, not detail. Order: register first, then choose the medium (the existing DM raster placed by the fitted transform, or registered atlas-px vectors).
- **[view] The view zooms out on a pan** (refs C3). `worldCityUpdate` runs on `zoomend moveend` and calls `map.setMaxZoom(allowed)` ("Leaflet eases down"); in Leaflet 1.9.4 `setMaxZoom` calls `setZoom(newMax)` when the current zoom is above it, which animates a zoom-out about the centre. `allowed` drops to `Z_OPEN_MAX` 8.0 when `nearestCity` returns null, i.e. when the pan takes the centre more than 3·footR from every anchor (or no reg exists); between cities this fires only if the path crosses open country or the 3r zones overlap, or a neighbour has a lower `cityMaxZ` (about 9.9 for a 3072-px capital, 10.8-11 for 2048-px villages and ruins). *Corrected tag:* this is a view-control behaviour, not registration: fixing it moves no overlay relative to the coast, rivers or roads, and it must not be counted toward the alignment fix. It is intentional ("soft ceiling") but unrequested motion that adds to the owner's "things shift". Fix options: lower maxZoom only on a user zoom gesture, add hysteresis, or clamp with a visible gentle `setView`. Touches no pinned function; queue behind Mobile 5-9 (Mobile 8 adds zoom steps) and coordinate with the living track, which owns deep zoom.

### 4.11 What the references add up to

1. **Register first, in data, offline.** One stored transform per canon city (Epēshu, Summarch), fitted from control points on the atlas coast (and river), with road exits only as a tie-breaker, and its residual reported in atlas px. Art bends to the atlas; the atlas never moves. Start with scale+translate (bounds only), escalate to Helmert or a warp only with evidence and an owner ruling.
2. **One transform, every consumer.** `cityBox`/`cityMaxZ` read it; overlay bounds, wash, `worldPOICoord`/`buildWorldPOIs`, any vector geometry, the tap lookup and the H2z hand-off all inherit it. Cities without a record keep today's fit, so pinned hashes stay valid.
3. **Then choose the medium.** Vectors as `L.polyline`/`L.polygon` in atlas px (one `L.canvas` per city on phones), never an `svgOverlay` through `cityBox`. They buy crispness, interactivity and shared road vertices, not alignment.
4. **Expect a residual and hide the seam deliberately.** Even 0.6x+3E leaves ~3.6 px rms of both signs; clip relief to the print mask and treat chart sea over atlas land explicitly.
5. **Separate the non-registration motion.** The `setMaxZoom` ease-down is a view fix; the H2/H2z link carries view state; neither touches placement.

### Refuted (not findings)

- **refs C4** (one overlay per nearest city, art pops between neighbours): the code observation is correct (`ensureCityOverlay` removes the overlay, POIs, wash and stubs and adds the next city's at `overlayOpacity(z)`, no cross-fade; close pairs such as Epēshu/Lepon the Old 89.8 px, Phumkha/Lendānak 24 px, Hordon/Maeges 0 px). Refuted because the comparative sentence ("neither the references nor Leaflet do this; they keep every sheet in place") has no evidence beyond atlas code, the `[registration]` tag is wrong (it is a stability fix: each sheet would still sit in its own `cityBox`), and the LRU (cap 6, `lruPut`) only caches overlay objects; it does not keep neighbours mounted. The pop also has a `setMaxZoom` component.
- **refs C10** (atlas-px geometry "is the medium that lines up"): Leaflet's vector re-projection is real (`Renderer._onZoomEnd` re-projects from stored latlngs; CSS transform only mid-animation; `smoothFactor` 1.0 in screen px), but the conclusion merges medium with registration. The shipped `buildCityStubs` polylines (only for `prov==='invented'`) map their inner end through `scl=S/(2*footR(a.kind))`, i.e. the same `cityBox` registration, and their outer end is a synthetic point at footR·1.18, not a vertex shared with `buildNamedWays`. The MapLibre-overzoom equivalence is an analogy, "sharp at any zoom" holds only at rest, and no `preferCanvas`/`L.canvas` is shipped.
- **refs C17** (`enterChart` lives in a frame unrelated to the atlas): the code fact holds (`atZoom = def.maxz`, `cpx = map.unproject([x,y],atZoom)`, `removeLayer(curBase)`, `setMaxBounds(cb)`). Refuted because the frames are not unrelated (the canon POIs are stored once in chart px and reach the overlay through `poiRef`, an implicit, wrong, chart-px→atlas-px map; whether overlay and pyramid are byte-the-same art is unsettled, mean RGB difference 13.6 in q08), because the pyramid cannot be re-pointed as a bounded `L.tileLayer` (its power-of-two chart grid cannot express an arbitrary registered scale and offset; that needs an offline re-cut near z10.2 or a custom transforming GridLayer), and because merging the modal does not address the owner's complaint, which lives in the meshed zoom path.

---

## 5. Ownership, constraints and sequencing across the tracks; owner questions

*Cross-references: the fixes being sequenced are section 1 (§1.6) and section 2 (§2.12); the vector-city order is section 3 (§3.8); the deduplicated owner questions, with the prior Q-numbers in brackets, are under "Owner questions" below.*

This section answers three questions for the owner and the planners. Who owns each part of the anchoring work today? Which gates, pins and rulings bind it? In what order can it land without colliding with the four live tracks (mobile, filigree, street, living)? Code anchors are in the HEAD export: `maps-site/index.html` is the atlas, `index.html` is the sim, unless a path says otherwise. Coordinates are atlas px (1 world px = 1 atlas image px at NZ=4, y down, no flip against the z5 tiles).

**The key distinction, which every finding below carries.** REGISTRATION decides where art and geometry sit relative to atlas features (coast, rivers, dotted roads, the printed ring-dots), and it is the only thing that fixes misalignment. The RENDERING MEDIUM (raster or vector) brings crispness past the stretched image, interactivity, and road vertices that can be shared with the atlas. **A vector city placed through the same `cityBox` misaligns exactly as the raster does.** Ownership follows the same split. Registration has no owner today. The medium and the look are split between filigree (slice C), living (the zoom-through) and street (the sim). Findings are tagged **[reg]**, **[medium]**, **[present]** (camera or presentation, neither of the two) or **[process]** (queue and gates).

Status markers: unmarked = survived (2/2 checks held). **[D]** = disputed (1/2): the wording given is the one the holding check supports, and what the failing check disputed is stated inline or, for every disputed item, in the table "Disputed items: what the failing check disputed" before the refuted list. Refuted items (0/2) appear only in the closing table. Where a check corrected an item, the corrected version is the one stated. Gap-round items supersede the earlier dossier where they differ. Ids from different lenses collide (three `C1`s), so the md names the lens as a prefix: `own-atlas` (a1-v6), `own-sim` (S1-S28), `gap-anchor`, `gap-coasts`, `gap-print`, `gap-consumers`, `gap-mobile`, `gap-vecbudget`, `gap-refs`, `G0`, `G7`, `G8`, `G9`. The JSON keeps each id and lens exactly as supplied.

---

### 5.1 Queue state at HEAD (read from the export, 2026-10-07)

- **Done:** Filigree 1 (the density bible; `docs/filigree/gates/1-research.json` exists) and Mobile 0-4.
- **In flight:** the mobile atlas lane. `docs/mobile/state.json` says `"item":"Mobile 5"`. A-U2 is `failed` after 3 attempts, and A-U3 and A-U4 are `skipped`. The recorded `maps-site/index.html` sha is `4f9757bf…`. Mobile 5, 6, 7, 8 and 9, and the sim lane 10-14, are unchecked (own-atlas q1, gap-mobile:C2).
- **Not run:** Filigree 2, 3 and 4, Street 1 to 4, and Living 0 (the living workflows are still staged under `docs/living/design/workflows/`). `docs/street/gates/` and `docs/living/gates/` do not exist, so **no street or living fixture is frozen yet** (own-sim S14, gap-consumers:C7).
- **Open siblings:** "Traced road network (USER FLAG)", "Region-chart zoom-through", "Sim ↔ atlas continuity", "Atlas reduced motion", "Living · the sheet-wide sea mask (tool)" and "Living later · trace the rivers".
- **POLISH order:** Mobile 5-15 → Atlas reduced motion → Filigree 2 → Street 1 → Street 2 → Region-chart → Sim ↔ atlas continuity → Filigree 3 → Filigree 4 → Street 3 → Street 4 → Living 0 → sea mask → Living 1-4 → Living 3z/4z.

| concern | owner today | binding constraints |
|---|---|---|
| **Registration of city art** (canon overlays, their POIs, wash, cap, fly target) **[reg]** | **Nobody.** No track or POLISH item names `cityBox` or `FOOT_R` (own-atlas a1). No Filigree 2 section owns placement: s03 "The city rule" covers only R4, R7 and R13, and s01 covers the fade ramps, which decide *when* the overlay shows, not *where* it sits (own-atlas v5). G7-11 **[D]** confirms that no chartered item exists to build the vector city or reconcile it with the sim. Its holding check splits the work: registration first, as its own medium-independent atlas item, then the vector city as a second item. | Per a1's correction, the replacement is a **per-overlay registration record for the DM canon art** (Epēshu, Summarch and their `charts/` pyramids), chosen when present. `cityBox`/`FOOT_R` stay the default for procedural plans. The record must feed **every** reader of `cityBox` in one place: `ensureCityOverlay` (bounds), `buildWorldPOIs` (hover halos), `worldPOICoord` (deep-link targets, `flyIntoCity`) and `washMake` (wash bounds). `cityMaxZ` repeats the same maths. It may be part of what the owner sees as "things shift". |

Filigree recorded it as a gap (own-atlas a4 **[D]**; the line range 869-896 is stale, the handler is under `// ---- Mac trackpad gestures (spec WW)`). | `setZoomAround(ll,target,{animate:false})` holds the cursor point exactly, so it is **not** a cause of city drift. It owns names, not coordinates. |

Tying the city fix to an evaluate entry would hold it back (b11's holding check). |
